# Crewboard v0.1 — implementation tickets

Generated from `SDD.md` (requirement IDs), `decisions/` 0001–0062 (source of truth), `design-briefs/` and the pen.dev document
`crewboard.pen`. **167 tickets in 12 epics**, one epic per milestone (M0–M11, decision 0053), in build order; M13 and M12
were added later (206 tickets in 14 epics).

- **Format:** each ticket follows the shape for `crewboard create -f` (decision 0032). IDs `T-0001…` are provisional. Real keys
  (`CREW-…`) are assigned at import. `Depends on` uses these provisional IDs. No ticket depends on a ticket placed after it.
- **IDs added after review (2026-10-09):** T-0161–T-0166 were appended for decisions 0060 and 0061. They sit at the end of their
  milestone (M2, M4, M7, M9), so IDs jump there.
- **Definition of done** (SDD §23.3, decision 0056) applies to every ticket: checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests), new
  behaviour has tests, the "Done when" items are met, and the commit names the requirement IDs and decisions. **A conflict with
  a decision → stop and ask the user.**
- **Tests:** core logic without a UI; git behaviour against temporary repositories; **no fake `claude`**. Hooks are tested by
  feeding `crewboard hook <event>` real-shaped JSON, and PTY plumbing uses an ordinary command in place of `claude`. UI tickets are
  checked by hand against the named `crewboard.pen` frame (agents need the pen.dev connection, decision 0057).
- **Platforms:** macOS only until M11.
- **Built outside Crewboard:** all of v0.1 is built by the implementing agent with `prompts/implementer.md`, one milestone per
  session, without a review stop between milestones; the user reviews everything after M11. Crewboard is used from the work after v0.1 (decision 0062, superseding 0053's dogfooding).
- **Open questions:** `tickets/questions.md`. All questions (Q1–Q15) are resolved; see its Resolutions
  section and the resolution lines under Q13–Q15. Tickets that touch a question say so in their Notes.
- **Coverage:** `tickets/coverage.md` maps every SDD requirement ID to its tickets.
- **M13 · Themes (added 2026-10-11, decision 0069):** T-0168–T-0174 are appended at the end of the file. M13 is built
  before M12 (decision 0053: it touches nothing M12 changes); M12's tickets are written later and take the numbers after
  T-0174. The cut follows the M13 assignment with one change: the gallery's palette switch sits in T-0169 (the palettes)
  rather than in the picker ticket, so every theme can be compared with *01 · Theme palettes* before any picker exists.
- **M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents (added 2026-10-11, decisions 0063–0068):** T-0175–T-0206 are
  appended after M13 and built after it. Their Design lines cite `crewboard.pen` after design updates K, L and M. Core
  tickets come first (Done when removal, priority, Ready for Dev, validation, Auto-merge, Project agents), each UI ticket
  after the core it draws; T-0206 re-runs the checks in the Windows and Linux VMs. The "Done when" sections of these tickets
  are the implementer's checklist; Crewboard's own tickets lose the field in T-0175 (decision 0067).

| Epic | Tickets |
|---|---|
| M0 · Foundations | T-0001 – T-0005 (5) |
| M1 · Background service | T-0006 – T-0018 (13) |
| M2 · Board | T-0019 – T-0045, T-0161, T-0162 (29) |
| M3 · Sessions | T-0046 – T-0063 (18) |
| M4 · Agent signals | T-0064 – T-0076, T-0163, T-0164 (15) |
| M5 · Scheduler | T-0077 – T-0083 (7) |
| M6 · Review & Local merge | T-0084 – T-0109 (26) |
| M7 · Attention & recovery | T-0110 – T-0124, T-0165, T-0166 (17) |
| M8 · Usage | T-0125 – T-0132 (8) |
| M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal | T-0133 – T-0138, T-0167 (7) |
| M10 · PR flow & agent questions | T-0139 – T-0154 (16) |
| M11 · Windows & Linux | T-0155 – T-0160 (6) |
| M13 · Themes | T-0168 – T-0174 (7) |
| M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents | T-0175 – T-0206 (32) |

## M0 · Foundations
Goal: an empty but correct workspace — three crates, the name constant, the database schema and a green local check script.

### T-0001 · Initialize the repository and Cargo workspace
- **Epic:** M0 · Foundations
- **Requirements:** ARC-01
- **Decisions:** 0057, 0054, 0002
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** —
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
This folder becomes the Crewboard code repository (d0057). Run `git init`, add the MIT `LICENSE` (d0054), a Rust
`.gitignore`, and a Cargo workspace with `crates/crewboard-core`, `crates/crewboard-app` and `crates/crewboard-cli`, plus an
empty `plugin/` folder with `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (d0002). `decisions/`, `design-briefs/`, `DESIGN.md` and
`SDD.md` stay where they are. Every later ticket builds on this layout.

**Done when**
- [x] `git init` done; `LICENSE` contains the MIT text with the author's name and 2026
- [x] `cargo build --workspace` builds three crates: `crewboard-core` (lib), `crewboard-app` (bin), `crewboard-cli` (bin named `crewboard`)
- [x] `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` exists with a name and version (skills and hooks come in M4)
- [x] the existing `decisions/`, `design-briefs/`, `DESIGN.md`, `SDD.md` are untouched and committed
- [x] tests: `cargo test --workspace` runs (one placeholder test per crate)
- [x] `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` pass (run by hand; `scripts/check.sh` arrives in T-0005, next); commit names ARC-01 and decisions 0057, 0054, 0002 (SDD §23.3)

**Notes:** a toolchain file (`rust-toolchain.toml`) pinning a stable Rust is an implementation choice; name the version in the PR.

### T-0005 · Local check script: fmt, clippy, tests
- **Epic:** M0 · Foundations
- **Requirements:** ARC-01, ARC-02, PLT-01
- **Decisions:** 0056, 0021
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0001
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Every ticket's definition of done is "checks green" (SDD §23.3). The repository has no remote yet, so there are **no GitHub
Actions** for now (d0056): add `scripts/check.sh`, which runs `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets
-- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace`, stops at the first failure and exits non-zero. It is run **before every commit**.
Git must be available, because many core tests run against temporary repositories. GitHub Actions are added later, once the
repository has a remote.

**Done when**
- [x] `scripts/check.sh` exists, is executable, runs the three steps in that order and prints which step failed
- [x] it works from any folder inside the repository (it changes to the repository root first)
- [ ] a deliberately unformatted file or a clippy warning makes it fail (checked once, by hand)
- [x] `README` or a comment at the top of the script says: run it before every commit; it is the definition of done (d0056)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ARC-01, ARC-02, PLT-01 and decision 0056

**Notes:** Windows and Linux are checked in local virtual machines in T-0160 (M11). No `.github/workflows` file is added now.

### T-0002 · One name constant for product, command, plugin namespace and env prefix
- **Epic:** M0 · Foundations
- **Requirements:** ARC-01
- **Decisions:** 0028
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0001, T-0005
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The product name spreads into the command, the plugin namespace (`/crewboard:work-ticket`) and environment variables
(`CREWBOARD_TICKET`). Decision 0028 requires all of them to come from **one constant**, so a forced rename later is cheap. Add a
small `naming` module in `crewboard-core` that every crate (and the code that writes plugin/skill names) uses.

**Done when**
- [x] one module defines the product name (`Crewboard`), command name (`crewboard`), plugin namespace (`crewboard`) and env prefix (`CREWBOARD_`)
- [x] helpers build derived names: `ticket_env_var()` → `CREWBOARD_TICKET`, `skill("work-ticket")` → `/crewboard:work-ticket`, branch prefix `crewboard/`
- [x] no other source file hard-codes these strings (a test greps the crates' `src/` for the literals outside the module)
- [x] tests: unit tests for every helper
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ARC-01 and decision 0028

### T-0003 · Headless core baseline: data folder and daily logs
- **Epic:** M0 · Foundations
- **Requirements:** ARC-02
- **Decisions:** 0002, 0055, 0056
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0002, T-0005
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`crewboard-core` must have no UI dependency and be testable headless (d0002, d0056). Set up its base: `tokio`, `tracing`, and
one place that resolves the data folder `~/.crewboard/` and its parts — the database file, `wt/<repo-name>/<KEY>` worktrees,
`logs/` and the socket path (d0055). Logs go to `~/.crewboard/logs/`, rotated daily. Tests must be able to point the whole data
folder at a temporary directory.

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard-core`'s dependency tree contains no `gpui` (checked by a test or a step in `scripts/check.sh`, e.g. `cargo tree -p crewboard-core`)
- [x] a `DataDir` type returns the database path, `wt/<repo>/<KEY>` paths, `logs/` and the socket path under `~/.crewboard/`, overridable for tests (e.g. `CREWBOARD_HOME`)
- [x] `tracing` writes to `~/.crewboard/logs/`, rotated daily; folders are created on first use with user-only permissions
- [x] tests: unit tests for path building (including `SHOP-40.1` keys) against a temp dir
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ARC-02 and decisions 0002, 0055, 0056

**Notes:** database file name and log rotation size are implementation choices (SDD §26).

### T-0004 · SQLite schema and migrations (SDD §8)
- **Epic:** M0 · Foundations
- **Requirements:** ARC-02
- **Decisions:** 0003, 0055, 0045
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0003
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Create the SQLite store in `crewboard-core` with `sqlx`, migrations from day one, and the tables of SDD §8: `projects`,
`settings`, `tickets`, `ticket_done_when`, `ticket_deps`, `ticket_key_aliases`, `sessions`, `events`, `review_comments`,
`reviews`, `messages`, `pending_deliveries`, `usage`, `plan_limits`. Write SQL that stays portable to Postgres (d0003). The facts
to store are fixed by §8; names and types may be refined. `sessions` must be able to keep **every** Claude session ID per ticket
(d0045).

**Done when**
- [x] migrations create all §8 tables with foreign keys and indexes for the obvious lookups (ticket by project+number, deps both directions, sessions by ticket)
- [x] opening the store runs pending migrations; opening it twice is a no-op
- [x] a thin repository layer exists for at least projects and tickets (insert/get), used by the tests
- [x] no SQLite-only features that would block Postgres (no `AUTOINCREMENT` reliance on rowid semantics, explicit types); deviations listed in the PR
- [x] tests: migration runs on an empty temp database; a round trip for one row in every table
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ARC-02 and decisions 0003, 0055, 0045

**Notes:** derived values (inbox items, readiness, sort order, cleanup candidates) are **not** stored (§8). Later tickets add
columns through new migrations, never by editing this one.

## M1 · Background service
Goal: `crewboard daemon` runs, owns the store behind a user-locked socket, and the app connects to it, starts it, survives
its restarts and shows the service banners and the first-run checklist.

### T-0006 · Protocol: versioned JSON messages, events and build ID
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-01, SVC-03
- **Decisions:** 0055, 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0003
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
App, CLI and service all speak one protocol: JSON messages over the local socket, as requests/replies plus a live event stream
(d0055). **Every message carries a protocol version**, and the service reports its **build ID**, which drives the "Service update
ready" / "Restart required" check (d0022). Put the message types in `crewboard-core` (a `protocol` module) so app and CLI share
them. CLI requests carry a **caller identity** field (filled in M4, SDD §11.3).

**Done when**
- [x] an envelope type with `protocol_version`, a request id, and request / reply / event / error variants; errors are `{"error": {...}}`
- [x] a build ID constant generated at compile time (e.g. git hash + build time) is available to all three crates
- [x] framing over a byte stream is implemented and documented in the code (length-prefixed or newline-delimited JSON: implementation choice, SDD §26)
- [x] an incompatible `protocol_version` is detected and reported as a distinct error kind (used by "Restart required")
- [x] tests: serialize/deserialize round trips, framing with partial reads, version mismatch detection
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-01, SVC-03 and decisions 0055, 0022

**Notes:** exact message names are an implementation choice (SDD §5.4). Start with `ping` and `service.status`; later tickets add
their requests.

### T-0007 · CLI skeleton and global conventions
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-01
- **Decisions:** 0032, 0031
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0002
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The `crewboard` binary in `crewboard-cli` launches the app when run without a subcommand and hosts `daemon`, `attach`, `hook` and
the agent commands. Set up argument parsing with the beads-style global conventions now (d0032): `--json`, `-q/--quiet`,
`--silent` (print only the ID), `-P/--project <KEY>`, `--actor`; JSON errors as `{"error": {...}}` with a non-zero exit;
human-readable output by default. The CLI stays a thin client (d0031).

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard --help` lists `daemon` (and placeholders are not shown for commands not built yet)
- [x] global flags parse on every subcommand; an output helper prints human text, `--json`, `--quiet` or `--silent` consistently
- [x] errors print `{"error": {"kind": …, "message": …}}` with `--json` and exit non-zero
- [x] running `crewboard` with no subcommand launches the app binary (path resolution next to the CLI binary; documented)
- [x] tests: CLI parsing tests and error-format tests (`assert_cmd` or similar)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-01 and decisions 0032, 0031

### T-0008 · `crewboard daemon start / stop / status`
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-01, SVC-07, PLT-01
- **Decisions:** 0022, 0027, 0055
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0004, T-0006, T-0007
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard daemon` is the background service that will own every PTY, process, the scheduler, the store and git (d0022). In this
ticket it starts, opens the store, listens on the Unix domain socket in `~/.crewboard/` (macOS, PLT-01), answers `ping` and
`service.status`, and stops cleanly. Only one instance may run. Keep it **small and boring** (SVC-07, d0027): it is the component
whose crash interrupts every agent. **No auto-start at login** (d0055).

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard daemon start` detaches and runs the service; a second `start` reports "already running" and exits 0
- [x] `crewboard daemon status` prints running/stopped, PID, build ID, protocol version and uptime (`--json` supported)
- [x] `crewboard daemon stop` asks the service to shut down; it closes the socket and store and removes its lock/PID file
- [x] a stale socket or lock file from a crash is detected and replaced on start
- [x] nothing installs a launch agent or login item
- [x] tests: start/status/stop against a temp `CREWBOARD_HOME`; double start; stale socket recovery
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-01, SVC-07, PLT-01 and decisions 0022, 0027, 0055

### T-0009 · Socket locked to the current OS user (macOS)
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-02
- **Decisions:** 0027
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0008
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The socket can move tickets and type into agent sessions, so **only the current OS user** may connect (d0027). On macOS: the
socket's folder is `0700`, the socket `0600`, and the service checks the peer's UID on every connection.

**Done when**
- [x] the socket directory and file are created with user-only permissions, also when they already exist with looser ones
- [x] connections whose peer UID differs from the service's UID are closed with a logged warning
- [x] tests: permission bits checked in a test; the peer-UID check is unit-tested through an injectable credential source
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-02 and decision 0027

**Notes:** the Windows named pipe with an owner-only ACL is T-0155 (M11).

### T-0010 · Event stream subscription
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-01
- **Decisions:** 0055
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0008
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The app follows the service through a live **event stream** (d0055): ticket changed, card status changed, session output,
session state, inbox changed, banner changed, usage changed, run output, toast (SDD §5.4). Build the subscription mechanism in
the service now, with a broadcast channel that later tickets publish to. Slow subscribers must never block the service.

**Done when**
- [x] a client can subscribe on its connection and receives events in order; several clients can subscribe at once
- [x] a subscriber that falls too far behind is disconnected (it reconnects and refetches), never blocking publishers
- [x] a test event kind exists so the mechanism is testable before real events
- [x] tests: two subscribers receive the same events; lagging subscriber is dropped while others continue
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-01 and decision 0055

### T-0011 · Client library: connect, start the service, reconnect
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-04, ARC-03
- **Decisions:** 0022, 0055
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0009, T-0010
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
App and CLI share one client in `crewboard-core`: connect to the socket, send requests, subscribe to events. The app **starts
`crewboard daemon` when it isn't running** (d0055). When the connection drops, the client reconnects automatically with backoff
and reports its state (connected / reconnecting) so the app can show "Reconnecting… agents keep running" (SVC-04). The app is
only a client: closing it never stops the service (ARC-03).

**Done when**
- [x] `connect_or_start()` starts the daemon if the socket is absent, then connects; it never starts a second daemon
- [x] on disconnect the client retries with backoff and emits `Reconnecting` / `Connected` state changes; subscriptions are re-established after reconnect
- [x] requests issued while disconnected fail fast with a typed "disconnected" error (the UI disables input meanwhile)
- [x] tests: start-if-missing, reconnect after the daemon is stopped and started again, resubscription after reconnect
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-04, ARC-03 and decisions 0022, 0055

### T-0012 · Build-ID / protocol check and user-clicked service restart (core)
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SVC-03
- **Decisions:** 0022, 0045
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0011
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
After the user rebuilds Crewboard, the running service is older than the app. On connect the client compares build IDs: if the
service is older → **"Service update ready"**; if the protocol is incompatible → **"Restart required"** where only Restart works
(d0022). The banner needs the counts "3 agents working, 2 waiting", so `service.status` reports them (zero until sessions exist).
The restart itself is a request: stop the old service, start the new binary, reconnect. **The service never restarts itself.**
A restart is treated like a reboot (d0045); that handling (Interrupted, Resume all) arrives with reconcile in T-0111.

**Done when**
- [x] the client exposes `Compatibility::{Same, ServiceOlder, Incompatible}` after connecting
- [x] `service.status` includes working-agent and waiting-agent counts (computed from stored state; 0 for now)
- [x] a `service.restart` flow (client side) stops the old service, starts the current binary and reconnects; it runs only when called
- [x] with an incompatible protocol, every request except restart/status is refused client-side with a clear error
- [x] nothing in the service schedules its own restart (reviewed in the PR)
- [x] tests: older build ID → `ServiceOlder`; version mismatch → `Incompatible`; restart replaces the process (temp `CREWBOARD_HOME`)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-03 and decisions 0022, 0045

**Notes:** until T-0111 lands, a restart simply ends sessions; tickets are reconciled when reconcile exists.

### T-0013 · Tools checklist and permission-mode check (core)
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** SET-02, PLT-03
- **Decisions:** 0021, 0017, 0011, 0016
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0008
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
First run shows which tools exist (d0021, d0017): **git** and **claude** required; **gh** and **az** with the `azure-devops`
extension optional, with login status (`gh auth status`, `az account show`). Each missing tool has a copyable install command for
the current OS. Crewboard **never installs anything** and does **no claude version checks** (PLT-03). It also detects whether the
user's Claude Code permission mode prompts, to show a **warning only** (d0011, d0016). Build this as a service request
(`tools.check`) in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] `tools.check` returns, per tool: found/missing, version string (display only), required/optional, login status for gh/az, the `azure-devops` extension status, and the install command for macOS (e.g. `brew install gh`)
- [x] the permission-mode check reads the user's Claude Code settings files **read-only** and reports "prompting" vs "not prompting"; unreadable or unknown → "unknown" (no warning)
- [x] no code path installs, updates or version-gates a tool
- [x] tests: detection with a fake `PATH` containing stub scripts named `git`/`gh` (ordinary scripts, not a fake claude session); settings parsing from fixture files
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SET-02, PLT-03 and decisions 0021, 0017, 0011, 0016

**Notes:** which settings keys indicate a prompting mode (e.g. `permissions.defaultMode`) must be verified against current
Claude Code docs; name the source in the PR. Windows/Linux install commands come in T-0159.

### T-0014 · GPUI app skeleton connected to the service
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** ARC-03
- **Decisions:** 0001, 0022, 0055
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0011
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Create the `crewboard-app` GPUI application with `gpui` **pinned to one Zed commit** (d0001; use Zed's source as the reference).
On launch it connects through the client library (starting the service if needed) and shows an empty window with a connection
state. Closing the window **never stops the service** (ARC-03, d0022). Keep UI logic in view models that can be unit-tested
(SDD §23.1).

**Done when**
- [x] `gpui` is pinned to a specific Zed commit in `Cargo.toml`, with the commit and date noted in a comment
- [ ] launching the app starts the daemon if needed and shows "connected" / "reconnecting" from the client state
- [ ] closing the last window quits the app process; `crewboard daemon status` still reports the service running
- [x] an app-level view-model pattern is established (one example view model with a unit test)
- [ ] tests: view-model unit test; by hand: launch, close, confirm the service keeps running
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ARC-03 and decisions 0001, 0022, 0055

### T-0015 · Design foundations in GPUI: tokens, themes, fonts, icons
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** UI-07, UI-08
- **Decisions:** 0001
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Foundations → Color Tokens, Status Legend, Type · Spacing · Icons
- **Depends on:** T-0014
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Every screen reuses one visual language (SDD §21.2): calm, dense, custom-drawn GPUI with flat fills, borders, radius and simple
shadows, no blur. Implement the color tokens (light and dark), the status legend colors + icon pairs (colour is never the only
signal), the type scale with the bundled UI and monospace fonts, spacing/radius scale, and the Lucide icon set named in the
frame. Theme follows the OS by default with light/dark override (setting stored later in T-0045). The M1 banners and onboarding
are the first users.

**Done when**
- [x] a `Theme` with every token from *01 · Foundations → Color Tokens* (light and dark), switchable at runtime
- [x] status tiers from *Status Legend* exposed as named styles (needs-you / working / review / done) each with an icon
- [x] the fonts shown in *Type · Spacing · Icons* are bundled in the app (licences checked and listed) and the type scale is available as named text styles
- [x] the icons listed in the frame are bundled (Lucide, ISC) at 14 px / 1.5 stroke
- [ ] tests: theme token lookup unit tests; checked by hand against *01 · Foundations* in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-07, UI-08 and decision 0001

### T-0016 · Components: Button, Icon Button, Kbd, Banner, Toast
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** UI-07
- **Decisions:** 0058
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Primitives (Kbd, Button, Icon Button), Buttons → Button matrix, Banners → Banner, Overlays & feedback
- **Depends on:** T-0015
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Build the reusable components that M1's screens need, matching the pen.dev components `Button`, `Icon Button`, `Kbd`, `Banner`
and `Toast`. Buttons have primary, secondary, destructive and ghost variants with default, hover, pressed, focused, disabled and
loading states. `Kbd` shows shortcut hints in the platform's form (⌘ on macOS, Ctrl on Windows/Linux, d0058). Banner and Toast
are the containers used by the banner area (T-0017). The remaining components come early in M2 (T-0030, T-0031).

**Done when**
- [x] each component exists in `crewboard-app` with every state shown in *Button matrix*, *Primitives* and *Banners*
- [x] `Kbd` renders from an action id + platform, so the same action shows `⌘J` or `Ctrl+J`
- [x] Toast supports success and error kinds with an optional action link; Banner supports icon, text, actions and dismiss
- [x] a hidden debug gallery view shows all components for manual checking
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for state handling (disabled ignores clicks, loading blocks double press); checked by hand against the frames in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-07 and decision 0058

### T-0017 · Banner area, toasts and service banners
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** UI-07, SVC-03, SVC-04
- **Decisions:** 0016, 0022
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → Banners (Background service reconnecting, Background service stopped, Service update ready, Restart required, Stacking · usage limit + reconnecting) and Row → Service restart · confirm; 02 · App shell & project switcher → Background service reconnecting
- **Depends on:** T-0012, T-0016
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Add the window's banner area with the **one banner per situation** rule and the stacking rules from the frame (P6, d0016), plus a
toast host. Wire the service banners: a calm **"Reconnecting… agents keep running"** while disconnected (terminals keep their
scrollback, input disabled, SVC-04); **"Background service stopped"**; **"Service update ready · Restart now (3 agents working, 2
waiting)"** and **"Restart required"** where only Restart works (SVC-03). Restart asks first: "3 working agents and 2 waiting ones
will be interrupted. You can Resume all afterwards." The old service keeps running until the click.

**Done when**
- [x] a banner model keyed by situation: raising the same situation twice never shows two banners; stacking order follows *Stacking · usage limit + reconnecting*
- [ ] Reconnecting appears on disconnect and disappears on reconnect; global input is disabled meanwhile
- [ ] Service update ready / Restart required appear from the compatibility result of T-0012 with live counts; Restart opens the confirmation and only restarts on confirm
- [x] the toast host shows stacked success/error toasts that time out; error toasts can carry an action link
- [ ] tests: banner-model unit tests (dedupe, stacking, restart-required disables other actions); checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-07, SVC-03, SVC-04 and decisions 0016, 0022

### T-0018 · Onboarding: welcome and tools checklist
- **Epic:** M1 · Background service
- **Requirements:** UI-08, SET-02
- **Decisions:** 0021, 0011, 0016, 0023
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 14 · First run & onboarding → 1 · Welcome, 2 · Tool checklist · everything installed, claude missing · blocking, gh and az missing · non-blocking (Windows), Dark · welcome; 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → Banners → Permission-mode warning · first run
- **Depends on:** T-0013, T-0017
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The first launch shows a short **Welcome**, then the **tools checklist** from `tools.check` (T-0013): found tools with versions,
missing ones with their copyable install command, a **Re-check** button. `claude` missing is blocking (agents can't start
without it); `gh`/`az` missing is non-blocking. If the user's Claude Code mode prompts, the **permission-mode warning** banner
appears: "With 5–10 agents, permission prompts will stop agents often", with links to the docs on allowlists and auto mode.
**Warning only**, dismissable, it changes nothing (d0011, d0016). The "Add your first project" step follows in T-0034.

**Done when**
- [ ] first run (no projects stored) opens Welcome → Tool checklist; the checklist shows every state in the frames
- [ ] copy buttons put the exact install command on the clipboard; Re-check calls `tools.check` again
- [x] with `claude` missing the Continue button explains why it is blocked; with only gh/az missing it continues
- [x] the permission-mode warning appears only when the check reports "prompting", can be dismissed, and its dismissal is remembered
- [x] Crewboard never runs an install or settings change from this screen
- [ ] tests: onboarding view-model tests for each checklist state; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-08, SET-02 and decisions 0021, 0011, 0016, 0023

**Notes:** the "Windows" label in the gh/az frame is a platform example; on macOS show the macOS install commands.
## M2 · Board
Goal: projects, tickets, epics and dependencies live in the service, and the app shows a working board with the editor, drag
rules, pins, chain highlight, search and settings — no agents yet.

### T-0019 · Domain types: keys, kinds, columns, states, flags
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-01, TKT-02
- **Decisions:** 0029, 0039, 0004
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0004
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Every later ticket talks about the same nouns, so define them once in `crewboard-core` as plain types with no I/O. Ticket keys are
`<KEY>-<number>` (SHOP-18) and epic children `<EPIC-KEY>.<n>` (SHOP-40.1), one level only (d0029, d0039). Types are `ticket` and
`epic`, with **no priorities** (TKT-02). Columns, sub-states and flags follow SDD §7.1–§7.2 exactly: five columns, the Dev
sub-states (Queued reasons, working, background, orange family, Interrupted…), Done · Merged / Closed · not merged, and the
independent flags list.

**Done when**
- [x] a `TicketKey` type parses and formats `SHOP-18`, `SHOP-40.1`, and rejects `SHOP-40.1.1`, `shop-18`, `SHOP-` and `SHOP-0`
- [x] a bare number (`18`) resolves to a key given a current project; a bare `40.1` resolves to the epic child
- [x] `Kind { Ticket, Epic }`; no priority field anywhere
- [x] `Column` has exactly Backlog, Dev, Ready for Review, Approved, Done; a `Status` enum covers every row of SDD §7.2 (incl. Parked · has work, Start interrupted, Parent closed, Working in background, Asks you / Needs you / Idle, Waiting for limit reset, Interrupted, Resume failed, PR open, Merged, Closed · not merged)
- [x] a `Flags` set covers every flag in §7.2 (pinned, sent back · N · round K, conflicts with `<base>`, changed since review, N messages waiting, Run running, updated by agent, answering, last signal, tokens, PR #N, PR provider not detected)
- [x] no stacking states exist (no Paused, no needs_restack) (d0049)
- [x] tests: unit tests for key parsing/formatting and round-tripping every enum through its stored string form
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-01, TKT-02 and decisions 0029, 0039, 0004

### T-0020 · Git runner and temp-repo test harness
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** PRJ-03
- **Decisions:** 0055, 0056
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0003
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Crewboard runs **the user's own `git` command**, never a git library, so the user's config, hooks and credentials apply (d0055).
Add a small async git runner in `crewboard-core` (cwd, args, env, captured stdout/stderr, typed errors, tracing of every call)
and a test harness that builds **temporary repositories** with commits, branches and a bare "origin" remote (d0056). Its first
real use is reading a repo's default branch for the project base branch (PRJ-03); M3 and M6 build on it.

**Done when**
- [x] `Git::run(cwd, args)` returns stdout or a typed error with exit code and stderr; every call is logged at debug level
- [x] helper queries: is-repo, repo root, current branch, `origin/HEAD` target (if any), list local and remote branches
- [x] the git binary is found on `PATH`; a missing git gives a clear error (the tools checklist already flags it, T-0013)
- [x] test harness: create a temp repo with N commits, a bare origin with `origin/HEAD` → `develop`, and a repo with no remote
- [x] tests: temp git repo tests for each helper query
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PRJ-03 and decisions 0055, 0056

**Notes:** the runner never runs `git stash` and has no helper for it (P3, d0038).

### T-0021 · Projects: add, validate, key, base branch, remove
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** PRJ-01, PRJ-02, PRJ-03, PRJ-04
- **Decisions:** 0023, 0024, 0029, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0019, T-0020, T-0008
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
A project is **one git repository**, added with nothing but its folder (zero config, d0023). Validation: "not a git repo" and
"already added". The **project key** is suggested from the repo name (`shop-web` → `SHOP`, `billing-api` → `BILL`), editable
when adding, and **never changeable afterwards** (d0029). The **base branch** is prefilled from where `origin/HEAD` points; a
repo without a remote uses its current branch; it stays editable later (d0059). Removing a project deletes its tickets in
Crewboard and **never touches the repo** (PRJ-04). All in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] `projects.add(path, key?)` validates: path is a git repo (repo root is stored), not already added (same root), key is uppercase letters/digits, starts with a letter, is unique
- [x] key suggestion: `shop-web` → `SHOP`, `billing-api` → `BILL`, `crewboard` → `CREW`; collisions get a distinct suggestion
- [x] base branch prefill: `origin/HEAD` → `develop` in a repo whose remote default is `develop`; no remote → current branch
- [x] update allows name and base branch changes; any attempt to change the key is refused
- [x] remove deletes the project's tickets, deps, comments, sessions rows and settings from the database; the repo folder is untouched (asserted in a test)
- [x] nothing is written into the repo on add (a test compares `git status --porcelain` and the file list before/after)
- [x] tests: temp git repo tests for every validation, prefill and the remove guarantee
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PRJ-01, PRJ-02, PRJ-03, PRJ-04 and decisions 0023, 0024, 0029, 0059

**Notes:** removing a project with running agents is handled once sessions exist; until then remove is purely database work.
Worktree removal for a removed project goes through the Cleanup view (T-0116), never silently.

### T-0022 · Tickets: create, edit, key allocation, fields, actor, events
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-01, TKT-03
- **Decisions:** 0019, 0029, 0051, 0059, 0032
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0021
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Tickets and epics are created in **Backlog** with the fields of TKT-03: title, What and why (Markdown), Done when (ordered
checklist), base branch ("Start from and merge into") and External ID. Numbers increase per project and are **never reused**,
even after deletion (d0029); epic child numbers are never reused within the epic. The base branch defaults to the project's and
applies to standalone tickets and epics only; epic children inherit the epic's branch; it is **locked once started** (d0051,
d0059). External ID exists only on epics and standalone tickets. Every change records its **actor** (`agent:SHOP-18` or the OS
user) in `created_by`/`updated_by` and appends an `events` row — the source for History and "last signal" later. Lives in
`crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] create ticket/epic: allocates the next project number atomically; child create allocates the next `.n` within the epic
- [x] deleted numbers are never handed out again (test deletes SHOP-24's row directly and creates a new ticket → SHOP-25+)
- [x] update title, body, Done-when items (add, edit, reorder, remove), base branch, External ID; empty title is refused
- [x] base branch is refused on epic children ("From the epic's branch (SHOP-40)") and once the ticket has started (`branch_locked`)
- [x] External ID is refused on epic children
- [x] each Done-when item records `added_by`; each change writes `updated_by` and an `events` row with actor and payload
- [x] tests: unit tests on a temp database for numbering, field rules and the event log
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-01, TKT-03 and decisions 0019, 0029, 0051, 0059, 0032

**Notes:** the actor comes from the caller; the CLI fills it from `--actor` in T-0069, agent identity in T-0064.

### T-0023 · Agent-edit history (before/after)
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-05
- **Decisions:** 0019, 0036
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
When an agent edits a ticket (`crewboard update`, d0036), the user must see it: an **"Updated by agent"** marker and a simple
before/after view (d0019). Record, for every field changed by an `agent:` actor, the old and new value, so the editor (T-0042)
can show the marker and the comparison. User edits are recorded too but don't raise the marker.

**Done when**
- [x] each agent edit stores field name, before, after, actor and time (in `events.payload_json` or a dedicated table; implementer's choice)
- [x] a query returns "updated by agent" (bool + last agent + time) and the list of before/after changes since the user last viewed them
- [x] a "seen" mark clears the marker without deleting history
- [x] Done-when items added by an agent are identifiable (`added_by`)
- [x] tests: unit tests: user edit → no marker; agent edit of body and two Done-when items → marker with three changes; seen → cleared
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-05 and decisions 0019, 0036

### T-0024 · Dependencies: DAG, cycle path, same project, same base, epic deps, tree
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** DEP-01, DEP-02, DEP-04, PRJ-05
- **Decisions:** 0005, 0049, 0051, 0046, 0024
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Tickets form a DAG with **any number of dependencies** (d0049). The service rejects: a link that would create a **cycle**, with an
error that names the loop ("SHOP-21 → SHOP-20 → SHOP-21"); a link to another project (d0024); and a link across **different base
branches** ("SHOP-31 merges into `release/2.3`, SHOP-20 into `main`. Linked tickets must use the same base branch.", d0051). Epic
children share the epic's branch. A ticket may **depend on an epic** if it has the epic's base (d0046). Also provide the tree that
the Stack list and `dep tree` print, and a `cycles` check. In `crewboard-core`; the same rules serve editor, protocol and CLI.

**Done when**
- [x] `deps.add(child, parent)` refuses self-links, duplicates, cross-project links, cycles (error contains the full path) and cross-base links (error text as above, with both bases)
- [x] base comparison uses the resolved base: own base for standalone tickets and epics, the epic's branch for children
- [x] a ticket can depend on an epic only with the epic's base; a ticket that is a child of that epic can't depend on its own epic
- [x] `deps.remove`, `deps.tree(key)` (parents up and children down, with statuses), `deps.cycles(project)` (empty on a valid DB)
- [x] changing a ticket's base branch is refused while it has links to tickets on another base (same message)
- [x] tests: unit tests for SHOP-17 → SHOP-18 → SHOP-20 ← SHOP-19, SHOP-21 → SHOP-20 cycle attempt, SHOP-31 on `release/2.3` vs SHOP-20 on `main`, SHOP-50 depending on epic SHOP-40
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names DEP-01, DEP-02, DEP-04, PRJ-05 and decisions 0005, 0049, 0051, 0046, 0024

**Notes:** readiness ("ready when every parent is Done · Merged") is computed in T-0077 (M5); this ticket only stores and
validates links.

### T-0025 · Epics: children, add to epic, move to epic + alias, close-epic plan, progress
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** EPC-01, EPC-06, EPC-07, TKT-07
- **Decisions:** 0039, 0044, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022, T-0024
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Epics are containers and delivery units (d0059). This ticket covers their bookkeeping in `crewboard-core`: **Add ticket to epic**
creates a child (SHOP-40.1, 40.2…); **Move to epic** is allowed only for a ticket that **never went to Dev**, gives it the epic's
next child key (SHOP-51 → SHOP-40.4), adopts the epic's branch, and keeps the **old key as an alias** that resolves everywhere
(d0044); **progress** "2 / 3 merged"; and the **close-epic plan**: the list of unfinished children to close and agents to stop,
used by the single confirmation (EPC-06). Merged children stay merged. A child can't move to another epic.

**Done when**
- [x] add child: key `SHOP-40.<next>`, base = epic's branch (read-only), no External ID
- [x] move to epic: refused if the ticket has ever started, is an epic, or is already a child; on success the key changes, a `ticket_key_aliases` row keeps `SHOP-51`, and key lookup by `SHOP-51` returns the moved ticket
- [x] move to epic refuses when the ticket's dependencies would cross bases after adopting the epic's branch (DEP-02 message)
- [x] `epic.progress` returns merged / total children; `epic.close_plan` lists unfinished children and which have a running agent
- [x] `epic.close` (bookkeeping part) closes the listed children and the epic; merged children unchanged; an epic with nothing merged ends "Closed · not merged" (d0044)
- [x] tests: unit tests with SHOP-40 Wishlist and children 40.1 (Merged), 40.2 (working, simulated state), 40.3 (Backlog); move SHOP-51 → SHOP-40.4 and resolve the alias
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names EPC-01, EPC-06, EPC-07, TKT-07 and decisions 0039, 0044, 0059

**Notes:** stopping agents and removing worktrees on close is wired in T-0100 (M6). The epic's integration branch is created in
T-0048 (M3). Follow decision 0033 for worktrees on close (questions Q4, resolved: 0033 wins; the dialog text is fixed by design update H).

### T-0026 · Board state machine: transitions, drag rules, empty Done-when warning
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-04, UI-02
- **Decisions:** 0004, 0034, 0036, 0037, 0041, 0044, 0052
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Encode SDD §7.3 transitions and §7.4 drag rules as one pure function in `crewboard-core`: given a ticket, its state and a
requested move, return allowed / allowed-with-confirmation / allowed-with-warning / refused (with a reason). Allowed drags:
Backlog → Dev; Dev → Backlog (Park, confirm); Ready for Review → Approved (asks Local merge or Open PR); Approved → Ready for
Review (PR rework); Done (Closed only) → Backlog. Refused: into Agent Working or Ready for Review by drag, Merged out of Done,
epics into Dev. An empty "Done when" on Backlog → Dev returns a **warning, never a block** ("Start anyway" / "Add criteria",
d0019). This ticket applies only Backlog → Dev (Queued) and reopen-as-bookkeeping; the other moves' side effects come later.

**Done when**
- [x] `check_move(ticket, target)` returns the outcome for every pair of columns and every relevant sub-state, including epics and Merged vs Closed in Done
- [x] Backlog → Dev sets Dev · Queued; with no Done-when items it returns `Warning(EmptyDoneWhen)` and proceeds only when the caller confirms
- [x] moves whose effects aren't built yet (Park, Approve, PR rework, Close, Reopen with agent work) are reported as allowed by the rules but executed by their own tickets (T-0104, T-0089, T-0144, T-0100, T-0101)
- [x] refused moves carry a human reason ("Only the agent or Submit for review moves a ticket to Ready for Review", "Merged tickets are locked", "Epics don't go to Dev")
- [x] tests: table-driven unit tests covering every row of §7.3 and §7.4
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-04, UI-02 and decisions 0004, 0034, 0036, 0037, 0041, 0044, 0052

**Notes:** until the M3 start trigger (T-0053) and the M5 scheduler (T-0079), a Queued ticket just stays Queued.

### T-0027 · Pinning and column sort order
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-06, UI-02
- **Decisions:** 0047, 0037, 0042, 0016
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0026
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Any ticket or epic can be pinned/unpinned (d0047). A pinned ticket is **always first in its column**; several pins → most recently
pinned first; re-pinning bumps it; the pin follows the ticket across columns; pinning affects board order only. Implement
`pin`/`unpin` and the full ordering function of SDD §7.5 in `crewboard-core`, so it is tested once and every later state slots
into it: Dev = pinned → orange (inbox order) → Interrupted / Start interrupted / Resume failed / Parent closed → working (incl.
background, limit wait) → Queued section (pins first there too); inside a group, tickets that **block more tickets** first.

**Done when**
- [x] `pin` stores `pinned_at = now` (re-pin updates it); `unpin` clears it; neither changes column, inbox or agent state
- [x] `sort_column(column, tickets)` implements §7.5 for every column, with a stable tie-breaker (oldest first) documented in code
- [x] the "blocks more" count uses direct and indirect dependents within the project
- [x] the Dev fold rule is exposed: needs-you cards are never folded; the fold covers only working and queued cards ("4 more · 2 working · 2 queued")
- [x] tests: unit tests with the `shop-web` sample (pinned SHOP-19 above orange SHOP-22/SHOP-23; SHOP-28 Start interrupted floats above working; SHOP-18 blocking 2 sorts first in its group)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-06, UI-02 and decisions 0047, 0037, 0042, 0016

### T-0028 · Settings store: global and per-project values, validation
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** SET-01
- **Decisions:** 0023, 0003
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0021
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
All settings live in the app's database and **nothing is written into repos** (d0023). Add typed access in `crewboard-core` for
the global settings (agent limits, agent instructions, workflow skills, dollar estimate, notifications, appearance, terminal font
size) and per-project settings (setup command, run command, Open URL, env files to copy, agent instructions override, workflow
skill overrides, "Let finished tickets answer questions" — on by default), with defaults from SDD §20. Invalid values are
**flagged**, not silently fixed (e.g. an env file that doesn't exist in the repo).

**Done when**
- [x] typed getters/setters with defaults: limits off (max 5, back-pressure 3 when enabled), dollar estimate off, notifications on, appearance system, let-finished-answer on, workflow skills `/crewboard:commit`, `/crewboard:push`, `/crewboard:open-pr`
- [x] per-project values fall back to the global value when empty (agent instructions, workflow skills)
- [x] validation reports per-field problems: missing env file (checked relative to the repo root, read-only), Open URL that isn't a URL; values are still stored and flagged
- [x] no code path writes into the repo (test as in T-0021)
- [x] tests: unit tests for defaults, fallback and validation against a temp git repo
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SET-01 and decisions 0023, 0003

**Notes:** features that use these values arrive in their own milestones (limits T-0080, notifications T-0117, Run T-0133,
workflow skills T-0139, let-finished-answer T-0153).

### T-0029 · Protocol handlers and events: projects, tickets, deps, epics, pins, settings
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** SET-01, PRJ-01, TKT-03
- **Decisions:** 0055
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0010, T-0021, T-0024, T-0025, T-0026, T-0027, T-0028
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Expose the M2 core over the socket so the app (and later the CLI) can use it: requests for projects (add, update settings,
remove, list), tickets (create, update, move, pin, close-plan, move to epic), dependencies (add, remove, tree, cycles) and
settings, plus the events **ticket changed** and **card status changed** (SDD §5.4). The service enforces every rule; the clients
stay thin (d0055). Board snapshots include the sorted order from T-0027.

**Done when**
- [x] each request maps to the core function and returns its typed result or its error (cycle path, cross-base text, key change refused…)
- [x] a `board.snapshot(project)` returns columns with sorted cards and their status/flags
- [x] every mutation publishes a `ticket changed` event (and `card status changed` when status changes) to subscribers
- [x] a `project.list` result carries per-project counts (agents working, items needing the user — zero until later milestones)
- [x] tests: protocol-level tests against a running service in a temp `CREWBOARD_HOME` (create SHOP-18, add a dep, receive the events)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SET-01, PRJ-01, TKT-03 and decision 0055

**Notes:** caller identity and agent permission scope are added in M4 (T-0064, T-0065); until then every caller has full access.

### T-0030 · Components: Status Pill, Meta Chip, tabs, inputs, menus, dialogs
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** UI-03, UI-05
- **Decisions:** 0001
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Primitives (Status Pill, Meta Chip), Badges & Inputs (Badges & pills, Tabs & segmented, Fields), Overlays & feedback
- **Depends on:** T-0016
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Build the rest of the shared components before the screens that use them, matching the pen.dev components `Status Pill` and
`Meta Chip` and the sheets *Badges & Inputs* and *Overlays & feedback*: count and dependency badges (`⛓ SHOP-18`), "last signal
40 s" pills, tabs and the segmented control (Unified / Side-by-side), text input, multi-line input, checklist item, dropdown,
toggle, tooltip, context menu and confirm dialog. Each with default, hover, pressed, focused and disabled states. In
`crewboard-app`.

- [x] Status Pill takes a status from the domain types (T-0019) and renders its colour + icon + label (never colour alone)
- [x] Meta Chip renders key/value metadata (deps, port, tokens, PR #) per the frame
- [x] tabs with indicators, segmented control, text/multi-line inputs with validation message, checklist item (check, drag handle, remove), dropdown, toggle
- [x] tooltip, context menu (keyboard navigable) and confirm dialog (primary/destructive, Esc cancels, ↵ confirms)
- [x] all appear in the debug gallery from T-0016
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for input validation and menu keyboard navigation; checked by hand against the named sections in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-03, UI-05 and decision 0001

### T-0031 · Ticket Card component and flag priority
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** UI-03
- **Decisions:** 0047, 0042, 0027, 0020
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Card component; 04 · Ticket card variants · Light → Variant grid, Compact density; 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Variant grid
- **Depends on:** T-0019, T-0030
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
The card is the most repeated element; it must answer in under a second: what is it, is it OK, does it need me, what is it waiting
on. Build the `Ticket Card` component in `crewboard-app` with all its slots: key (+ pin icon), title (2 lines then truncate, full
title in tooltip), status pill, orange border + callout for needs-you states, meta row (dependency badges, last signal, tokens,
port, PR chip), hover quick actions, and selected / focused / hovered / dragging / chain-highlighted / dimmed states. Implement
the **flag priority** from the frame as a tested view-model function: one pill, one callout; the highest flag takes the pill, lower
flags fall back to the meta row; **pinned is not a status** and never takes the pill.

**Done when**
- [x] a `CardViewModel::from(ticket, flags, now)` maps every status and flag in SDD §7.2 to pill / callout / meta row per *Which flag wins the pill slot*
- [x] the pill is empty when the status equals the column's base status (frame header rule)
- [x] the component renders every variant shown in *Variant grid* from fixture data, in light and dark; compact density per *Compact density*
- [x] long titles truncate after 2 lines with the full title on hover
- [x] variants whose data arrives later (orange family M4, Queued reasons M5, review flags M6, usage M8, Run M9, PR M10) render from fixtures now and light up as their tickets land
- [ ] tests: unit tests for the priority function (e.g. Needs you + Running + 2 deps → Needs you pill, `▶ :4020` and `⛓` in the meta row); checked by hand against *04 · Ticket card variants* Light and Dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-03 and decisions 0047, 0042, 0027, 0020

**Notes:** use the sample tickets (SHOP-17…SHOP-28, BILL-4) as fixtures. The "asked SHOP-40.1 · waiting" flag (MSG-06) is added to the
priority function by T-0154 (questions Q9, resolved).

queued"** fold that never hides needs-you cards; a collapsible **Done rail**; epic cards in Backlog; empty column and empty board
states; very full columns scroll. Follow the recommended width strategy in the frame. Cards update live from events.

### T-0036 · Drag and drop, allowed/forbidden feedback, empty Done-when warning
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** UI-02, TKT-04
- **Decisions:** 0019, 0052, 0041
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 03 · Board & columns → Drag · over an allowed column, Drag · over a forbidden column, Annotations, Epic can’t be dragged to Dev; 08 · Ticket editor → Starting with an empty “Done when”
- **Depends on:** T-0035
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Build drag and drop on the board (GPUI has none built in, d0001): lifted card, drop indicator, and **allowed vs. not-allowed**
feedback driven by `check_move` (T-0026). Dropping Backlog → Dev moves the ticket to Queued; with an empty "Done when" it first
shows the warning dialog **"Start anyway" / "Add criteria"** (TKT-04), which never blocks. **⇧⌘D** (Start / move to Dev) does the
same from the keyboard. Forbidden drops (into Agent Working or Ready for Review, Merged out of Done, epics into Dev) show the
reason and snap back.

**Done when**
- [x] dragging shows the lifted card; hovering a column shows allowed or forbidden styling with the reason from `check_move`
- [x] Backlog → Dev (drop or ⇧⌘D) moves to Queued; empty Done-when → warning dialog; "Add criteria" opens the editor at the Done-when list
- [x] drops whose effects belong to later tickets (Park T-0107, Approve choice T-0095, PR rework T-0150, Reopen T-0106) are accepted by the rules but show "not available yet" until those tickets land
- [x] epic SHOP-40 dragged toward Dev shows the forbidden state from *Epic can’t be dragged to Dev*
- [x] Esc cancels a drag
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for drop-target evaluation; checked by hand against the named frames (SHOP-24 has no Done-when → warning)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-02, TKT-04 and decisions 0019, 0052, 0041

### T-0041 · Editor: Depends-on picker, base branch, External ID
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** UI-05, DEP-01, DEP-02, DEP-04, TKT-03
- **Decisions:** 0051, 0059, 0046
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 08 · Ticket editor → Depends on · picker open, Cycle prevention, Row · base branch per ticket (0051) (Hotfix on another base (SHOP-31), Base locked after start (SHOP-19), Depends on · different base rejected), Row · epics and base branches (0051) (Epic base branch (SHOP-40), Epic child · base from the epic (SHOP-40.2), Depends on an epic · different base rejected)
- **Depends on:** T-0040
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Add the remaining form fields. **Depends on**: a picker that searches tickets **in the same project only**, shows each one's
status, lists epics too, and shows the service's errors inline: the **cycle** error with the loop (SHOP-21 → SHOP-20 → SHOP-21)
and the **cross-base** error ("SHOP-31 merges into `release/2.3`, SHOP-20 into `main`…"). **Start from and merge into**: a
dropdown of local and remote branches for standalone tickets and epics, defaulting to the project's base, **locked once started**;
epic children show it read-only ("From the epic's branch (SHOP-40)"). **External ID**: optional, epics and standalone tickets only
(d0059).

**Done when**
- [x] the picker excludes other projects and the ticket itself, shows statuses, and includes epics
- [x] picking a ticket that would create a loop or cross bases shows the exact service error inline and doesn't add the link
- [x] base dropdown lists local and remote branches (from T-0020 helpers); SHOP-31 can choose `release/2.3`; a started ticket shows the field locked
- [x] epic children show the read-only base line and no External ID field; epics show both (base locked once a child started)
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for field visibility per ticket kind and error display; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-05, DEP-01, DEP-02, DEP-04, TKT-03 and decisions 0051, 0059, 0046
