### T-0161 · Delete never-started tickets (core)
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-08
- **Decisions:** 0060, 0029, 0018, 0044
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0024, T-0025, T-0029
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Add **Delete** to `crewboard-core` for tickets that **never went to Dev**: no branch, worktree or conversation exists (d0060). It is
a deletion, so it needs the user's confirmation (d0018): a `tickets.delete_preview(key)` request returns what the confirmation must
name (the dependent tickets whose links will be removed), and `tickets.delete(key)` performs it. The ticket's number is **never
reused** (d0029) and its key (and any alias pointing to it, d0044) stops resolving. Anything that has been to Dev can only be
**Closed** (d0033). **Deleting an epic** deletes its never-started children with it, in the same confirmation (d0060).

**Done when**
- [x] delete is refused for any ticket that has been to Dev (Queued or later at any time, Parked, Closed after a start, or with a branch, worktree or session row): "SHOP-19 has been to Dev. Close it instead."
- [x] `delete_preview` lists dependents, e.g. "SHOP-21 is a dependency of SHOP-22; that link will be removed"; `delete` removes the ticket, its Done-when items, its dependency links in both directions, its aliases and its review/message rows, and emits a "ticket deleted" event so boards update live
- [x] the project's `next_ticket_number` (and an epic's next child number) is unchanged, so SHOP-21 is never handed out again; `crewboard show SHOP-21` then reports "unknown ticket"
- [x] an epic whose children all never started is deleted **together with its children**: `delete_preview(SHOP-40)` lists them for the confirmation ("Delete SHOP-40 and its 2 tickets (SHOP-40.1, SHOP-40.2)? None of them started.") plus any dependents of the epic or its children; `delete` removes all of them in one transaction
- [x] an epic with any started child is refused ("SHOP-40 has started tickets. Close it instead.")
- [x] the request is available to the app and the user's own CLI connections only; agent callers are refused under the permission scope (CLI-02, T-0065)
- [x] tests: unit tests for the refusal rules on every state; delete with dependents (links removed and named); epic delete removes its never-started children in one transaction; number not reused after delete; alias stops resolving
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-08 and decisions 0060, 0029, 0018, 0044

**Notes:** epic deletion was decided in questions Q13 (d0060 changelog). No `crewboard delete` CLI command is in SDD §11.2,
so none is added.

### T-0162 · Delete from the card menu, with confirmation
- **Epic:** M2 · Board
- **Requirements:** TKT-08
- **Decisions:** 0060, 0018
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → Row · answer options & delete (0060, 0061) → Delete · never-started ticket, Delete ticket · confirm, Dark · options & delete; 05 · Ticket side panel → Epic dialogs → Delete epic · confirm; 03 · Board & columns → Pinned tickets (card right-click menu); 17 · Project terminal → Undo a created ticket
- **Depends on:** T-0039, T-0161
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Add **Delete…** to the card's right-click menu in `crewboard-app` for never-started tickets (d0060), and build the reusable
confirmation dialog that the panel "…" menu (T-0060) and the Project terminal summary (T-0167) also use. The dialog follows the
*Undo a created ticket* pattern: "Delete SHOP-21 Order confirmation email?", says it hasn't started, and names every dependency link
that will be removed. Nothing is deleted without the confirm click.

**Done when**
- [x] the card menu enables Delete… only when `delete_preview` would allow it; for started tickets it is disabled with the hint "Started tickets can only be closed." (per *Delete · never-started ticket*), next to Close ticket…
- [x] the dialog names the ticket, says it never started, and lists the links to remove ("Its link to SHOP-20 is removed too"); Cancel is the default button
- [x] on confirm the card disappears via the "ticket deleted" event; a refusal from the service is shown inline, never swallowed
- [x] for an epic, the dialog lists its children that will be deleted too: "Delete SHOP-40 and its 2 tickets (SHOP-40.1, SHOP-40.2)? None of them started." (Q13, d0060)
- [x] the dialog is a reusable component taking a ticket key (used by T-0060 and T-0167)
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for menu visibility and dialog text with 0, 1 and 3 dependents, and for an epic with 2 children; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-08 and decisions 0060, 0018

**Notes:** the Delete item sits in the same menu as Pin / Unpin and Close ticket…; for started tickets the disabled item reads
"Started tickets can only be closed." (design update I).

## M3 · Sessions
Goal: dragging a ticket to Dev creates its worktree and branch, starts a real `claude` session in the service, and the user can
watch and type into it from the side panel or `crewboard attach`.

### T-0046 · Port pool: two ports per ticket
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-01
- **Decisions:** 0026
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0004
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Parallel agents must never collide on a port, and an agent's leftover dev server must never block the user's Run (d0026). Each
started ticket gets **two ports** from a pool in `crewboard-core`: the **agent port** (`$PORT` in the agent's session, e.g. 4019
for SHOP-19) and the **Run port** (`$PORT` in Run and Shell, e.g. 4020). Allocation is step 6 of the start sequence (SES-01).
Ports are stored on the ticket (`agent_port`, `run_port`) and freed at Done, Close and Park (later tickets call `free`).

**Done when**
- [x] `allocate(ticket)` returns two distinct ports from the pool, skipping ports already held by another ticket **and** ports currently in use on the machine (bind check)
- [x] allocation is idempotent: calling it again for a ticket that already holds ports returns the same two
- [x] `free(ticket)` releases both; freed ports can be reused by other tickets
- [x] allocations survive a service restart (read back from the store)
- [x] pool exhausted → a typed error that the start sequence reports (never an infinite loop)
- [x] tests: unit tests for allocation, idempotency, skipping a port bound by a test listener, exhaustion
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-01 and decision 0026

**Notes:** the port range and allocation strategy are implementation choices (SDD §26); document them in the code and PR.
Leaked ports are listed in Cleanup later (T-0116).

### T-0047 · Keep the base current: fetch, fast-forward, diverged hold
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-01, GIT-01
- **Decisions:** 0059, 0038
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0020
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Before a delivery unit starts (SES-01 step 2) — and later before a Local merge into a base branch (GIT-01, T-0089) — Crewboard
runs `git fetch`, fast-forwards the local base if it is **only behind** its remote, and **holds** if local and remote have
**diverged** (d0059). Build this as one reusable `crewboard-core` git operation using the user's `git` CLI. The fast-forward
touches the user's checkout only when the base is checked out there **and that checkout is clean**; Crewboard never stashes and
never touches a dirty checkout (d0038). Epic branches are local only and skip this step.

**Done when**
- [x] `sync_base(project, branch)` returns one of: `UpToDate`, `FastForwarded`, `Diverged { message }`, `NoRemote`, or a fetch error
- [x] behind only → the local branch is fast-forwarded; if it is checked out in the user's folder, that checkout is updated only when clean (otherwise the ref is left alone and the result says why)
- [x] diverged → nothing is merged or rebased; the message reads "`develop` and `origin/develop` have diverged. Push or pull, then retry" (BILL-6 sample)
- [x] a project without a remote skips fetching and returns `NoRemote`
- [x] **never** pushes, stashes, or modifies a dirty checkout (asserted in tests by checking the working tree is byte-identical)
- [x] tests: temp git repos with a bare "origin": up to date, behind, diverged, behind with a dirty checked-out base, no remote
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-01, GIT-01 and decisions 0059, 0038

### T-0048 · Worktrees, temporary branches, base_sha, epic integration branch
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-01, SES-02, EPC-02, GIT-07
- **Decisions:** 0010, 0023, 0029, 0050, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0020, T-0025
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Each ticket works in its own git worktree **outside the user's repo**, at `~/.crewboard/wt/<repo-name>/<KEY>` (d0010, d0023), on
a **temporary branch** `crewboard/<KEY>` (GIT-07, d0050; the real name is chosen at the first push in M10). Resolve the base
first (SES-01 step 1): standalone tickets and epics use their base branch; **epic children use the epic's branch**, which is
created on the first child's start as `crewboard/SHOP-40` from the epic's base (EPC-02). Record `base_sha` (where the ticket left
its base). Everything must be **idempotent** (SES-02): re-running reuses an existing worktree or branch.

**Done when**
- [x] `prepare_worktree(ticket)` resolves the base (ticket base, or the epic's branch for SHOP-40.2), creates `crewboard/<KEY>` from it and adds the worktree at `~/.crewboard/wt/shop-web/SHOP-18`
- [x] the first child start of an epic creates `crewboard/SHOP-40` from the epic's base; later children reuse it; the epic gets no agent and no worktree of its own
- [x] `base_sha`, `branch_name` and `worktree_path` are stored on the ticket; the base branch becomes locked once started (`branch_locked`)
- [x] re-running after any partial state (branch exists, worktree exists, worktree folder missing but registered) converges to the same result without errors
- [x] nothing is written inside the user's repo folder other than git's own worktree metadata under `.git/`
- [x] tests: temp git repos — fresh start, re-run, partial states, epic child creating then reusing the epic branch, `SHOP-40.1` key in paths
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-01, SES-02, EPC-02, GIT-07 and decisions 0010, 0023, 0029, 0050, 0059

**Notes:** merges into the epic branch run in a Crewboard-owned scratch worktree (T-0089); this ticket only creates the branch.

### T-0049 · Env files and setup command
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-01
- **Decisions:** 0010, 0023, 0007
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0048
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Start steps 4 and 5 (SES-01): copy the project's **env files** (e.g. `.env.local`) from the user's repo into the new worktree,
then run the project's **setup command** (e.g. `pnpm install`) in the worktree, if one is set (d0010, d0007). Settings come from
the app's store, never from a file in the repo (d0023). A failure here must surface as **Start interrupted** in T-0053, with the
setup output available.

**Done when**
- [x] each configured env file is copied into the worktree at the same relative path; a missing source file fails the step with a clear message ("`.env.local` not found in ~/code/shop-web")
- [x] the setup command runs with cwd = worktree, the user's environment, and its output captured to a per-ticket log readable later
- [x] a non-zero exit fails the step with the exit code and the last lines of output
- [x] re-running overwrites copied env files and re-runs setup (idempotent from the caller's view)
- [x] zero config (no env files, no setup command) passes straight through
- [x] tests: temp repo + worktree; copying, missing file, setup success, setup failure using ordinary shell commands (`true`, `false`, `echo`)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-01 and decisions 0010, 0023, 0007

### T-0050 · PTY session manager with stored scrollback
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-04, SVC-05, SVC-01
- **Decisions:** 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0008
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The background service owns every PTY (d0022, SVC-01). Build a session manager in `crewboard-core` on `portable-pty`: spawn a
command with a cwd and env, stream its output as `session output` events, accept resize, detect exit and its reason, and **store
scrollback** per session so the app can redraw a terminal on reconnect (SVC-05). Each spawn writes a `sessions` row (kind
agent/shell/project/run…, state, started/ended, end reason). One agent session per ticket (SES-04). The same manager serves Shell
and the Project terminal later (M9).

**Done when**
- [x] `spawn(kind, ticket?, cmd, cwd, env, size)` starts a PTY process and records a `sessions` row; a second agent session for the same ticket is refused
- [x] output is published as events and appended to a bounded per-session scrollback buffer; a reconnecting client can fetch the buffer then continue live
- [x] `resize(session, cols, rows)` reaches the child (verified with `stty size` in a test)
- [x] process exit updates the session row (ended_at, end_reason) and emits a `session state` event
- [x] protocol requests for attach (fetch scrollback + subscribe), resize and session list exist
- [x] tests: PTY tests with ordinary commands in place of `claude` (`sh -c 'echo hi; sleep 1'`, `cat`), scrollback bound, exit detection, duplicate-agent refusal
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-04, SVC-05, SVC-01 and decision 0022

**Notes:** scrollback size is an implementation choice (SDD §26). Scrollback lives in the service's memory; Crewboard never
copies terminal output to disk (d0045).

### T-0051 · Keystroke relay and unsent-input flag
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SVC-06
- **Decisions:** 0048, 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0050
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The service **relays every keystroke** from the app or `crewboard attach` to the PTY (SVC-06, d0022) and tracks a per-session
**"unsent input" flag**: set by any typed character, cleared by Enter, or when Crewboard itself clears the line (d0048). This flag
is what lets Crewboard never mix its own text with the user's (P7). The relay also reports "Enter pressed in this terminal",
which clears orange signals in M4 (ATT-02).

**Done when**
- [x] an `input` request writes bytes to the session's PTY; several attached clients can type into the same session
- [x] the unsent flag is set by printable input, stays set across arrow keys/backspace that leave text, and is cleared by Enter (`\r`) and by Crewboard's own `clear_line` operation
- [x] backspacing the line to empty clears the flag (tracked by a simple per-line character count; documented limits in the code)
- [x] an "Enter pressed" event is emitted per session for later consumers (ATT-02)
- [x] tests: unit tests for the flag state machine with byte sequences; PTY test with `cat` echoing relayed input
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SVC-06 and decisions 0048, 0022

**Notes:** the exact line-clear sequence sent to `claude` (e.g. Ctrl+U) must be verified by hand against the real CLI; name it in the PR.

### T-0052 · Agent launch command and Claude session ID
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-03, SES-04
- **Decisions:** 0009, 0011, 0031
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0050
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Launch the real interactive `claude` CLI for a ticket through the session manager (SES-03), with cwd = the worktree and env
`CREWBOARD_TICKET=SHOP-18`, `PORT=<agent port>`, `PATH=<crewboard bin dir>:$PATH`, running
`claude --plugin-dir <crewboard plugin> --allowedTools "Bash(crewboard:*)" "/crewboard:work-ticket"`. **No permission mode or other
permission flags**: the user's own Claude Code settings apply (d0011); the only pre-approval is `Bash(crewboard:*)`. Store the
**Claude session ID** on the session row, because Resume, Park and "Before the restart" depend on it (d0045). Names come from the
name constant (T-0002). This ticket launches **without** `--settings`; the status-line wrapper is added by T-0127 (M8).

**Done when**
- [x] a `launch_agent(ticket)` builder produces exactly the command and env above (plugin dir resolved next to the installed binary or from the repo in dev builds)
- [x] the Claude session ID is known and stored before or right after launch, and a `resume_agent(ticket, session_id)` builder produces `claude --resume <id>` with the same env and flags
- [x] no `--permission-mode`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions` or similar flag can appear (unit test on the builder)
- [ ] verified by hand once with the real `claude`: the plugin loads and `/crewboard:work-ticket` is invoked (result noted in the PR)
- [x] tests: builder unit tests; PTY plumbing test with an ordinary command substituted for `claude` that prints its env and args
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-03, SES-04 and decisions 0009, 0011, 0031

**Notes:** the exact plugin-loading flag must be verified when built (d0009, SDD §26). Capturing the session ID is an
implementation choice: passing `--session-id <uuid>` at launch is preferred if the installed CLI supports it; otherwise read
`session_id` from the first hook payload (M4). Document which.

### T-0053 · Start sequence, Start interrupted, Retry start (+ temporary start trigger until T-0079)
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-01, SES-02
- **Decisions:** 0018, 0007
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0046, T-0047, T-0048, T-0049, T-0052
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Orchestrate the start sequence in `crewboard-core`, in order (SES-01): resolve base → sync base (hold on diverged) → branch +
worktree + `base_sha` → env files → setup command → two ports → launch the agent, store the session ID, set **Agent Working**.
The start is **idempotent** (SES-02). A start that stops halfway (e.g. SHOP-28's setup failed) shows **"Start interrupted"** with
one action, **Retry start**, which re-runs the **whole** sequence; it is **never re-run silently** (d0018). **Temporary trigger:**
until the scheduler exists (T-0079, M5), a ticket entering Queued starts immediately, without readiness checks; T-0079 replaces this.

**Done when**
- [x] a successful start leaves the ticket in Dev · Working with worktree, branch, `base_sha`, both ports and a live agent session recorded, plus History/`events` entries
- [x] diverged base → the ticket stays in Dev · Queued · held with the diverged message (BILL-6); no worktree is created
- [x] a failure in any step → Dev · Queued · **Start interrupted** with the failing step and its message stored; nothing is rolled back or deleted
- [x] **Retry start** (a request, user click only) re-runs the full sequence and reuses what exists
- [x] no code path re-runs a failed start by itself (reviewed; test asserts the state stays Start interrupted across ticks)
- [x] the temporary "start on enter Queued" trigger is isolated in one function and marked for removal by T-0079
- [x] tests: temp git repo + ordinary command in place of `claude`; success, setup failure → Start interrupted → Retry start succeeds, diverged hold
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-01, SES-02 and decisions 0018, 0007

### T-0054 · Automatic deliveries and user-click deliveries
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-06, SES-07
- **Decisions:** 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0051
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Crewboard types into live sessions in two ways (d0048). **Automatic deliveries** (another agent's question or answer, the
ticket-change note, "continue" after the limit reset) are stored in `pending_deliveries` and sent **only when the agent is idle
between turns and the input line is empty** (SES-06); until then the ticket shows **"1 message waiting · Deliver now"**. **The
user's own clicks** (Send back, an inbox answer) are never held: if the line has unsent text, the request returns a prompt —
*"SHOP-23 has unsent text in its terminal"* → **Keep it and cancel** / **Clear it and send** (SES-07). Crewboard **never clears the
user's text silently**. The "idle between turns" predicate comes from hook signals (T-0066/T-0067); here it is an injected input.

**Done when**
- [x] `queue_delivery(session, kind, text)` stores a pending delivery and emits a card-status event with the waiting count
- [x] pending deliveries are sent in order the moment the session is idle **and** the unsent flag is clear; each is typed then submitted, and removed from the queue
- [x] **Deliver now** (user click) sends immediately if the line is empty; if it has unsent text it returns the Keep/Clear prompt instead
- [x] `deliver_from_click(session, text)` returns `NeedsConfirmation { ticket }` when unsent text exists; `clear_and_send` clears the line (resetting the flag) then sends; `keep` sends nothing
- [x] tests: unit tests with an injected idle predicate and unsent flag covering: held while busy, held while unsent text, delivered when both clear, Deliver now, Keep/Clear outcomes; PTY test with `cat`
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-06, SES-07 and decision 0048

**Notes:** the restack note from d0048 is deferred with stacking (d0049); don't build it.

### T-0055 · `crewboard attach <KEY>`
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-05
- **Decisions:** 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0007, T-0051
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`crewboard attach SHOP-18` opens a ticket's live session from any terminal (SES-05, d0022). In `crewboard-cli`: put the local
terminal in raw mode, fetch the stored scrollback, then stream output and relay keystrokes (through the same relay, so the unsent
flag stays correct) and window resizes. Detaching leaves the session running.

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard attach SHOP-18` shows the scrollback, then live output; typing reaches the agent; resizing the terminal resizes the PTY
- [x] a documented detach key sequence returns to the shell and restores the terminal mode; the session keeps running
- [x] a ticket without a live session → a clear error ("SHOP-18 has no live session") and non-zero exit
- [x] a bare number (`attach 18`) resolves in the current project; omitted KEY uses `$CREWBOARD_TICKET`
- [x] tests: integration test attaching to a session running `cat` through a PTY harness; error cases
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-05 and decision 0022

**Notes:** the detach key is an implementation choice; it must not collide with keys Claude Code uses (Esc, Ctrl+C).

### T-0056 · Terminal view: grid rendering, input, reconnect redraw
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-09, SVC-04, SVC-05
- **Decisions:** 0001, 0022
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 06 · Terminal tab → Live · streaming, app has focus; Reconnecting to the background service; Session ended normally; Narrow panel · 480 px; Dark · live terminal
- **Depends on:** T-0014, T-0050
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
Render a session in `crewboard-app` using `alacritty_terminal` for emulation, drawn with GPUI, as Zed does (d0001): monospace
grid, ANSI 16/256/truecolor, cursor, bold/italic/underline, wide characters. Feed it the service's output events and send typed
keys through the relay. On reconnect, redraw from the **stored scrollback** (SVC-05); while disconnected, keep the scrollback
visible and **disable input** (SVC-04). It must work as a real terminal grid — no chat-bubble re-rendering.

**Done when**
- [ ] a `TerminalView` attaches to a session, replays scrollback, then renders live output at 60 fps without dropping input
- [ ] colours (16, 256, truecolor), cursor shapes and text attributes render correctly (checked with a test script printing a colour chart)
- [ ] resizing the panel resizes the PTY (cols/rows); narrow panel (480 px) and full window both work
- [x] disconnect → input disabled with the frame's reconnecting state, scrollback kept; reconnect → redrawn from the service without duplicate output
- [x] session ended → the frame's ended state; input disabled
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for attach/replay/disconnect states; checked by hand against the named frames in light and dark, running real `claude` once
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-09, SVC-04, SVC-05 and decisions 0001, 0022

**Notes:** consider splitting the grid renderer and the session wiring if the PR grows large.

### T-0057 · Terminal: selection, scrollback, find, jump to latest, font size
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-09
- **Decisions:** 0058
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 06 · Terminal tab → Scrolled up · find in scrollback
- **Depends on:** T-0056
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Complete the terminal view (SES-09): mouse selection with copy, scrolling through scrollback, **find in scrollback** with
**⌘F** (Ctrl+Shift+F on Windows/Linux, d0058), a **"jump to latest"** control when scrolled up, and a font-size control (also
⌘+ / ⌘− inside a focused terminal, per the settings frame). Scrolling up must not be yanked back down by new output.

**Done when**
- [x] drag-select and double/triple-click select; ⌘C copies the selection
- [x] wheel/trackpad scrolling through scrollback; new output while scrolled up shows "jump to latest" instead of moving the view
- [x] ⌘F opens find: highlights matches, next/previous, match count, Esc closes find (Esc reaches Claude only when find is closed)
- [x] font size changes per terminal and the default comes from the terminal font size setting (stored in T-0045)
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for find (matches across wrapped lines) and scroll anchoring; checked by hand against the frame
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-09 and decision 0058

### T-0058 · Terminal focus and key routing
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** SES-10
- **Decisions:** 0058
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 06 · Terminal tab → Answering a question (SHOP-23) · focused, Annotations; 01 · Components → Keyboard map
- **Depends on:** T-0056
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
While a terminal has focus, **every key goes to Claude Code, Esc included** (Claude uses Esc to stop and Esc Esc to rewind)
(SES-10). Leave focus with **⇧⌘`**, a click outside, or the inbox shortcut. On macOS, ⌘ app shortcuts keep working because the
terminal doesn't use ⌘ (d0058). Make the focus state obvious (focus ring plus hint, per the frame). Implement the keyboard map in
`crewboard-app` as one action table so platforms can differ (Windows/Linux Ctrl+Shift mapping comes in T-0158).

**Done when**
- [x] focusing the terminal routes all non-⌘ keys (Esc, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+J, arrows, Tab) to the PTY; app handlers never see them
- [x] ⇧⌘`, a click outside the terminal, and the inbox shortcut leave focus; Esc does not
- [x] ⌘ shortcuts from the keyboard map (⌘J, ⇧⌘I, ⌘[ / ⌘], ⌘K, ⌘P, ⌘N, ⇧⌘T, ⌘,, ⇧⌘E, ⌘⌥1–6, ⌘↵, ⌘⌫, ⇧⌘D, ⌘F) still work while focused
- [ ] the focus ring and hint match *Answering a question (SHOP-23) · focused*
- [x] focus changes are reported to the service per session (consumed by the focused-terminal rule, T-0110)
- [ ] tests: unit tests on the key-routing table (focused vs not, macOS); checked by hand against the frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-10 and decision 0058

### T-0059 · Side panel: frame, header, tabs, navigation
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** UI-04
- **Decisions:** 0058, 0035
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → SHOP-18 · Ready for Review, Long title & long branch, Expanded · ⇧⌘E; 05/06 · Panel — SHOP-22 Needs you · Terminal · Light / 05/06 · Panel — SHOP-22 Needs you · Terminal · Dark
- **Depends on:** T-0030, T-0035
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
Clicking a card (or ↵ on a selected card) opens the side panel next to the board (UI-04). Build the frame: open/close (↵ / Esc),
drag to resize, **expand** to full window (⇧⌘E), previous/next ticket (⌘[ / ⌘]). The header shows key, title, status, project,
branch name (copyable), base ("on `main`"), tokens and last signal (filled when those exist). Tabs **Terminal · Diff · Run · Shell ·
Ticket · History** with indicators (live dot, +412 −38, running :4020, session count) and ⌘⌥1–6 to switch (d0035, d0058).
Tabs whose features land later show their empty state until then.

**Done when**
- [x] panel opens from click or ↵, closes with Esc (when the terminal isn't focused) and with its close button; the board stays visible
- [x] resize handle with sane min/max widths; width remembered per session of the app; ⇧⌘E toggles the expanded view
- [x] ⌘[ / ⌘] move to the previous/next ticket in the current column order

- [x] tab bar with indicators; ⌘⌥1–6 switch tabs; the Terminal tab hosts the `TerminalView` (T-0056)

### T-0060 · Panel action bar per state
- **Epic:** M3 · Sessions
- **Requirements:** UI-04, TKT-08
- **Decisions:** 0015, 0018, 0033, 0052, 0060
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → Action bar · every state
- **Depends on:** T-0059, T-0162
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The panel shows the right buttons for each state (brief 05, UI-04): Backlog → Start, Edit; Working → Open terminal, Run; orange →
Answer in terminal; Interrupted → Resume; Start interrupted → Retry start; Ready for Review → Approve ▾, Send back; PR open → Open
PR in browser, Mark merged, Mark closed; Done → View diff; Closed → Reopen; any state → "…" menu (Close ticket, Pin, Submit for
review, Move to epic…, and **Delete…** for tickets that never went to Dev, d0060). Build the **state → actions** mapping as a tested view model in `crewboard-app`, and render it. Actions
whose features arrive in later milestones are wired by those tickets; until then they are hidden, not shown disabled.

**Done when**
- [x] a pure function maps (ticket state, flags, kind ticket/epic/child) to the primary actions and "…" menu items for every row of *Action bar · every state*
- [x] Start (⇧⌘D) and Edit work now; Retry start is wired once T-0063 lands; other actions register as hidden until their tickets enable them
- [x] epics and epic children follow their rules (no Start on an epic; children never offer Open PR)
- [x] the "…" menu enables **Delete…** only for never-started tickets (and epics with no started children), opening the confirmation from T-0162 (TKT-08); for anything that has been to Dev it is disabled with "Started tickets can only be closed." (per *05 · Ticket side panel → Delete · never-started ticket*)
- [ ] tests: table-driven unit tests for every state in the frame; checked by hand against the frame
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-04, TKT-08 and decisions 0015, 0018, 0033, 0052, 0060

cancel** / **Clear it and send** (SES-07).

## M4 · Agent signals
Goal: agents talk to Crewboard through the plugin and the `crewboard` CLI, within their permission scope, and the board shows
what each agent is doing — working, in the background, or waiting for the user.

### T-0064 · Caller identity by process ancestry, `--actor`
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** CLI-01, CLI-04
- **Decisions:** 0031, 0032
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0007, T-0050
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The service must know **who** is calling: an agent session or the user (CLI-01). It decides by **where the process runs** —
walking the caller's process ancestry up to one of its agent PTY sessions — not only by `$CREWBOARD_TICKET`, so unsetting the
variable doesn't grant full access (d0031). The CLI sends its PID with each request; the service resolves it. `--actor` defaults
to `agent:<KEY>` for agent callers, otherwise the OS user (CLI-04, d0032), and drives "created/updated by agent".

**Done when**
- [x] the service maps a caller PID to `Caller::Agent { ticket, session }` when any ancestor is the root process of one of its agent sessions, else `Caller::User`
- [x] the PID is verified against the socket peer credentials (a client can't claim another process's PID)
- [x] `--actor` defaults to `agent:SHOP-18` for agent callers and to the OS username otherwise; an explicit `--actor` from an agent can't claim to be the user
- [x] Shell, Project terminal and Run sessions are **not** agent sessions (their callers are the user)
- [x] tests: process-tree tests spawning ordinary nested shells inside a PTY session; `CREWBOARD_TICKET` unset inside an agent session still resolves to the agent
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names CLI-01, CLI-04 and decisions 0031, 0032

**Notes:** macOS process ancestry via `libproc`/`sysctl`; Windows comes in T-0156.

### T-0065 · Permission scope enforced by the service
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** CLI-02, CLI-03
- **Decisions:** 0031
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0029, T-0064
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Agents run `crewboard` without permission prompts, so the **service** limits them (CLI-02, d0031): **read anything** in the
project; **change only their own ticket** (`update`, `review submit`, `ask`, `answer`, `pr propose`, `pr opened`, `branch
rename`); **create tickets in Backlog** (including `--parent`); add dependencies only **from their own ticket** or **between
tickets they created**. Everything else is refused: "SHOP-18's agent can't change SHOP-12". The Project terminal and the user's
own terminals have full access (CLI-03). The CLI stays a thin client; every rule (cycles, same base, permissions) lives in the
service.

**Done when**
- [x] one policy function `authorize(caller, request)` gates every mutating request in `crewboard-core`
- [x] agent reads across the project succeed; agent writes to another ticket fail with the exact refusal text and a typed error kind
- [x] agent `create` lands in Backlog only (a requested column is ignored or refused); `dep add` allowed only from its own ticket or between tickets whose `created_by` is that agent
- [x] user callers (Project terminal, own terminal) pass every check
- [x] tests: table-driven unit tests of the policy for every command in CLI-02; integration test through the socket with a resolved agent caller
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names CLI-02, CLI-03 and decision 0031

### T-0066 · Hook entry point and hook-to-status mapping
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** PLG-04, ATT-01, ATT-04
- **Decisions:** 0009, 0042, 0020
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0064
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard hook <event>` (in `crewboard-cli`) is what the plugin's hooks call. It reads the hook JSON from stdin and **does
nothing when `CREWBOARD_TICKET` is unset** (PLG-04), so the user's ordinary `claude` sessions are unaffected. The service maps
events per SDD §11.4: `Notification` with `permission_prompt`, `elicitation_dialog`, `elicitation_url_dialog` or
`agent_needs_input` → **Needs you** (other types ignored); `Stop` with non-empty `background_tasks` or `session_crons` → **Working
in background** with what it waits for; `Stop` with both empty and no `review submit` / `ask human` this turn → **Idle**;
`UserPromptSubmit` → back to working. Every hook updates the **last signal**. `StopFailure` is logged only; acting on it
(Waiting for limit reset) is T-0128 in M8.

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard hook Notification|Stop|StopFailure|UserPromptSubmit` exits 0 quickly and silently when `CREWBOARD_TICKET` is unset, and never blocks the agent if the service is unreachable
- [x] the mapping above is a pure function from (event, payload, turn facts) to a signal, with the background summary text ("background: test run, 1 helper agent · 6 min", "wakes up at 4:10pm")
- [x] the service tracks per turn whether `review submit` or `ask human` ran, reset on `UserPromptSubmit`
- [x] raw payloads are logged (debug level) so undocumented fields can be verified against real sessions
- [x] tests: feed `crewboard hook <event>` real-shaped JSON for every row of §11.4 (incl. a permission prompt inside a helper agent and `idle_prompt` ignored); unset `CREWBOARD_TICKET` → no-op
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLG-04, ATT-01, ATT-04 and decisions 0009, 0042, 0020

**Notes:** payload fields beyond the documented ones must be verified by logging real payloads (d0020, d0042, SDD §26); name
the Claude Code version checked in the PR.

### T-0067 · Orange family, clearing rules, last signal
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** ATT-01, ATT-02, ATT-04, ATT-05
- **Decisions:** 0042, 0027
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0051, T-0066
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Turn signals into ticket status in `crewboard-core` (d0042). The **orange family** — **Asks you** (`ask human`), **Needs you**
(permission/elicitation), **Idle** (turn ended, nothing pending) — keeps the label and question text (ATT-01). It **clears** on the
user's input: Enter in that ticket's terminal (from the relay, T-0051), an answer from the inbox (M7), or the agent running any
`crewboard` command (ATT-02). **Working in background** is neutral and counts as working (ATT-04). **Last signal** ("active 40 s
ago") comes from the latest hook event, `crewboard` command or background activity and turns into a **warning when stale** (more
than 5 min) (ATT-05, d0027). This state also provides the "idle between turns" predicate for automatic deliveries (T-0054).

**Done when**
- [x] a per-ticket signal state with transitions for every hook mapping, `ask human`, any `crewboard` command and "Enter pressed"; emits `card status changed`
- [x] orange label and question/prompt text are stored (SHOP-22: "Allow `rm -rf .next`?"; SHOP-23's question)
- [x] Enter in SHOP-23's terminal clears Asks you; a `crewboard` command from the agent clears any orange label
- [x] `last_signal_at` updates on every hook and command; `is_stale` is true after 5 minutes without a signal, excluding Working in background with live background activity
- [x] the idle-between-turns predicate is exposed to the delivery queue (T-0054) and delivers waiting messages when it becomes true
- [x] tests: unit tests for every transition and clearing rule; stale threshold with a fake clock; delivery triggered on Idle
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-01, ATT-02, ATT-04, ATT-05 and decisions 0042, 0027

**Notes:** the stale threshold may change if 5 min proves wrong (SDD §26); keep it one constant. The focused-terminal rule (no
orange while the user looks) is T-0110 (M7).

### T-0068 · CLI read commands: show, list, ready, blocked, project
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** CLI-02, CLI-03
- **Decisions:** 0032, 0039
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0065
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Add the read commands of SDD §11.2 to `crewboard-cli` (d0032): `show [KEY]` (alias `view`; `--long` adds messages and history;
`--children` for epics), `list` (`--status`, `--project`, `--tree`, `--parent <EPIC>`), `ready` / `blocked` (dependency state per
project), `project list | show`. Omitted KEY → `$CREWBOARD_TICKET`; a bare number resolves in the current project; old keys
resolve through aliases (`show SHOP-51` after a move to epic). Human-readable by default, `--json` everywhere. Agents may read
anything in the project (CLI-02).

- [x] `show SHOP-40 --children` lists SHOP-40.1–40.3 with statuses; `list --parent SHOP-40` and `list --tree` work
- [x] `ready` / `blocked` use the readiness function (stubbed to "parents merged" until T-0077 replaces it)
- [x] unknown key → `{"error": {...}}` with non-zero exit; alias resolution covered

- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names CLI-02, CLI-03 and decisions 0032, 0039

### T-0069 · CLI create and update (incl. `-f`, `--parent`, `--type epic`)
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** PLG-05, CLI-02, CLI-04
- **Decisions:** 0032, 0039, 0019
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0065
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard create "Title"` (alias `new`) with `-d`, `--done-when <item>` (repeatable), `--deps A,B`, `--stdin` (Markdown body),
`-f <file>` (batch), `--type epic`, `--parent <EPIC>`, `--silent`; new tickets land in **Backlog** (d0019). `crewboard update
[KEY]` with `--title`, `-d`, `--add-done-when`, `--stdin`. Long text always goes through **stdin** (PLG-05). The actor is recorded
(CLI-04), so agent-created tickets show "created by agent" and agent edits are tracked as before/after (T-0023). The `-f` format is
the Markdown shape of the tickets file itself, so `crewboard create -f` can import it (d0032).

**Done when**
- [x] `create` with every flag creates the ticket in Backlog and prints its key (`--silent` prints only the key)
- [x] `--parent SHOP-40` creates SHOP-40.4; `--type epic` creates an epic; `--deps` uses the service's cycle and same-base checks
- [x] `create -f plan.md` creates every `### KEY-less · title` section with body, Done when and dependencies resolved within the batch; on any error nothing is created
- [x] `update` changes only the given fields; for agent callers it records before/after and refuses other tickets (T-0065)
- [x] tests: CLI integration tests incl. stdin bodies with quotes/backticks, batch import of a small fixture, agent-created origin
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLG-05, CLI-02, CLI-04 and decisions 0032, 0039, 0019

**Notes:** the exact batch format (headings, "Depends on" with provisional IDs) is documented in the code; keep it compatible
with `tickets/v0.1-tickets.md`.

### T-0070 · CLI dependency commands
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** DEP-01, DEP-02, CLI-02
- **Decisions:** 0032
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0065

**What and why**
`crewboard dep add <KEY> <DEPENDS-ON>`, `dep remove`, `dep tree [KEY]` (prints the Stack list), `dep cycles` (d0032). The service
rejects cycles with the loop path ("SHOP-21 → SHOP-20 → SHOP-21") (DEP-01) and links across bases ("SHOP-31 merges into
`release/2.3`, SHOP-20 into `main`. Linked tickets must use the same base branch.") (DEP-02). Agent callers are limited by CLI-02.

- [x] `dep tree SHOP-20` prints the indented Stack list with statuses, matching the panel's order

- [x] an agent can add a dependency from its own ticket but not between two user-created tickets
- [x] tests: CLI integration tests for each command and refusal

`crewboard prime` gives the current ticket's agent its context (d0032): ticket details, **Done when**, parents and their

everything with the Commit skill, submit with `review submit`, ask with `ask human`), and the user's **Agent instructions**:

agent; `--json` also works.

### T-0072 · `ask human` and `review submit` (refuses uncommitted files)
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** ATT-01, REV-07
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0037
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0065, T-0067
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard ask human --stdin` sets **Asks you** with the question text, sorted to the top of Dev (ATT-01, d0037).
`crewboard review submit [KEY] --stdin` stores the summary and moves the ticket to **Ready for Review**, recording the review
head for "changed since review" later (T-0097). It **refuses while the worktree has uncommitted changes**: "2 files are not
committed. Commit them, or discard them if they're scratch files" (REV-07, d0050). Both mark the turn so `Stop` doesn't turn into
Idle (T-0066). Crewboard runs no tests or checks of its own (P5).

**Done when**
- [x] `ask human` with a question on stdin → ticket shows Asks you with the text; empty stdin is refused
- [x] `review submit` with a clean worktree → Ready for Review with the summary stored and `review_head_sha` / `review_entered_at` recorded
- [x] uncommitted or untracked files → refused with the exact message and the file count; the ticket stays in Dev
- [x] both commands set the "submitted/asked this turn" fact used by the Stop mapping
- [x] tests: temp git repo worktree — clean submit, dirty submit refused (modified and untracked cases), ask human state
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-01, REV-07 and decisions 0050, 0037

### T-0073 · Plugin: manifest, hooks, work-ticket and commit skills
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** PLG-01, PLG-02, PLG-03, PLG-04, PLG-05, GIT-08
- **Decisions:** 0009, 0031, 0040, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0066, T-0071, T-0072
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Agent behaviour lives in Markdown in `plugin/`, not in Rust strings (PLG-01, d0009). Ship `plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`,
`hooks/hooks.json` wiring `Notification`, `Stop`, `StopFailure` and `UserPromptSubmit` to `crewboard hook <event>` (no-ops when
`CREWBOARD_TICKET` is unset, PLG-04), and two skills: **`work-ticket`** (run `crewboard prime` → follow the Agent instructions →
plan → implement → commit everything with the configured **Commit** skill → `crewboard review submit --stdin`) and the built-in
**`commit`** skill (commit everything with `SHOP-19: …` messages) (PLG-02, PLG-03, d0050). **The agent commits; Crewboard never
commits** (GIT-08). Long text always via `--stdin` (PLG-05). The user's instructions can't remove Crewboard's own steps (d0040).

**Done when**
- [x] `plugin.json`, `hooks.json`, `skills/work-ticket/SKILL.md`, `skills/commit/SKILL.md` exist; skill and command names come from the name constant where generated
- [x] work-ticket tells the agent: run `crewboard prime` first, follow the printed Agent instructions, use `crewboard ask human --stdin` when stuck, commit via the Commit skill named by `prime`, then `review submit --stdin`; never push
- [ ] the plugin also works by hand: `CREWBOARD_TICKET=SHOP-12 claude --plugin-dir plugin/` in a worktree (checked by hand once, noted in the PR)
- [ ] a plain `claude` session without `CREWBOARD_TICKET` is unaffected by the hooks (checked by hand)
- [ ] tests: a test validates the JSON files and that every hook command matches a `crewboard hook` event the CLI accepts; real `claude` behaviour checked by hand
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLG-01…PLG-05, GIT-08 and decisions 0009, 0031, 0040, 0050

**Notes:** `address-review` and `restack` come in M6 (T-0087, T-0090); `push`, `open-pr`, `answer-question` in M10 (T-0140).
Until workflow-skill settings exist (T-0139), `prime` names `/crewboard:commit`.

### T-0074 · Ticket-change note when the user edits a working ticket
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** SES-08
- **Decisions:** 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022, T-0054
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
When the **user** saves a change to a ticket whose agent is running, Crewboard queues a short note with **only what changed**,
for example *"The ticket was updated by the user. Added to 'Done when': 'works on mobile widths'. Run `crewboard prime` for the
full ticket."* (SES-08, d0048). It goes through the automatic-delivery queue (T-0054), so it waits for an idle agent and an empty
input line. Agent edits send nothing; Queued, Parked and Backlog tickets get nothing.

**Done when**
- [x] a pure function builds the note from a before/after ticket diff (title, What and why, Done when added/removed/edited, dependencies, base not possible after start)
- [x] the note is queued only for user edits on tickets with a live agent session in Dev; agent edits and non-running tickets queue nothing
- [x] several saves before delivery merge into one note (or queue in order; documented)
- [x] tests: unit tests for note text per change kind; integration test showing the note queued and delivered when idle
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SES-08 and decision 0048

### T-0075 · Cards and panel: orange family, background work, last signal
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** ATT-01, ATT-04, ATT-05, UI-03
- **Decisions:** 0042, 0027
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Working · Needs you, Dev · Asks you, Dev · Idle, Dev · working in background, Dev · background · scheduled wakeup, Dev · needs you · helper agent, Working · stale signal; 05/06 · Panel — SHOP-22 Needs you · Terminal · Light
- **Depends on:** T-0031, T-0060, T-0067
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Show the signals from T-0067 on cards and in the panel (UI-03). Orange cards (SHOP-22 Needs you with the shortened prompt, SHOP-23
Asks you with the question, Idle with the last line) get the orange border and callout and sort to the top of Dev (ATT-01). Working
in background is calm blue with a cpu icon, or an alarm-clock icon for wakeups ("wakes up at 4:10pm") (ATT-04). Working cards show
**last signal** ("active 40 s ago"), turning into a warning when stale (BILL-4: "active 4 min ago") (ATT-05). The panel's action
bar shows **Answer in terminal** for orange tickets, which focuses the terminal.

**Done when**
- [ ] each listed card variant renders from live service state in light and dark, using the flag priority of T-0031
- [x] last signal updates live (relative time ticks without refetching) and switches to the warning style when stale
- [x] orange cards reorder to the top of Dev as signals change, below pinned cards
- [x] Answer in terminal opens the panel on the Terminal tab with the terminal focused
- [ ] tests: view-model tests mapping signal states to card content; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-01, ATT-04, ATT-05, UI-03 and decisions 0042, 0027

Make agent activity visible as it happens. Tickets created through the CLI appear in Backlog live with a **"created by agent"**
origin label (TKT-03) and a toast ("SHOP-21 created by agent"); agent edits show the **"Updated by agent"** marker (TKT-05) without
a refresh. In the editor, saving a working ticket shows "SHOP-19's agent will be told what changed." under Save (SES-08), and the
card shows "1 message waiting" until the note is delivered.

### T-0163 · `ask human` answer options: CLI flags and storage
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals
- **Requirements:** ATT-01, ATT-12
- **Decisions:** 0061, 0031, 0032
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0072
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Extend `crewboard ask human` in `crewboard-cli` and the service in `crewboard-core` with answer options (d0061):
`crewboard ask human --option "Postmark" --option "SES" --option "Resend" --recommended 1 --stdin`, question text on stdin (d0031).
**2–4 options**, short labels; `--recommended` is optional; without options the question is free text as before. Store the options
and the recommended index with the question, so the inbox, card and panel (T-0165, T-0166) can show them, and add the core function
that formats the delivered answer: *"Answer to your question: Postmark (option 1)."* Options apply **only** to `ask human`;
`ask <KEY>` stays free text.

**Done when**

- [x] the question, options and recommended index are stored (new migration; table/column names are an implementation choice under SDD §8) and exposed in the needs-you item data (T-0078) and over the protocol
- [x] `--json` returns the question id, options and recommended index; the ticket still shows **Asks you** exactly as without options (ATT-01)
- [x] a core `format_answer(question, choice)` produces "Answer to your question: Postmark (option 1)." for an option and "Answer to your question: <text>" for Other…; used by the answer delivery path
- [x] `crewboard ask SHOP-18 --option …` is refused ("Options are only for questions to the human")
- [x] tests: CLI parsing and refusal tests; storage round trip; `format_answer` unit tests
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-01, ATT-12 and decisions 0061, 0031, 0032

**Notes:** `crewboard questions` (T-0151, M10) also shows the chosen answer. The UI comes in T-0165 and T-0166 (M7).

### T-0164 · Skills: offer 2–4 options with a recommendation
- **Epic:** M4 · Agent signals

- **Decisions:** 0061, 0040, 0009
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0073, T-0163

**What and why**
Teach the agent when to use answer options (d0061, PLG-06): in `plugin/skills/work-ticket/SKILL.md`, a question to the user that
has clear alternatives uses `crewboard ask human --option … --recommended N --stdin` with 2–4 short options and a recommendation;
an open question stays free text. The guidance is Markdown, so it changes without a recompile (d0009) and sits beside the user's
Agent instructions, which still take precedence (d0040). `address-review` gets the same text when it is created (T-0087).

- [x] the text says: 2–4 options, short labels, recommend one when there is a sensible default, never invent options for an open question

## M5 · Scheduler
Goal: Queued tickets start on their own as soon as every dependency is merged (beads-style), within the optional limits, and the
board says what each waiting ticket waits for.

### T-0077 · Readiness (beads-style) and waiting reasons
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** DEP-03, DEP-04
- **Decisions:** 0049, 0044, 0046, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0024
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
A dependency is ready only when the parent is **Done · Merged** into the child's base; for epic children, merged into the epic's
branch. **Closed never counts** (DEP-03, d0049, d0044). A dependency on an epic is ready when the epic is **Done · Merged**; an
epic that ended "Closed · not merged" never counts (DEP-04, d0046). Build this as a pure function in `crewboard-core` that also
returns the **waiting reason** for the card: "waits for SHOP-18, SHOP-19 to merge (SHOP-18 in Review)", "waits for SHOP-18 · PR
#482 open", or "parent SHOP-26 closed".

**Done when**
- [x] `readiness(ticket) -> Ready | Waiting { parents with their states } | ParentClosed { parent }` covers tickets, epic children and epic dependencies
- [x] the waiting text matches the samples above and updates as each parent moves (Review, Approved · PR open, Merged)
- [x] closed parents never count; an epic with all children closed never counts
- [x] `crewboard ready` / `blocked` (T-0068) switch to this function
- [x] tests: unit tests for the SHOP-17 → SHOP-18 → SHOP-20 ← SHOP-19 chain at each storyboard step, epic dependency, closed parent
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names DEP-03, DEP-04 and decisions 0049, 0044, 0046, 0059

### T-0078 · Needs-you item derivation (shared by back-pressure and inbox)
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** ATT-06, SCH-03
- **Decisions:** 0016, 0042, 0024
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0067
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Back-pressure (T-0080) needs to know how many items wait for the user, so the needs-you items are derived here in M5 and the M7
inbox reuses them. Derive them in `crewboard-core`, across **all projects** (d0024), in the inbox order of ATT-06: group 1 orange
(Asks you, Needs you, Idle); group 2 Interrupted, Start interrupted, Resume failed, Parent closed; group 3 Ready for Review
(tickets and epics). Within a group, tickets that **block more tickets** come first, then oldest. Each item carries project, key,
title, reason, age and blocked count. Derived, never stored (SDD §8).

**Done when**
- [x] `needs_you_items()` returns the ordered list across projects with the fields above
- [x] the count used by back-pressure is exposed separately (definition documented: all groups as listed by ATT-06)
- [x] states that arrive later (Interrupted, Resume failed, inbox-routed questions) slot in through the same function as their tickets land
- [x] tests: unit tests with the shared sample data across shop-web and billing-api, checking group order and blocks-more-first ordering
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-06, SCH-03 and decisions 0016, 0042, 0024

**Notes:** the focused-terminal suppression (ATT-03) and plan-limit suppression (ATT-09) filter this list later (T-0110,
T-0129).

### T-0079 · Scheduler loop (replaces the M3 temporary trigger)
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** SCH-01, SCH-02
- **Decisions:** 0007, 0016
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0053, T-0077
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
A loop in the service runs on **every state change plus a slow tick**: select Queued tickets whose dependencies are ready
(T-0077) and start them with the start sequence (T-0053), **oldest first** (SCH-01, d0007). **The scheduler never queues tickets
by itself**: only the user's drag puts a ticket in Queued (SCH-02, d0016). This replaces the temporary "start on entering Queued"
trigger from T-0053. Tickets that are held (diverged base) or Start interrupted are never retried by the loop.

**Done when**
- [x] the temporary trigger from T-0053 is removed; starts happen only through the scheduler
- [x] a state change (e.g. SHOP-19 merged) triggers a pass within one event; a slow tick (interval documented) catches anything missed
- [x] ready tickets start oldest first by the time they entered Queued
- [x] the scheduler never moves a ticket into Queued, never retries Start interrupted, and leaves held (diverged) tickets until a user's Retry
- [x] two passes running at once can't start the same ticket twice
- [x] tests: unit tests with injected start function and clock; storyboard chain (SHOP-20 starts only after SHOP-18 and SHOP-19 are merged)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-01, SCH-02 and decisions 0007, 0016

**Notes:** the plan-limit hold (SCH-04) is added in T-0129 (M8).

### T-0080 · Optional limits: max_concurrency and back-pressure
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** SCH-03
- **Decisions:** 0016, 0042
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0078, T-0079
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Both limits are **optional and OFF by default** (SCH-03, d0016). `max_concurrency` (suggested 5) counts Agent Working tickets
**including orange and background ones**; Review tickets and Parked tickets don't count (d0042). **Back-pressure** (suggested 3):
no new starts while that many needs-you items wait (T-0078). Both control **starting only**: an agent the user unblocks always
continues, and Send back into a full limit waits in Queued (d0034). Settings are global across projects (d0024) and adjustable
live.

**Done when**
- [x] settings `limits.enabled`, `limits.max_concurrency`, `limits.back_pressure` (global) with defaults off / 5 / 3
- [x] with limits on, the scheduler starts at most `max - working` tickets per pass, oldest first; queued tickets show "waiting for a slot"
- [x] with back-pressure reached, no new starts; starts resume when items are cleared
- [x] nothing already running is stopped or paused by a limit change
- [x] tests: unit tests for counting rules (orange and background count, Review/Parked don't) and back-pressure
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-03 and decisions 0016, 0042

### T-0081 · Fetch the base after a parent merges, then start ready tickets
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** SCH-05
- **Decisions:** 0049
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0047, T-0079
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
When a parent becomes Done · Merged, its waiting children must start from the merged code (d0049). Add a service hook "parent
merged" that **fetches the base** (T-0047) and then runs a scheduler pass. It is fired by the user's Local merge click (M6) and by
**Mark merged** (M10, SCH-05) — both user clicks, so there's still no polling. A diverged base holds the children with the
diverged message instead of starting them.

**Done when**
- [x] `on_parent_merged(ticket)` syncs the relevant base (the epic branch is local and skips fetching) then triggers the scheduler
- [x] children of a ticket merged through Mark merged start from the fetched base (verified on `base_sha`)
- [x] a fetch failure leaves the children Queued with an error reason; nothing retries silently
- [x] tests: temp git repos with a bare origin: parent merged on the remote, child starts at the new remote head
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-05 and decision 0049

### T-0082 · Agent limits: settings section and header control
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** SCH-03, UI-01
- **Decisions:** 0016
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Global · Agent limits OFF (default), Global · Agent limits ON; 02 · App shell & project switcher → Header states (Header · agent limits ON + inline popover)
- **Depends on:** T-0033, T-0045, T-0080

Expose the limits in `crewboard-app`: a **Global · Agent limits** section in Settings (off by default; max agents, "pause new
starts when the inbox has N items") and, when enabled, a header control **"3 / 5 agents"** that opens an inline popover to adjust
the values live (SCH-03, UI-01). When off, the header shows the plain running count. Changes apply immediately and never stop
running agents.

- [ ] the settings section matches both frames; toggling on reveals the two fields with defaults 5 and 3
- [x] header shows "3 / 5 agents" only when limits are on; the popover edits the same settings
- [x] changes reach the scheduler without restarting anything
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for the toggle and popover; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-03, UI-01 and decision 0016

### T-0083 · Queued cards: waits-for text, waiting for a slot, held, start toast
- **Epic:** M5 · Scheduler
- **Requirements:** DEP-03, UI-03
- **Decisions:** 0049, 0059
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Queued · waiting on deps, Queued · waits for merge, Queued · ready to start, Queued · held: base diverged; 12 · Stack & dependencies → 2 · The chain runs, 3 · One parent merged, 4 · All parents merged
- **Depends on:** T-0031, T-0077
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Queued cards say what they wait for (DEP-03): "waits for SHOP-18, SHOP-19 to merge (SHOP-18 in Review)", "waiting for a slot" when
the limit is full, and BILL-6's held state "`develop` and `origin/develop` have diverged. Push or pull, then retry" with a Retry
action (a user click that re-runs the start, d0059). When a waiting ticket starts, a toast says so: "SHOP-20 started · all
dependencies merged". The Stack list and panel reuse the same reason text.

**Done when**
- [x] each Queued variant renders from the readiness reason and updates live as parents move
- [x] the held card offers Retry, which calls Retry start; nothing retries without the click
- [x] a toast appears when a ticket leaves Queued because its dependencies merged
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for reason formatting; checked by hand against the named frames and storyboards 2–4
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names DEP-03, UI-03 and decisions 0049, 0059
## M6 · Review & Local merge
Goal: the user reviews a ticket's diff, comments, sends it back or Local-merges it (epic children into their epic), and can
close, reopen, park and submit tickets — the core loop is complete (Crewboard is still built
outside Crewboard until v0.1 is done, decision 0062).

### T-0084 · Diff engine (base_sha, epics by child)
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-01, REV-02
- **Decisions:** 0017, 0049, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0048
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The Diff tab compares a ticket's branch with its **`base_sha`**, where it left its base (d0017, d0049), so a ticket never shows
someone else's changes. An epic's diff is the **epic branch against its base**, grouped by child (one squash commit per merged
child, d0059). Build a diff service in `crewboard-core` on top of the user's `git` CLI: file list with status
(added/modified/deleted/renamed), per-file `+/-` counts, hunks, and the base indicator ("diff vs `main` at `a1b2c3d`"). It must
handle large diffs, binary files, renames and empty diffs, and work for Done tickets whose worktree was removed (the branch is kept).

**Done when**
- [x] `diff.files(ticket)` returns the file list with status, old/new path for renames, counts and a binary flag; `diff.file(ticket, path)` returns hunks with line numbers for both sides
- [x] the diff is always `base_sha...branch` (never against the current base head); the reply carries the base branch name and the short `base_sha`
- [x] epics: the diff is the epic branch vs its base, grouped by child using the child squash commits; a child with nothing merged is not listed
- [x] works from the branch alone when the ticket's worktree no longer exists (Done · Merged, Closed)
- [x] large diffs are paged per file (no full-repo diff in one message); binary files report size only; an empty diff returns an empty list, not an error
- [x] tests: temp git repos — SHOP-18-like branch (added, modified, deleted, renamed, binary file), a base that moved after `base_sha` (diff unchanged), an epic with two merged children, empty diff
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-01, REV-02 and decisions 0017, 0049, 0059

**Notes:** paging size and how hunks are streamed over the protocol are implementation choices (SDD §26).

### T-0085 · Review comments and rounds
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-03
- **Decisions:** 0017, 0034
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0029
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The user writes line comments on the diff; **Send back** bundles them into one message for the agent (d0017). Store comments in
`review_comments` (file, line, side, body, round) and review actions in `reviews` (d0034). Comments belong to the current round
until they are sent; a pending counter shows how many will be sent. **No partial approval**: comments never approve part of a
ticket. Add the protocol requests in `crewboard-core` and publish "ticket changed" events.

**Done when**
- [x] requests: add, edit, delete a comment; reply in a thread on the same line; list comments for a ticket and round; pending count
- [x] a comment records file, line, side (old/new), body, round, created_at; `sent_at` is set only by Send back (T-0086)
- [x] sent comments are read-only; pending ones are editable and deletable
- [x] the round number starts at 1 and is incremented by each Send back; `sent_back_round` on the ticket matches
- [x] tests: unit tests for add/edit/delete/thread, pending counter, round increments, sent comments refusing edits
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-03 and decisions 0017, 0034

### T-0086 · Send back (core)
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-04
- **Decisions:** 0034, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0054, T-0085
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Send back is an action, not a column (d0034). In `crewboard-core`, it bundles the pending line comments and an optional note,
marks them sent, and **moves the ticket straight to Dev**: it types `/crewboard:address-review` into the **same session**, or, if
the session is gone, starts `claude --resume <id>` as part of the same click. If the optional agent limit is on and full, the
ticket waits in **Queued**. The card gets **"Sent back · N comments · round K"**. Send back is a user click, so it is never held,
but it follows the unsent-text rule (d0048): if the input line has unsent text the reply asks *Keep it and cancel / Clear it and
send*. Not available on epics.

**Done when**
- [x] `ticket.send_back(key, note)` moves Ready for Review → Dev · Working (live or resumed session) or → Queued when the limit is on and full
- [x] the session receives `/crewboard:address-review` through the user-click delivery path of T-0054; with unsent text the request returns "needs confirmation" and only proceeds with `clear: true`
- [x] if no live session exists, the same click starts `claude --resume <stored session id>` in the ticket's worktree, then types the skill (tokens spent only because the user clicked)
- [x] comments get `sent_at`; a `reviews` row with action `send_back`, the note and the round is written; History logs it
- [x] the ticket carries the "sent back · N comments · round K" flag until it next reaches Ready for Review
- [x] refused on epics with "Add or reopen a child ticket to change it"; refused outside Ready for Review
- [x] tests: unit tests for state transitions and limit-full → Queued; PTY test with an ordinary command as the session checking the typed text; unsent-text confirmation path
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-04 and decisions 0034, 0048

### T-0087 · `review feedback` and the address-review skill
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-05, PLG-02, PLG-03, PLG-06
- **Decisions:** 0017, 0050, 0061
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0073, T-0085, T-0164
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The agent pulls review feedback itself with `crewboard review feedback [KEY]` (d0017): the in-app comments of the latest sent
round, with file, line and the user's note. Unresolved PR comments are added in M10 (T-0148). Add the command in `crewboard-cli`
and the `plugin/skills/address-review/SKILL.md` skill: run `crewboard prime` first and follow the Agent instructions it prints
(PLG-02), fetch feedback, fix, **commit everything with the configured Commit skill** (PLG-03), then `crewboard review submit
--stdin`. PR feedback never re-queues a ticket by itself (REV-05).

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard review feedback [KEY]` prints the latest sent round's comments grouped by file and the note; `--json` returns structured items; omitted KEY → `$CREWBOARD_TICKET`
- [x] nothing in the feedback path moves the ticket or starts a session
- [x] `address-review/SKILL.md` contains: prime first, follow Agent instructions, fetch feedback, fix, Commit skill, `review submit` with the summary on stdin
- [x] `address-review/SKILL.md` carries the same answer-options guidance as `work-ticket` (T-0164, PLG-06): 2–4 options with a recommendation for questions with clear alternatives, free text otherwise
- [x] tests: CLI output tests (human and JSON) against a temp service; skill file lint test that checks the required steps are present
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-05, PLG-02, PLG-03, PLG-06 and decisions 0017, 0050, 0061

### T-0088 · Checkpoint commit around Crewboard rebases
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** GIT-02
- **Decisions:** 0005, 0038
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0020
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Before any Crewboard rebase in an agent's worktree, the agent's uncommitted changes are kept with a **checkpoint commit**:
commit as `wip: paused by Crewboard`, rebase, then `git reset --soft HEAD~1` so the files come back exactly as they were (d0005,
d0038). **Never stash.** If the rebase or the undo conflicts, the caller (Local merge, reopen) applies its own conflict handling.
Build this as one helper in `crewboard-core`'s git module. This is the only commit Crewboard ever makes itself (GIT-08), and it is
always undone.

**Done when**
- [x] `rebase_with_checkpoint(worktree, onto)` returns `Clean`, `RebaseConflict` or `UndoConflict`; a clean worktree skips the checkpoint
- [x] after a clean run, `git status` shows the same staged/unstaged/untracked state as before (untracked files included in the checkpoint and restored as untracked)
- [x] on conflict the rebase is aborted and the branch and files are back to their original state; no stash entries are ever created
- [x] the user's git config and hooks apply (the user's `git` binary is used); a failing pre-commit hook is reported as an error, never bypassed silently
- [x] tests: temp git repos — clean worktree, dirty worktree with staged/unstaged/untracked files, rebase conflict, undo conflict; `git stash list` empty after every test
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names GIT-02 and decisions 0005, 0038

**Notes:** the checkpoint commit respects the user's git hooks; a hook rejection stops the operation with a clear error and
never passes `--no-verify` (questions Q12, resolved; d0005 changelog, SDD GIT-02).

### T-0089 · Local merge in a scratch worktree
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** GIT-03, GIT-01, REV-08, EPC-03
- **Decisions:** 0015, 0038, 0059, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0047, T-0084, T-0088
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
Local merge is the user's approve action that needs no tokens (d0015, d0038). On the click, in a **Crewboard-owned scratch
worktree** (never the user's folder): (1) refuse if the ticket's worktree has **uncommitted files** ("SHOP-19 has 2 uncommitted
files", REV-08); (2) update the target — for a base branch: fetch, fast-forward if only behind, refuse if diverged (GIT-01); an
epic branch is local only; (3) **free rebase** of the ticket branch onto the latest target (checkpoint rule, T-0088); (4) clean →
**squash-merge** with the confirmed message; if the target is checked out in the user's folder and that checkout is **clean**,
fast-forward it; if it has **uncommitted changes**, refuse: "shop-web has uncommitted changes on main. Clean up and retry."; (5)
conflict → nothing is merged, the ticket stays in Ready for Review with **Send to agent to fix** (T-0090). Epic children merge into
the **epic branch** (EPC-03). **Local merge never pushes.** On success the ticket becomes Done · Merged.

**Done when**
- [x] `ticket.local_merge(key, squash_message)` implements steps 1–5 in order and returns `Merged`, `RefusedUncommitted{files}`, `RefusedDiverged`, `RefusedDirtyCheckout`, or `Conflict`
- [x] a proposed squash message (ticket key + title + agent summary) is available before the click and is editable by the caller
- [x] the scratch worktree lives under `~/.crewboard/` and is reused/cleaned by Crewboard; the user's checkout is only ever fast-forwarded, and only when clean
- [x] never pushes, never stashes, never commits in the user's folder; a dirty user checkout leaves the target branch unchanged
- [x] epic children target the epic's branch (created in T-0048); standalone tickets and epics target their base branch
- [x] on success: ticket → Done · Merged, `outcome = merged`, History entry, "ticket changed" event, and the "parent merged" hook for the scheduler (T-0081) fires
- [x] tests: temp git repos — clean merge with the base checked out clean in the "user folder" (fast-forwarded), base checked out dirty (refused, untouched), base behind remote (fast-forwarded first), diverged base (refused), uncommitted files in the ticket worktree (refused), rebase conflict (nothing merged), epic child into epic branch
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names GIT-03, GIT-01, REV-08, EPC-03 and decisions 0015, 0038, 0059, 0050

**Notes:** Done cleanup (worktree removal, ports) is T-0099; the early conflict flag after the merge is T-0096.

### T-0090 · Send to agent to fix (restack skill)
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** GIT-03
- **Decisions:** 0038
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0086, T-0089
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
When Local merge's free rebase conflicts, the ticket stays in Ready for Review with one button, **Send to agent to fix** (d0038).
That click returns the ticket to Dev and types the `restack` skill into its session (or `claude --resume` if the session is gone),
like Send back; it is the click that spends tokens. Add `plugin/skills/restack/SKILL.md`: run `crewboard prime`, follow the Agent
instructions, rebase onto the latest base (target named by `prime`), resolve conflicts, commit with the Commit skill, then
`crewboard review submit`. The ticket shows "conflicts with `<target>`" until it is back in Review.

**Done when**
- [x] the conflict result of T-0089 stores a "conflicts with `<target>`" flag and exposes the Send-to-agent-to-fix action
- [x] `ticket.send_to_fix(key)` moves Ready for Review → Dev (or Queued if the limit is on and full) and delivers `/crewboard:restack` through the user-click path (unsent-text rule applies)
- [x] `crewboard prime` names the rebase target for a ticket in this state
- [x] `restack/SKILL.md` contains: prime first, follow Agent instructions, rebase, resolve, Commit skill, `review submit --stdin`
- [x] tests: unit tests for the transition; PTY test with an ordinary command checking the typed text; skill lint test
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names GIT-03 and decision 0038

**Notes:** questions Q1, resolved: the `restack` skill stays in v0.1 for conflicts with the base (Send to agent to fix, reopen);
only its stacking uses are deferred (d0049 wording fixed).

### T-0091 · Diff tab: file list and unified view
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-01, REV-02
- **Decisions:** 0017
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 07 · Diff, review, approve & send back → Diff tab · SHOP-18 · unified (panel expanded), Dark · diff tab, Annotations
- **Depends on:** T-0059, T-0084
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
The Diff tab in the side panel (`crewboard-app`) shows SHOP-18's 9 files (+412 −38): the **agent's summary** at the top
(collapsible), the **base indicator** ("diff vs `main` at `a1b2c3d`"), a file list with status, per-file counts and **"viewed"
ticks**, and the unified diff with `syntect` highlighting (d0017). There is no test result: Crewboard runs no tests. The tab
header shows "+412 −38". Done tickets show the diff read-only.

**Done when**
- [x] the tab loads the file list and per-file hunks from T-0084 and renders the unified view with syntax highlighting by file extension
- [x] the agent's summary (from `review submit` or "Submitted by you") is shown collapsed/expanded and remembers the state per ticket
- [x] viewed ticks are toggled per file and kept per ticket and round; the file list shows added/modified/deleted/renamed icons and counts
- [x] the Diff tab indicator shows "+N −M"; Done tickets open read-only (no comment affordances)
- [x] no spinner for local data; files load lazily as they scroll into view
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for file list state, viewed ticks and summary collapse; checked by hand against the named frames (light and dark)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-01, REV-02 and decision 0017

### T-0092 · Diff tab: side-by-side and edge cases
- **Epic:** M6 · Review & Local merge
- **Requirements:** REV-02
- **Decisions:** 0017
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 07 · Diff, review, approve & send back → Side-by-side, Large diff · 54 files, Binary, renamed, empty
- **Depends on:** T-0091
