**What and why**
When an Asks you question carries options (T-0163), the inline answer box in the inbox (T-0120) lists them, numbered (ATT-12,
d0061). The recommended one is marked "Recommended" and **preselected**; **1–4** picks; **Enter** sends; **Other…** opens a
free-text field, so the user is never forced into the agent's list. Without a recommendation nothing is preselected and Send stays
disabled until the user picks (*Answer · options, no recommendation*). The answer is delivered with the usual rules (d0048) using
the text from `format_answer` (T-0163). Build the option list as a reusable component in `crewboard-app` for T-0166.

**Done when**
- [x] options render numbered with their labels; the recommended one has the badge and is preselected; 1–4 select; Enter sends the selection; Esc cancels
- [x] Other… switches to a free-text field; Enter sends that text
- [x] with no recommendation, nothing is preselected and Send/Enter is disabled until a choice is made
- [x] the delivered text is exactly "Answer to your question: Postmark (option 1)." (or the free text); the unsent-text prompt from T-0061 still applies; an Interrupted orange ticket resumes and delivers in one click (ATT-08)
- [x] questions without options keep the plain free-text box of T-0120
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for keyboard selection, preselection, disabled Send, Other…; checked by hand against the named frames in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-12, ATT-08 and decisions 0061, 0048, 0016

**Notes:** options appear only for `ask human` questions; agent-to-agent items stay free text (d0061). Design update I moved the
*Answer · inline* example onto an Asks you item (questions Q14, resolved). Dark: *10 · Needs-you inbox → Dark · answer with options*.

### T-0166 · Answer options on the card and in the panel
- **Epic:** M7 · Attention & recovery
- **Requirements:** ATT-12
- **Decisions:** 0061, 0048, 0042
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Row · answer options (0061) → Asks you · with options, Asks you · options · 3 picked; 05 · Ticket side panel → Row · answer options & delete (0060, 0061) → SHOP-21 · Asks you · answer options, Dark · options & delete; 06 · Terminal tab → Answering a question (SHOP-23) · focused
- **Depends on:** T-0075, T-0165
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
ATT-12 also puts the options on the **card** and in the **panel** (d0061). An Asks you card whose question has options shows them
compactly (numbered, recommended marked) in its callout, and the panel shows the same option list above the terminal for that
ticket, reusing the component from T-0165. Picking and sending works the same way (1–4, Enter, Other…), delivers with
`format_answer`, and clears the orange state like any answer (ATT-02). Typing straight into the terminal keeps working as before.

**Done when**
- [x] an Asks you card with options shows them numbered with the recommended one marked; clicking an option on the card opens the panel with that option preselected (sending stays one deliberate Enter or click)
- [x] the panel shows the option list above the terminal while the question is open; 1–4, Enter and Other… behave as in T-0165 when the list has focus, while keys go to the CLI when the terminal has focus (SES-10)
- [x] after delivery the list disappears and the card shows the after-answer state
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for card and panel option states and focus routing; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names ATT-12 and decisions 0061, 0048, 0042

**Notes:** the card and panel option frames were added by design update I (questions Q14, resolved); build to those frames.

## M8 · Usage
Goal: the user sees tokens per card and today, the plan meter before agents stop, and one calm banner when the plan limit is
hit; nothing starts while it lasts and nothing resumes until the user clicks Resume all.

### T-0125 · Token usage reader (read-only)
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-01
- **Decisions:** 0020
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0052
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Crewboard shows tokens used per ticket and today's total by **reading Claude Code's own session data read-only** for the Claude
session IDs it stored (d0020). Build a reader in `crewboard-core` that finds the transcript for each stored session ID, sums
input / output / cache tokens per session and per day, and writes the `usage` table. Claude Code's files are internal and may
change: a format change may only **break this display**, never a workflow (P8). Publish a "usage changed" event so the app can
update.

**Done when**
- [x] for every stored Claude session ID the reader locates its transcript and records tokens in/out/cache per session and day in `usage`
- [x] totals are available per ticket (all its sessions, including resumed and answer sessions) and for "today" across all projects
- [x] reading is incremental (only new lines since the last read) and runs on a slow tick plus on session state changes
- [x] an unreadable or unrecognised file logs one warning and yields "unknown" for that session; no error reaches tickets, sessions or the scheduler
- [x] the reader never writes to, moves or deletes anything under Claude Code's folders
- [x] tests: parsing from fixture transcript files (real-shaped lines), incremental reads, a malformed line, a missing file → "unknown"
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-01 and decision 0020

**Notes:** the transcript location and the usage fields must be verified against the installed Claude Code (name the version and
path layout in the PR). Fixture files are copied from a real session with content stripped.

### T-0126 · Tokens in header, cards and panel; dollar estimate
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-01, USG-02
- **Decisions:** 0020
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 02 · App shell & project switcher → Header states (Header · dollar estimate enabled); 15 · Settings → Global · Usage, notifications, data, appearance (Usage)
- **Depends on:** T-0045, T-0125
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Show the numbers from T-0125 in `crewboard-app`: "1.84M tokens today" in the header (all projects), a small token count on each
card and in the panel header, and per session in the History tab. Add the **dollar estimate** setting, **off by default** and
meant for API-key users (USG-02); when on, the header shows "≈ $38.20 today" and the figure says on hover that it is an estimate.

**Done when**
- [x] header shows today's total across all projects, updating on "usage changed" events without a spinner
- [x] cards, panel header and History rows show tokens; "unknown" renders as "—" with a tooltip, never as an error
- [x] Settings → Usage: token count "Always on", Dollar estimate toggle (off by default) stored in the app database
- [x] with the estimate on, the header shows "≈ $… today"; the price table used is in one place in the code and named in the PR
- [ ] tests: number formatting (1.84M, 182k), estimate calculation unit tests; checked by hand against *Header · dollar estimate enabled* and the Usage section
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-01, USG-02 and decision 0020

### T-0127 · Transparent status-line command and launch `--settings`
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-04, SES-03
- **Decisions:** 0020, 0012
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0052
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Reset times and the plan meter come from Claude Code's status-line data (d0020). Add a `crewboard` subcommand in `crewboard-cli`
(its name is an implementation choice) that reads the status-line JSON on stdin, records `rate_limits.five_hour` and
`rate_limits.seven_day` (`used_percentage`, `resets_at`) into `plan_limits` through the service, then runs the **user's own**
status-line command with the same stdin and prints its output **unchanged** (d0012). Agent launches (T-0052) now pass
`--settings <Crewboard status-line settings>` pointing at this command (SES-03). The user's settings files are never edited.

**Done when**
- [x] the subcommand records `rate_limits` when present and does nothing to the store when absent (API-key users)
- [x] it finds the user's configured status-line command (read-only from their Claude Code settings), runs it with the same stdin and passes its stdout through byte for byte; with none configured it prints nothing
- [x] it never blocks the status line: if the service is unreachable it still runs the user's command and exits quickly
- [x] agent launches add `--settings` with a generated settings file in `~/.crewboard/` (never in the worktree or the user's repo)
- [x] tests: real-shaped status-line JSON on stdin with and without `rate_limits`; an ordinary `echo` script as the "user's command" proves pass-through; service-down path
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-04, SES-03 and decisions 0020, 0012

**Notes:** the exact status-line JSON fields and the `--settings` merge behaviour must be verified with the installed Claude Code;
name the version in the PR.

### T-0128 · Limit detection (StopFailure) and reset time
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-03, USG-04
- **Decisions:** 0020, 0046
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0066, T-0127
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
"It happened" is detected by the plugin's **`StopFailure`** hook: error `rate_limit` plus the limit text ("You've hit your session
limit · resets 3:45pm" / "…weekly limit…") (d0020). Extend the hook mapping in `crewboard-core`: the agent shows **"Waiting for
limit reset"** (neutral, no inbox item, d0046) and the global `limit_active` flag in `plan_limits` turns on with its kind (session
/ weekly). The reset time comes from the recorded `rate_limits.resets_at`; fallback: parse it from the limit text; else "resets at
an unknown time".

**Done when**
- [x] `crewboard hook StopFailure` with `rate_limit` and the limit text sets the ticket to Waiting for limit reset and activates the limit (session or weekly)
- [x] other `StopFailure` errors don't touch the limit state (they keep their existing handling)
- [x] reset time resolution order: status-line data → parsed text → unknown; the result is stored with `observed_at`
- [x] when the recorded `used_percentage` falls back below 100 % after `resets_at`, the limit is marked as reset (bookkeeping only; nothing resumes)
- [x] tests: real-shaped StopFailure JSON fed to `crewboard hook StopFailure` for session, weekly, unknown-time and non-limit errors; reset-time parsing unit tests
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-03, USG-04 and decisions 0020, 0046

**Notes:** the hook payload fields and the exact limit wording must be verified by logging real payloads (SDD §11.4, §26).

### T-0129 · Plan-limit hold, no inbox items, Resume all → continue
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** SCH-04, USG-06, ATT-09
- **Decisions:** 0020, 0046, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0079, T-0115, T-0128
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
While the usage limit is active, **nothing starts** (SCH-04): the scheduler holds every Queued ticket with "held: plan limit".
Limit-stopped agents create **no inbox items and no OS notifications** (ATT-09, d0046). After the reset the user clicks **Resume
all**, which queues a "continue" message for every limit-stopped session as an **automatic delivery** (sent when the agent is idle
and its input line is empty, d0048). Nothing restarts on its own. Implement this in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] with `limit_active` the scheduler starts nothing and Queued tickets carry the "held: plan limit" reason; starts resume on the next scheduler pass after the user's Resume all or once the limit is no longer active
- [x] Waiting for limit reset tickets never produce inbox items or notifications; they count as working for the optional agent limit
- [x] a `usage.resume_all` request queues one "continue" delivery per limit-stopped session and clears the banner; it does nothing without the click
- [x] tests: scheduler unit test with the limit active; inbox derivation excludes limit-stopped tickets; Resume all queues deliveries that wait for an empty input line (PTY test with an ordinary command)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-04, USG-06, ATT-09 and decisions 0020, 0046, 0048

### T-0130 · Plan meter in the header
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-05
- **Decisions:** 0020
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 02 · App shell & project switcher → Header states (Header · plan meter (Pro / Max))
- **Depends on:** T-0033, T-0127
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Show the limit before agents stop: **"5h · 82% · week · 40%"** in the header of `crewboard-app`, with reset times on hover
("5-hour session 82% · resets 3:45 pm (in 1 h 20 min)", "Week 40% · resets Mon 12:00 am"). A value turns amber from 80 %. Pro/Max
only: the meter is **hidden when no `rate_limits` data has been recorded** (API-key users, who get Claude Code's automatic
retries instead).

**Done when**
- [x] the meter shows both percentages from the latest `plan_limits` row and updates on "usage changed" events
- [x] amber from 80 %; colour is paired with the number, never the only signal
- [x] hover popover shows both reset times and "New tickets hold at 100 % · nothing resumes by itself"
- [x] hidden when no data exists; stale data older than its `resets_at` shows the percentage as unknown
- [ ] tests: view-model unit tests (formatting, amber threshold, hidden state); checked by hand against *Header · plan meter (Pro / Max)*
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-05 and decision 0020

### T-0131 · Usage-limit banner
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-06, USG-08
- **Decisions:** 0020, 0046
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → Banners (Usage limit · just hit, Usage limit · waiting for reset, Usage limit · after reset, Usage limit · weekly, Usage limit · reset time unknown); 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Dev · waiting for limit reset; 10 · Needs-you inbox → Usage limit active · no duplicates
- **Depends on:** T-0017, T-0129
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
One banner, never one per agent (P6): **"Usage limit reached · resets at 3:45 pm · no new tickets will start"**, with session,
weekly ("resets Mon 12:00am") and unknown-time variants and a countdown. After the reset, **Resume all** is highlighted; clicking
it calls `usage.resume_all` (T-0129). Cards of limit-stopped agents show the neutral "Waiting for limit reset" status. Build in
`crewboard-app` using the banner area of T-0017 and its stacking rules.

**Done when**
- [x] the banner appears once when the limit activates, showing how many agents stopped ("8 agents stopped")
- [x] countdown to the reset time; at the reset the banner switches to the "after reset" state with Resume all highlighted
- [x] session / weekly / unknown-time wording matches the frames; Resume all is available but never triggered automatically
- [x] cards show "Waiting for limit reset" (neutral, not orange); the inbox shows no per-agent items while the banner is active
- [x] a limit with no reset time (e.g. an enterprise plan) shows the "reset time unknown" variant, and Resume all is its retry; no enterprise-specific banner, Request more link or Retry button exists (USG-08)
- [ ] tests: banner view-model unit tests for every state and the countdown; checked by hand against the named frames in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-06, USG-08 and decisions 0020, 0046

**Notes:** questions Q7, resolved → option 2: the "Usage limit · enterprise" frames are labelled "after v0.1" (design update H)
and are **not** built (SDD USG-08, d0020 changelog).

### T-0132 · Safety net: "This is the usage limit"
- **Epic:** M8 · Usage
- **Requirements:** USG-07
- **Decisions:** 0020
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 10 · Needs-you inbox → Row · Idle, after reboot (Idle · “This is the usage limit” (safety net, 0020), After “This is the usage limit”)
- **Depends on:** T-0120, T-0129
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
If detection misses (the CLI changed its wording), many agents just look Idle. Every **Idle** inbox item gets a **"This is the
usage limit"** button (d0020). One click activates the limit state (unknown reset time unless status-line data has one), folds all
current Idle items into the single banner and marks those agents **"Waiting for limit reset"**. Core request in `crewboard-core`,
button in `crewboard-app`.

**Done when**
- [x] the button appears only on Idle items (not on Asks you / Needs you)
- [x] one click: limit active, every Idle ticket becomes Waiting for limit reset, their inbox items disappear, the banner appears once
- [x] the action is logged in each ticket's History ("marked as usage limit by you")
- [ ] tests: core unit test with three Idle tickets and one Asks you ticket; checked by hand against the two named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names USG-07 and decision 0020

## M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
Goal: the user can run any project's run command per ticket on its own port, open a plain shell in the worktree, and use a
Project terminal in the repo folder — all click-only and owned by the service.

### T-0133 · Run process manager (core)
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-01
- **Decisions:** 0035, 0026, 0045
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0046, T-0050
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
**Run** starts the project's run command in the ticket's worktree with `$PORT` = the ticket's **Run port** (d0026, d0035), only
on the user's click. The service in `crewboard-core` owns the process, captures its output log (streamed as "run output" events)
and tracks its state: not started, starting, running (`▶ :4020`), stopped, failed to start (exit with log), port in use. Run
**stops at Done and on Close** (hooks from T-0099 / T-0100, with a toast event) and shows **"stopped by restart"** after a reboot
or service restart; it is never restarted automatically (d0045).

**Done when**
- [x] `run.start` / `run.stop` requests; start refuses without a run command (the UI then asks for one, T-0135)
- [x] the process gets the ticket's env, cwd = worktree, `PORT` = Run port; stdout/stderr go to a bounded log kept by the service
- [x] "port in use" is detected before start (the Run port is taken by another process) and reported with the port number
- [x] an early exit becomes "failed to start" with the log kept; Stop terminates the whole process group
- [x] reaching Done or Close stops it and emits a toast event ("Run stopped · SHOP-19 is done"); reconcile marks it "stopped by restart"
- [x] tests: ordinary commands (`sh -c 'echo ready; sleep 30'`, an immediately failing command, a listener on the port) for each state
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-01 and decisions 0035, 0026, 0045

### T-0134 · Run tab, card chip, Open URL
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-01, RUN-03
- **Decisions:** 0035
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 13 · Run & Shell → Not started, Starting, Running, Stopped, Failed to start, Port already in use, Stopped by restart, Reaching Done stops it, Card & panel-header controls, Run · backend (BILL-4) · no Open URL, Dark · Run tab
- **Depends on:** T-0062, T-0133
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The panel's **Run** tab in `crewboard-app` shows the Run port, Run / Stop, and the live output log (monospace) for every state in
the frames. Cards get a `▶ :4020` chip whose hover shows Open / Stop; the panel header shows a compact running pill. **Open URL**
appears only if the project sets one (e.g. `http://localhost:$PORT/swagger`), with `$PORT` replaced by the Run port, and opens the
**system browser** (RUN-03). No proxy, no embedded browser, no auto-start.

**Done when**
- [x] Run tab renders every state in the frames, with "edit command" on failure and a hint on port in use
- [x] card chip and panel-header pill appear only while running; the Run tab indicator shows "running :4020"
- [x] Open is hidden when the project has no Open URL (BILL-4 backend case) and otherwise opens the substituted URL in the default browser
- [x] the log view stays responsive with long output and follows the end unless scrolled up
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for state → controls and URL substitution; checked by hand against the named frames in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-01, RUN-03 and decision 0035

### T-0135 · Run-command dialog with Ask Claude
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-02
- **Decisions:** 0026, 0035, 0023
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 13 · Run & Shell → 1 · Empty, 2 · Claude thinking, 3 · Suggestion, 4 · Editing, 5 · Saved
- **Depends on:** T-0134
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The first Run in a project asks "What command starts this project?" and saves the answer in the project settings in the app
database (never in the repo, d0023). **Ask Claude** is a click that launches `claude` non-interactively in the project folder to
look at the repo and suggest a command with a short explanation; the user confirms or edits it. There are **no auto-detection
rules** (RUN-02). Dialog in `crewboard-app`, the suggestion request in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] clicking Run without a run command opens the dialog; Save stores it and starts the Run
- [x] Ask Claude runs only on click, shows the thinking state, then the suggestion and explanation in an editable field; Cancel stops the `claude` process
- [x] a failed or empty suggestion shows an error and leaves the field editable; nothing is saved without the user's Save
- [x] the same command is editable later in project settings (T-0045 field)
- [x] tests: dialog view-model tests for all five states; the suggestion request tested with an ordinary command in place of `claude` that prints a fixed answer
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-02 and decisions 0026, 0035, 0023

**Notes:** the exact non-interactive invocation and prompt text are implementation choices; the prompt lives in `plugin/` or a
Markdown file, not in a Rust string literal (PLG-01 spirit).

### T-0136 · Running processes list in the header
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-04
- **Decisions:** 0035
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 13 · Run & Shell → All running processes · header menu
- **Depends on:** T-0033, T-0133
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The header shows **"▶ 3 processes"** across all projects; clicking opens a menu listing each running Run process (project, key,
title, port, uptime) with Stop and a jump to its ticket. It lets the user find and stop forgotten processes quickly.

**Done when**
- [x] the count updates live from run events; hidden or quiet at zero as in the frame
- [x] each row has Stop (stops only that process) and opens the ticket's Run tab on click
- [ ] tests: view-model unit test for grouping and counts; checked by hand against the named frame
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-04 and decision 0035

### T-0137 · Shell tab
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-05
- **Decisions:** 0035, 0026
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 13 · Run & Shell → Shell tab · BILL-4, Dark · Shell
- **Depends on:** T-0046, T-0050, T-0056
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The **Shell** tab is a plain terminal (not Claude) in the ticket's worktree with `$PORT` = the Run port and the ticket's env
(`CREWBOARD_TICKET` set), so the user can curl an API or run tests (d0035). It is a service-owned PTY session (kind `shell`), so it
survives closing the window, and it **closes when the ticket reaches Done** (or is closed). After a reboot it shows "stopped by
restart" and is never reopened automatically. Reuses the terminal view of T-0056.

**Done when**
- [x] opening the tab starts the shell session on first use (the user's login shell), cwd = worktree, `PORT` = Run port
- [x] reopening the panel or the app reattaches with scrollback; closing the window keeps it running
- [x] Done/Close ends the session; reconcile marks it "stopped by restart"; nothing restarts it without a click
- [x] the Shell has full `crewboard` access as the user's own terminal (it is not an agent session)
- [x] tests: PTY test with the shell running `echo $PORT`; session ends on Done
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-05 and decisions 0035, 0026

### T-0138 · Project terminal
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** RUN-06
- **Decisions:** 0019, 0031
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 17 · Project terminal → Empty · shell prompt, Running · creating tickets, Plain shell use, Ended, Cycle rejected by the tool, Adding a ticket to an epic (0039), Dark · Project terminal, claude running
- **Depends on:** T-0050, T-0056, T-0065
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
**⇧⌘T** opens the **Project terminal**: a plain terminal in the project's own folder (the user's checkout, e.g. `~/code/shop-web`),
one per project, owned by the service (kind `project`), surviving window close (d0019). It doesn't start Claude; `crewboard` is
on `PATH` with **full access** (CLI-03), and the user may run `claude` there themselves. Changes made there are the user's own.
Tickets created from it (by the user or a `claude` they started) appear in Backlog in real time with a subtle highlight.

**Done when**
- [x] ⇧⌘T and the header button open/focus the current project's terminal; the empty state shows the hint "run `claude` to plan with your skills, or `crewboard create`"
- [x] the session has cwd = repo folder, `crewboard` on `PATH`, no `CREWBOARD_TICKET`; the service treats its callers as the user (full access)
- [x] new tickets created there appear in Backlog live with a short highlight; a cycle refused by `crewboard dep add` shows the CLI error in the session
- [x] the session ends only when the user exits the shell (shows "Ended" with a reopen button) or after a restart ("stopped by restart")
- [x] tests: PTY test running `crewboard create "Checkout API"` from the session creates a Backlog ticket as the user; view-model tests for states
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-06 and decisions 0019, 0031

**Notes:** questions Q6, resolved: the summary of created tickets with delete ("The review moment", "Undo a created ticket") is
T-0167 (TKT-08, d0060).

### T-0167 · Project terminal: summary of created tickets, with delete
- **Epic:** M9 · Run, Shell, Project terminal
- **Requirements:** TKT-08, RUN-06
- **Decisions:** 0060, 0019, 0031
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 17 · Project terminal → The review moment, Undo a created ticket
- **Depends on:** T-0138, T-0161, T-0162
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
When tickets are created from the Project terminal (by the user or a `claude` they started there), a summary above the prompt lists
what was made, e.g. "4 tickets, 3 dependencies · all in Backlog", with each ticket's key, title, dependency badges and number of
"Done when" items, and links to open them (*The review moment*). Each row has a trash button that deletes that ticket after the
confirmation from T-0162 (TKT-08, d0060). Nothing starts: "Nothing runs until you drag a ticket to Dev." Build in `crewboard-app`
with the data from `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] the service attributes each created ticket to the Project terminal session it came from (by caller identity, T-0064); the summary lists the tickets created from this session since it was last dismissed, updating live
- [x] counts of tickets and dependencies are correct; rows link to the ticket's panel
- [x] trash on a row opens the T-0162 confirmation (naming removed links, e.g. "Its link to SHOP-20 is removed too"); after delete the row disappears and the counts update
- [x] a ticket that was dragged to Dev meanwhile shows no trash (it can only be closed)
- [ ] tests: core test that tickets created from a Project terminal session are attributed to it; view-model tests for the summary and delete flow; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-08, RUN-06 and decisions 0060, 0019, 0031

**Notes:** when the summary first appears (on the first ticket created, or when creation pauses) is an implementation choice; it
must never cover the live terminal output.

## M10 · PR flow & agent questions
Goal: delivery units reach the remote as one reviewed PR through the user's skills and a confirmation dialog, PR state is marked
by hand, and linked agents can ask each other questions through Crewboard.

### T-0139 · Workflow skills settings and resolution
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-09
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0040
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Global · Usage, notifications, data, appearance (Workflow skills), Project · configured + invalid value
- **Depends on:** T-0045
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Commit, Push and Open PR are skills the user can replace: each has a **global default** and a **per-project override** (empty =
global), defaults `/crewboard:commit`, `/crewboard:push`, `/crewboard:open-pr` (d0050, same pattern as Agent instructions,
d0040). Add the settings in `crewboard-core` with one resolver used by `crewboard prime` (Commit) and the PR flow (Push, Open
PR), and the fields in `crewboard-app`. Stored in the app, never in the repo.

**Done when**
- [x] resolver returns project override → global → built-in default for each step
- [x] `crewboard prime` prints the resolved Commit skill name so `work-ticket` / `address-review` use it
- [x] Settings global and project sections show the three fields; a value that isn't a `/skill-name` is flagged inline
- [ ] tests: resolver unit tests; prime output snapshot with an override; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-09 and decisions 0050, 0040

### T-0140 · Built-in push, open-pr and answer-question skills
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-09, REV-10, REV-11, REV-12, MSG-03
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0017, 0030
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0073
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Add three Markdown skills to `plugin/skills/` (agent behaviour lives in the plugin, PLG-01). **open-pr**: first runs `crewboard
prime`, then **proposes, doesn't push**: branch name (default `crewboard/SHOP-18-<slug>`, or per the user's rules / the External ID,
e.g. `feature/PAY-123-wishlist`), one message per commit, PR title and description, via `crewboard pr propose --stdin`; after
Crewboard confirms, it pushes and opens the PR with `gh` (github.com) or `az repos pr create` (dev.azure.com / visualstudio.com),
then reports `crewboard pr opened <url>`. **push**: pushes the confirmed branch for Push to PR. **answer-question**: answer the
linked agent's question only, change nothing, reply with `crewboard answer`. Auth reuses `gh` / `az` logins; no tokens stored
(REV-12).

**Done when**
- [x] `open-pr/SKILL.md`, `push/SKILL.md`, `answer-question/SKILL.md` exist; each starts with `crewboard prime` and follows Agent instructions
- [x] open-pr never pushes before Crewboard tells it to; it uses `--stdin` for every long text (PLG-05)
- [x] open-pr detects the provider from the remote URL the same way as T-0141 and stops with a clear message for other remotes ("use your own Open PR skill")
- [x] answer-question forbids file changes and commits and ends with `crewboard answer <ID> --stdin`
- [ ] tests: skill files lint (frontmatter present, referenced commands exist in the CLI); real `claude` behaviour checked by hand on a scratch GitHub repo
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-09, REV-10, REV-11, REV-12, MSG-03 and decisions 0050, 0017, 0030

### T-0141 · PR provider detection and login status
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-11, REV-12
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0017
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → PR provider · not detected (0050), Project · configured + invalid value
- **Depends on:** T-0013, T-0045
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
There is **no provider setting**: it is detected from the remote URL (REV-11). `github.com` → GitHub (`gh`); `dev.azure.com` /
`visualstudio.com` → Azure DevOps (`az`). Project settings show it read-only, e.g. "GitHub (detected from origin) · gh: logged
in"; other remotes (the `crewboard` sample at `git.acme.internal`) show "Not detected: use your own Open PR skill", and Open PR then
works only with a custom skill. Login status reuses the tools check (T-0013); `GH_TOKEN` / `AZURE_DEVOPS_EXT_PAT` work because
the CLIs read them. No token is stored (REV-12).

**Done when**
- [x] detection handles HTTPS and SSH remote forms for both providers and returns Not detected otherwise
- [x] project settings show provider + login status read-only, refreshed with Re-check
- [x] Approve ▾ disables Open PR with "PR provider not detected" when the project uses the built-in Open PR skill and the provider is not detected
- [x] nothing in Crewboard's database or files holds a provider token
- [ ] tests: URL parsing unit tests (github.com, dev.azure.com, `*.visualstudio.com`, ssh, git.acme.internal); checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-11, REV-12 and decisions 0050, 0017

### T-0142 · CLI: `pr propose`, `pr opened`, `branch rename`
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-10, GIT-07
- **Decisions:** 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0065
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Add the three agent commands to `crewboard-cli`, enforced by the service (agent callers only on their own ticket, CLI-02).
`crewboard pr propose --stdin` takes the proposal (branch name, commit messages in order, PR title, description) as structured
Markdown or JSON on stdin. `crewboard pr opened <url>` records the PR URL and number. `crewboard branch rename <name>` renames the
temporary branch and is **refused after the first push** (GIT-07); History logs the rename.

**Done when**
- [x] `pr propose --stdin` validates the shape and stores the proposal on the ticket; a malformed proposal returns a JSON error
- [x] `pr opened <url>` parses PR number for GitHub and Azure DevOps URLs and emits a ticket-changed event
- [x] `branch rename` refuses once the branch is locked ("SHOP-18's branch was pushed as `bugfix/SHOP-18-login-redirect` and can't be renamed")
- [x] an agent calling these for another ticket is refused ("SHOP-18's agent can't change SHOP-12")
- [x] tests: CLI tests with stdin fixtures; rename before/after lock against a temp git repo
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-10, GIT-07 and decision 0050

**Notes:** the exact proposal format is an implementation choice; document it in the open-pr skill.

### T-0143 · Open PR flow (core): proposal, validation, apply edits, push
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-10
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0140, T-0142
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
The Open PR flow in `crewboard-core`, for **delivery units only** (standalone tickets and epics; epic children never offer it,
d0059). On the user's click: refuse with uncommitted files (REV-08); run the resolved Open PR skill in the ticket's session
(resumed with `claude --resume` if gone, as part of the click); state "Open PR skill is preparing…" until `pr propose` arrives.
The dialog then returns the user's edits; **Crewboard applies them itself** — rename the branch, reword commit messages (no tokens,
no file changes) — locks the branch name, then types the instruction to push and open the PR with the confirmed title and
description. `pr opened` moves the ticket to **Approved · PR open**.

**Done when**
- [x] branch-name validation: valid ref name, not taken locally, not taken on the remote (`git ls-remote`); used live by the dialog
- [x] applying edits rewrites only commit messages (tree hashes unchanged, checked) and renames the branch; failure leaves the branch as it was
- [x] the push instruction is delivered as a user-click delivery (unsent-text check of T-0054); nothing is pushed before the confirm
- [x] Cancel discards the proposal; the ticket stays in Ready for Review
- [x] `pr opened` → Approved · PR open with PR number/URL; the branch is locked
- [x] tests: temp git repo + local bare repo as "remote" for validation, reword (tree equality) and rename; state machine unit tests; skill steps fed through the CLI commands, no fake `claude`
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-10 and decisions 0050, 0059

**Notes:** for an epic the skill runs in the delivery session of T-0146; this ticket provides the shared flow.

### T-0144 · Push to PR and PR rework (core)
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-14, REV-10
- **Decisions:** 0041, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0143
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
PR rework (d0041): **drag Approved → Ready for Review** is allowed; the PR chip stays; then the normal Send back. The agent never
pushes by itself. When a ticket with an open PR is in Review again, Approve offers **"Push to PR #481"**: the same proposal and
dialog flow with the **branch locked** and only the **new** commits' messages editable; on confirm Crewboard rewords them, the Push
skill pushes, and the ticket returns to **Approved · PR open**. For epics: adding or reopening a child returns the epic to Backlog;
once merged it is back in Review and offers Push to PR. Core in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] Approved → Ready for Review transition keeps `pr_url`/`pr_number`; Send back works from there as usual
- [x] Approve on a ticket with a PR offers only Push to PR (Open PR and Local merge hidden for it) and refuses with uncommitted files
- [x] the proposal lists only commits not on the remote branch; already-pushed commits are read-only
- [x] after the skill reports success, the ticket is Approved · PR open with the same PR number
- [x] tests: temp repo + bare remote: push a branch, add two commits, verify only those two are editable and reworded; transition unit tests
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-14, REV-10 and decisions 0041, 0050

### T-0145 · Pushed-content verification
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-10, REV-06
- **Decisions:** 0043, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0097, T-0143
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
After a push (Open PR or Push to PR), Crewboard checks that the pushed **content** equals what the user reviewed (d0050, d0043).
It compares trees, not commit IDs, because the dialog rewords commits. A mismatch shows the "changed since you reviewed it"
warning of T-0097 with the diff of only the extra content.

**Done when**
- [x] after `pr opened` (or the push skill's success), the remote branch tree is fetched and compared with the reviewed tree
- [x] equal → nothing shown; different → the changed-since-review warning with only the difference, on the ticket and as a toast
- [x] reworded-only commits never trigger the warning
- [x] tests: temp repo + bare remote: reword-only push (no warning), extra commit pushed (warning)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-10, REV-06 and decisions 0043, 0050

### T-0146 · Delivery session for epics
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** EPC-08
- **Decisions:** 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0105, T-0143
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
An epic has no agent, so Open PR / Push to PR on an epic (SHOP-40, PAY-123) runs the skill in a **short-lived `claude` session in
the epic's scratch worktree** (EPC-08), launched **only by the user's click** — the one `claude` launched for an epic. The proposal
has **one commit per merged child** (SHOP-40.1, SHOP-40.2…), branch e.g. `feature/PAY-123-wishlist`, title "PAY-123 Wishlist".
The session ends after `crewboard pr opened` (or Cancel); its Claude session ID is stored (session kind `delivery`).

**Done when**
- [x] the click creates/reuses the epic's scratch worktree on the epic branch and starts a `delivery` session with `CREWBOARD_TICKET=SHOP-40`
- [x] the epic branch is prepared with one commit per merged child before the proposal (each child's squash commit kept as-is)
- [x] after `pr opened` or Cancel the session ends and the scratch worktree is removed unless it has uncommitted changes (then listed in Cleanup)
- [x] the epic card shows Approved · PR open with "PR #490" after success
- [x] tests: temp repo with three child squash merges → three commits on the delivery branch; session lifecycle tested with an ordinary command in place of `claude`
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names EPC-08 and decision 0059

### T-0147 · Mark merged / Mark closed
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-13, CLS-02, SCH-05
- **Decisions:** 0017, 0033, 0049
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0081, T-0100, T-0143
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Crewboard **never watches PRs**: no polling, no webhooks (d0017). In Approved · PR open the user clicks **Mark merged** → Done ·
Merged, then the base is fetched before the tickets that just became ready start (SCH-05, T-0081); or **Mark closed** → Done ·
Closed · not merged, closing it like Close ticket (CLS-02, T-0100). Both are requests in `crewboard-core`.

**Done when**
- [x] Mark merged: Done · Merged, Done cleanup (T-0099), then base fetch and scheduler pass; dependents start from the fetched base
- [x] Mark closed asks for confirmation, then behaves like Close ticket (branch kept, worktree removed unless dirty, Run/Shell stop)
- [x] no background job queries GitHub or Azure DevOps at any time
- [x] tests: temp repo + bare remote: a dependent ticket starts from the fetched base after Mark merged; Mark closed state and cleanup
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-13, CLS-02, SCH-05 and decisions 0017, 0033, 0049

### T-0148 · PR comments in `review feedback`
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-05
- **Decisions:** 0017
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0087, T-0141
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`crewboard review feedback` returns in-app comments plus **unresolved PR comments fetched on demand** when the agent asks for them
(REV-05), using `gh` or `az` with the user's own login. PR feedback **never** re-queues a ticket by itself: the user presses Send
back. Implemented in `crewboard-core` (the CLI stays thin).

**Done when**
- [x] for a ticket with a PR, the command appends unresolved PR review comments (file, line, author, text) after the in-app ones
- [x] GitHub via `gh api` and Azure DevOps via `az repos pr` (BILL-5); an undetected provider or a logged-out CLI adds a one-line note instead of failing
- [x] no PR data is fetched except during this command
- [x] tests: parsing of recorded `gh` / `az` JSON fixtures; ordinary scripts named `gh`/`az` on a test `PATH` returning the fixtures
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-05 and decision 0017

### T-0149 · Open PR / Push to PR dialog UI
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-10, REV-14, UI-07
- **Decisions:** 0050, 0041, 0059
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 07 · Diff, review, approve & send back → Open PR · skill is preparing…, Open PR · confirmation (SHOP-18, 0050), Open PR · pushing, Open PR · success, Open PR · invalid or taken branch name, Open PR · not logged in, Approve · Push to PR #481 (0041), Push to PR · confirmation (branch locked, 0050), Open PR · epic SHOP-40 (PAY-123); 05 · Ticket side panel → Approve ▾ on an epic
- **Depends on:** T-0095, T-0143, T-0144
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
In `crewboard-app`, Approve ▾ gains **Open PR** for delivery units and **Push to PR #N** for tickets with a PR. The confirmation
dialog shows the target branch and makes **everything editable**: branch name (validated live: valid ref, not taken locally or on
the remote), one field per commit message, PR title and description; **Push & open PR** / **Cancel**. States: skill preparing,
pushing, success (PR #481 + link, toast "PR opened #481"), invalid/taken name, and the inline error "`gh` not logged in · run `gh
auth login`" (UI-07). Push to PR uses the same dialog with the branch locked and only new commits editable.

**Done when**
- [x] every state in the named frames is reachable and rendered; Push & open PR is disabled while the name is invalid
- [x] epic variant shows one commit per merged child and the External ID in the proposed name/title
- [x] Cancel at any point before pushing leaves the ticket in Ready for Review with nothing pushed
- [x] the uncommitted-files refusal (View · Send back) and the changed-since-review warning are shown before the dialog when they apply
- [ ] tests: dialog view-model tests (validation debounce, locked branch, per-commit fields); checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-10, REV-14, UI-07 and decisions 0050, 0041, 0059

### T-0150 · PR open state UI and PR rework drag
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** REV-13, REV-14
- **Decisions:** 0017, 0041
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → SHOP-26 · Approved, PR open; 07 · Diff, review, approve & send back → Send back · ticket with an Azure DevOps PR (BILL-5); 03 · Board & columns → PR rework · Approved → Ready for Review (allowed); 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Approved · PR open, Review · PR rework, Review · after rework
- **Depends on:** T-0144, T-0147
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Show the PR life cycle in `crewboard-app`: Approved · PR open cards with "PR #481 · GitHub"; the panel action bar with **Open PR
in browser**, **Mark merged**, **Mark closed** (confirmation) and the note that **Crewboard doesn't watch PRs**. Dragging Approved
→ Ready for Review is allowed (PR chip stays); the Send back dialog on a ticket with a PR explains that PR comments are fetched for
the agent (BILL-5, Azure DevOps).

**Done when**
- [x] card and panel render every named state; the PR chip persists through rework
- [x] Mark merged / Mark closed call T-0147; success toasts ("SHOP-26 merged · 2 waiting tickets started")
- [x] the drag rule Approved → Ready for Review shows allowed feedback; other drags out of Approved stay forbidden
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for action bar and card flags; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REV-13, REV-14 and decisions 0017, 0041

### T-0151 · Agent-to-agent questions: ask, answer, questions, live delivery
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** MSG-01, MSG-02, MSG-04
- **Decisions:** 0030, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0054, T-0065
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard ask <KEY> --stdin` works **only between directly linked tickets** (parent ↔ child) and is **non-blocking** (MSG-01).
Every question and answer is stored in `messages` and logged on both tickets. If the target has a **live session**, the question
is an automatic delivery: typed when its agent is idle and its input line is empty (MSG-02, d0048); it answers with `crewboard
answer <ID> --stdin`, and the answer is delivered back to the asker the same way. `crewboard questions` lists pending ones. A target
that **never had a session** is rejected (MSG-04). Service in `crewboard-core`, commands in `crewboard-cli`.

**Done when**
- [x] `ask` to a non-linked ticket is refused; to a linked ticket it returns a question ID immediately
- [x] live target: delivery waits for idle + empty input line; "1 message waiting · Deliver now" appears meanwhile
- [x] `answer` records the answer and queues delivery to the asker; `questions` lists pending questions for the current ticket
- [x] never-started target → "SHOP-18 hasn't started yet, so there is no progress to ask about. Ask the human instead (`crewboard ask human`)."
- [x] tests: core unit tests for link checks and rejection texts; PTY test (ordinary command) for held delivery
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names MSG-01, MSG-02, MSG-04 and decisions 0030, 0048

### T-0152 · Finished tickets answer automatically
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** MSG-03
- **Decisions:** 0030, 0018
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0140, T-0151
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
The one explicit exception to "nothing launches `claude` without a click" (d0018): when a question targets a ticket with **no live
session but a stored Claude session** (normally a merged parent) and the project setting is on, Crewboard **resumes it
automatically, answer-only** (MSG-03). It recreates the worktree **at the same path** on the target's kept branch, runs `claude
--resume <id>`, types the question with the `answer-question` skill, and after `crewboard answer` ends the session and removes the
temporary worktree — **kept and listed in Cleanup** if files changed. The target card shows "answering SHOP-40.2". The plan-limit
hold applies. If the conversation can't be resumed → reject with "SHOP-18's conversation is no longer available".

**Done when**
- [x] worktree recreated at the stored path on the kept branch; the session kind is `answer` and its Claude session ID is stored
- [x] the question is delivered with the answer-question skill; the session ends after the answer is recorded
- [x] the temporary worktree is removed only if clean; otherwise kept and flagged in Cleanup
- [x] while the plan limit is active the question waits; a resume that fails rejects the ask with the "no longer available" text
- [x] the answering state is visible on the target ticket and logged in both tickets' Messages
- [x] tests: temp git repo for worktree recreation and cleanup decision; lifecycle tested with an ordinary command in place of `claude`; limit-hold unit test
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names MSG-03 and decisions 0030, 0018

**Notes:** questions Q2, resolved: follow 0030 / MSG-03 (auto-resume answer-only when the setting is on, inbox when off);
decisions 0045 and 0049 were reworded to match.

### T-0153 · "Let finished tickets answer questions" and inbox routing
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** MSG-05
- **Decisions:** 0030
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 10 · Needs-you inbox → Agent question for you · new item type, Answer · inline; 15 · Settings → Project · configured + invalid value
- **Depends on:** T-0118, T-0151
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Per-project setting **"Let finished tickets answer questions"**, **on by default** (MSG-05). When off, questions to a ticket
without a live session go to the inbox (group 1) as "SHOP-21 asks SHOP-17 (Done): which Stripe API version?", where the user
answers in a free-text box; the answer is delivered to the asker and logged on both tickets. The item also offers "Resume SHOP-17"
to let that agent answer (a click).

**Done when**
- [x] the setting is stored per project and shown in project settings (default on)
- [x] off → no automatic resume; an inbox item appears in group 1 with project, keys, question and age
- [x] answering delivers to the asker under the automatic-delivery rules and removes the item; "Resume SHOP-17" runs the T-0152 flow on click
- [ ] tests: routing unit tests for on/off; inbox item derivation; checked by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names MSG-05 and decision 0030

**Notes:** answers here stay free text: decision 0061 limits answer options to `ask human`; agent-to-agent questions (`ask
<KEY>`), including inbox-routed ones, have no options. In the design the "SHOP-21 asks SHOP-17 (Done)" item is free text
(design update I, questions Q14 resolved).

### T-0154 · Messages section, "answering" and "asked · waiting" card flags
- **Epic:** M10 · PR flow & agent questions
- **Requirements:** MSG-01, MSG-03, MSG-06
- **Decisions:** 0030
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → Messages · agent-to-agent (SHOP-40.2), Message states, Dark · Messages; 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / 04 · Ticket card variants · Dark → Done · answering a linked ticket, Working · asked another agent
- **Depends on:** T-0062, T-0151
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The panel's **Messages** section in `crewboard-app` lists questions and answers between this ticket and its directly linked
tickets, with states asked, delivered, answered and "sent to your inbox", e.g. SHOP-20 → SHOP-18: "What does POST /checkout
return on a declined card?". It is **read-only** except answering an inbox-routed question. A target being resumed to answer shows
the card flag **"answering SHOP-40.2"** (MSG-03). The **asker's** card shows **"asked SHOP-40.1 · waiting"** until the answer is
delivered; it ranks **below every orange status** in the flag priority (MSG-06, d0030). `crewboard show --long` prints the same
messages.

**Done when**
- [x] Messages renders every state in the frames, newest last, with timestamps and direction
- [x] the answering flag appears on the target card while its answer session runs and disappears when it ends
- [x] the asker's card shows "asked SHOP-40.1 · waiting" from the moment `crewboard ask SHOP-40.1` is accepted until the answer is **delivered** (not merely answered); with several open questions it names the oldest and counts the rest ("asked SHOP-40.1 +1 · waiting")
- [x] the flag priority function of T-0031 places "asked · waiting" below Asks you / Needs you / Idle (an orange status always wins the pill; the asked flag falls back to the meta row)
- [x] `crewboard show --long` includes messages
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for message states; priority unit test (Idle + asked → Idle pill, asked in meta row); flag cleared on delivery, not on answer; checked by hand against the named frames in light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names MSG-01, MSG-03, MSG-06 and decision 0030

**Notes:** questions Q9, resolved → option 1 (the flag joins this ticket). Answers to agent-to-agent questions stay free text
(d0061 scope).

## M11 · Windows & Linux
Goal: the same app, service and agents run on Windows and Linux, checked with the same checks in local Windows and Linux
virtual machines (d0056). This is the last milestone of v0.1.

### T-0155 · Windows: named pipe with an owner-only ACL
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02, SVC-02
- **Decisions:** 0021, 0027
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0009
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
On Windows the service listens on a **named pipe** (the "socket" path under `~/.crewboard/` maps to a pipe name) with an
**owner-only ACL**, so no other account can drive agents (d0027). The client library, daemon start/stop and single-instance check
work the same way. Implemented in `crewboard-core` behind the transport abstraction used on macOS.

**Done when**
- [x] the pipe is created with a security descriptor granting access only to the current user's SID
- [x] daemon start/stop/status, reconnect and the event stream pass the same tests as on macOS
- [ ] a connection from another user is refused (checked once by hand on a second local account)
- [x] tests: transport tests run in the Windows VM; ACL inspection test
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02, SVC-02 and decisions 0021, 0027

### T-0156 · Windows: ConPTY sessions, paths and caller identity
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02, CLI-01
- **Decisions:** 0021, 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0050, T-0064
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Sessions on Windows use ConPTY through `portable-pty`; data stays in `~/.crewboard/` (the user profile folder), worktree paths use
Windows separators, and the agent launch sets `PATH` with `;`. Caller identity by **process ancestry** (CLI-01) must work with
Windows process APIs. The agent launch, Shell, Project terminal and Run run the user's default shell where relevant.

**Done when**
- [x] PTY sessions start, resize, relay input and keep scrollback on Windows
- [x] worktree and log paths resolve under the user profile; keys like `SHOP-40.1` are valid folder names
- [x] a `crewboard` call from inside an agent session is identified as that agent; from another terminal as the user
- [x] tests: PTY and identity tests in the Windows VM with ordinary commands (`cmd /c echo`)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02, CLI-01 and decisions 0021, 0022

### T-0157 · Linux: socket, PTY and paths
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02
- **Decisions:** 0021
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0009, T-0050
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Linux uses the Unix socket (user-only permissions and peer-UID check, as on macOS), `portable-pty`, and `~/.crewboard/`. Fix any
macOS-only assumptions (process ancestry via `/proc`, `open` vs `xdg-open` for Open URL and "Reveal", font loading).

**Done when**
- [x] service, client, sessions, caller identity and Run pass the macOS test suite on Linux
- [x] Open URL and "Reveal database" use `xdg-open`
- [x] tests: the existing suites run in the Linux VM
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02 and decision 0021

### T-0158 · Win/Linux keyboard: Ctrl+Shift shortcuts while the terminal has focus
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02, SES-10
- **Decisions:** 0058
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Keyboard map
- **Depends on:** T-0058
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
On Windows and Linux, Ctrl+letter belongs to the CLI, so while a terminal has focus app shortcuts move to **Ctrl+Shift** (the
"In terminal" column of d0058), e.g. next inbox item Ctrl+Shift+J, find in scrollback Ctrl+Shift+F, leave terminal
Ctrl+Shift+`. Outside the terminal the Windows/Linux column applies. No shortcut may appear twice across the three columns.

**Done when**
- [x] the keymap is data-driven from the d0058 table for all three columns
- [x] a test asserts no duplicate binding across the three columns and none hits an OS system shortcut listed in the code
- [x] with the terminal focused on Windows/Linux, Ctrl+J/Ctrl+C/Ctrl+T reach the PTY; Ctrl+Shift variants trigger the app actions
- [ ] tests: keymap unit tests; checked by hand on Windows and Linux against *Keyboard map*
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02, SES-10 and decision 0058

### T-0159 · Win/Linux window chrome, notifications and install commands
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02, UI-01, ATT-11, SET-02
- **Decisions:** 0021
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 02 · App shell & project switcher → Title bar · Windows & Linux; 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → OS notifications; 14 · First run & onboarding → gh and az missing · non-blocking (Windows)
- **Depends on:** T-0117, T-0155, T-0157
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Finish the platform details in `crewboard-app` and `crewboard-core`: caption buttons at the top right on Windows/Linux (same
look otherwise, UI-01); OS notifications for blocking items on both platforms with click → ticket with terminal focused (ATT-11);
and per-OS install commands in the tools checklist (`winget install GitHub.cli`, `winget install Microsoft.AzureCLI`, Linux
package hints) (SET-02). Crewboard still installs nothing.

**Done when**
- [ ] title bar matches *Title bar · Windows & Linux*; window controls work
- [ ] notifications fire only for blocking items and not for the focused ticket; clicking opens the ticket with its terminal focused
- [x] tools checklist shows the right copyable command per OS
- [ ] tests: install-command table unit tests; checked by hand on Windows and Linux against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02, UI-01, ATT-11, SET-02 and decision 0021

### T-0160 · Checks on Windows and Linux (local VMs)
- **Epic:** M11 · Windows & Linux
- **Requirements:** PLT-02
- **Decisions:** 0056, 0021
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0005, T-0155, T-0156, T-0157
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
There are no GitHub Actions while the repository has no remote (d0056), so Windows and Linux are checked in **local virtual
machines** that the user sets up as the last step of v0.1. Make the check script work there: `scripts/check.sh` runs unchanged
on Linux, and a `scripts/check.ps1` runs the same three steps (`cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace
--all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`) on Windows. Write down how to set up each VM (Rust, git, the GPUI system
dependencies on Linux, the Visual Studio build tools on Windows) so the run can be repeated.

**Done when**
