- [x] `scripts/README.md` (or a section in the repo README) lists the setup steps for the Windows and the Linux VM
- [x] platform-specific tests are gated with `cfg` so each runs on its platform
- [x] tests: the full suite is green in the Windows VM, the Linux VM and on macOS (results noted in the commit message)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02 and decisions 0056, 0021

**Notes:** GitHub Actions (a macOS / Windows / Linux matrix) are added later, once the repository has a remote (d0056).

## M13 · Themes
Goal: the mode plus a dark and a light theme from the 15 bundled themes, picked in onboarding and Settings, recolor every
window live, terminals included, with the Theme credits the licenses ask for (d0069). Built before M12 (d0053).

### T-0168 · Core: a dark theme and a light theme next to the mode
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11, SET-01
- **Decisions:** 0069, 0023
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0028, T-0029
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The mode stays the existing `appearance` setting (Light / Dark / System, default System). `GlobalSettings` in
`crewboard-core/src/board/settings.rs` gains **`dark_theme`** and **`light_theme`**, two `stored_enum!` types whose stored
strings are the design's palette keys: dark `crewboard`, `catppuccin-frappe`, `catppuccin-macchiato`, `catppuccin-mocha`,
`tokyo-night`, `dracula`, `nord`, `gruvbox-dark`, `rose-pine`, `one-dark`; light `crewboard`, `catppuccin-latte`,
`daylight`, `solarized-light`, `rose-pine-dawn`. Both default to `crewboard` (d0069). A stored name the app doesn't know (a
theme removed in a later version, or a dark name in `light_theme`) reads as Crewboard and keeps every other setting. The
values travel in the existing `settings.get` / `settings.set`; they live in the app's database, never in a repo (d0023).

**Done when**
- [x] `GlobalSettings` has `dark_theme` and `light_theme`, both `crewboard` by default; `appearance` is unchanged (System by default)
- [x] the stored strings are exactly the design's palette keys: 10 dark, 5 light, `crewboard` in both
- [x] an unknown stored name (`"monokai"`) or a wrong-kind one (`"dracula"` as the light theme) reads as Crewboard, and the other settings keep their values
- [x] a database written before M13 (no rows for the new keys) reads as Crewboard Dark and Crewboard Light with the stored mode
- [x] tests: core unit tests (defaults, every key round-trips, unknown and wrong-kind fallback); `settings.set` → `settings.get` over the protocol with `CREWBOARD_HOME` in a temporary directory
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11, SET-01 and decisions 0069, 0023

**Notes:** display names, taglines and colors live in the app (T-0169, T-0172); the core only knows the keys.

### T-0169 · Theme palettes in the app: 15 themes, resolved from the mode and the OS
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11
- **Decisions:** 0069, 0057
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Theme palettes → Dark themes, Light themes
- **Depends on:** T-0015, T-0168
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard-app/src/theme.rs` grows from two hard-coded sets (Crewboard Light and Dark) to the **15 bundled themes**: each has
its name, whether it is dark or light, its palette key (T-0168), the **39 color tokens** (`Colors::NAMES`, same names and
order) and **16 terminal colors** (`ansi-0` … `ansi-15`), copied exactly from the variables of `crewboard.pen` (theme axis
`palette`; Crewboard also uses `mode`). The app mixes nothing at run time (d0069). The theme choice becomes **mode + dark
theme + light theme**, resolved against the OS appearance: Dark mode uses the dark theme, Light mode the light theme,
System follows the OS. `ActiveTheme` keeps live switching: the stored choice is applied at start and whenever the
settings change, and an OS switch in System mode redraws every window. The debug gallery (`CREWBOARD_GALLERY=1`,
`gallery.rs`) gets a palette switch (every theme by name) next to its System / Light / Dark buttons, so each theme can be
compared with *01 · Theme palettes*. Status icons and labels don't change: color is still never the only signal. The Diff
tab's syntax colors already come from status tokens (`diff_view.rs`), so the Diff tab follows the theme with no change.

**Done when**
- [x] 15 themes, each with 39 tokens and 16 terminal colors; 10 dark, 5 light, as in d0069's table
- [x] resolution: System + Tokyo Night + Daylight → Tokyo Night while the OS is dark, Daylight while it is light; Dark mode → only the dark theme; Light mode → only the light theme
- [ ] the stored mode and themes are applied at start and when they change; an OS appearance switch in System mode recolors every window live
- [x] `token_values_match_the_design` pins **every token of every theme**, `ansi-*` included, to the design's values in the design's order; a value changed only in code fails it
- [ ] the gallery's palette switch shows any of the 15 themes
- [ ] the Diff tab follows the theme with no code change (syntax colors from status tokens), checked by hand in Dracula and Solarized Light
- [ ] tests: theme unit tests (token table, resolution for every mode × OS appearance); by hand in the gallery against *01 · Theme palettes* (at least Crewboard Light and Dark, one Catppuccin, Dracula, Solarized Light)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11 and decisions 0069, 0057

**Notes:** the gallery's palette switch moved here from the picker ticket so the palettes can be checked before any picker
exists. The token table may be generated once from the design's variables; it is then pinned by the test, as for T-0015.

### T-0170 · Terminal colors from the active theme
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11, SES-09
- **Decisions:** 0069
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Theme palettes → each theme's card → Terminal & diff
- **Depends on:** T-0056, T-0169
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`crewboard-app/src/terminal/colors.rs` stops owning the `LIGHT` and `DARK` tables: the 16 ANSI colors come from the active
theme, and `terminal/element.rs` builds `Base` (foreground, background and the 16 colors) from it. Every terminal uses
the same element, so the ticket Terminal tab, Run, Shell and the Project terminal all recolor live when the theme changes,
without restarting their sessions. The 256-color cube and grey ramp stay the xterm values; bold still brightens 0–7 to
8–15; dim colors are still mixed toward the background. A program that sets its own colors (OSC 4/10/11, truecolor) still
wins (d0069). Crewboard's own terminal colors change slightly to the design's values (for example dark black `#1C1E23`
instead of `#3A3E47`, d0069 Consequences).

**Done when**
- [x] the 16 colors come from the active theme; `colors.rs` has no palette of its own
- [ ] switching the theme recolors open terminals at once; sessions keep running
- [x] program-set colors and truecolor still win; bold and dim behave as before
- [ ] tests: the existing color tests updated to read from a theme (Crewboard Light and Dark at the design's values, and one other theme, e.g. Dracula red `#ff5555`); by hand: `ls --color` and a 16-color chart in Tokyo Night and Solarized Light
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11, SES-09 and decision 0069

### T-0171 · Colors that assumed Crewboard
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11, UI-01
- **Decisions:** 0069
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 02 · App shell & project switcher → Header states, Another project needs you, Quick switcher · ⌘P
- **Depends on:** T-0169
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
A few places in `crewboard-app` draw white text because both Crewboard themes have white "text on accent". Themes with light
accents set that token to a dark color (Catppuccin, Dracula), so white text on them is hard to read (d0069 Consequences).
The four `gpui::white()` texts in `shell.rs` (the inbox button's needs-you count, the rail's and the switcher's needs-you
counters, the selected project's icon) use `text_on_accent`. Look for any other place that assumes dark means Crewboard
Dark (a literal color, a check of `Appearance::Dark`) and fix it the same way, listing each in the commit body. The Windows
close button keeps its OS red and white glyph (`window_controls.rs`): that is the OS convention, not a theme color.

**Done when**
- [x] no `gpui::white()` (or other literal color) is left for text on a themed fill outside the OS-convention close button
- [ ] the needs-you counters and the selected project icon read well in Crewboard, Catppuccin Latte and Dracula
- [ ] tests: by hand against the named frames in Crewboard Dark and in Dracula (counters on orange, selected project icon on the accent)
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11, UI-01 and decision 0069

### T-0172 · Theme picker component
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11
- **Decisions:** 0069, 0023
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 14 · First run & onboarding → 2 · Theme → Mode, Dark theme, Light theme; crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Global · Usage, notifications, data, appearance → Dark theme, Light theme
- **Depends on:** T-0168, T-0169
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
One component in `crewboard-app`, shared by onboarding (T-0173) and Settings (T-0174), with its logic in a view model: the
**mode switch** (Light / Dark / System) and two sections, **Dark theme** and **Light theme**, each with its label and
description as in the frames and its row of **theme cards**. A card (`Theme · <Name>`) holds a **preview** (a small window
drawn with that theme's own colors: title bar dots, sidebar, columns with cards and status dots), a ring (accent when
selected, border otherwise), the name with a check when selected, and the **tagline** ("Neon city after dark"), all copied
from the frames. In System mode the section in use carries the tag **"In use · OS is light"** / **"In use · OS is dark"**.
**Every click applies at once to every window and is saved** (`settings.set`); there is no Save button (d0069). The save
goes through one shared place (for example the `Workspace`) so the stored settings the app re-applies never lag behind a
click.

**Done when**
- [ ] the mode switch and both sections render as in the frames: 10 dark cards in two rows of five, 5 light cards in one row, previews drawn with each theme's own tokens
- [ ] a click on a card or a mode recolors every window at once and stores the choice; reopening the app shows the same choice
- [ ] the "In use" tag shows only in System mode, on the section matching the OS appearance, and moves when the OS switches
- [x] the selected card in each section shows the accent ring and the check; picking a dark theme never changes the light one
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (selection per section, In use for every mode × OS, the setting a click stores); by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11 and decisions 0069, 0023

### T-0173 · Onboarding: the Theme step
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11, UI-08
- **Decisions:** 0069
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 14 · First run & onboarding → 1 · Welcome, 2 · Theme, Dark · welcome
- **Depends on:** T-0018, T-0034, T-0172
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
Onboarding (`view_models/onboarding.rs`: `Step`, `STEPS`; `onboarding.rs`) becomes **Welcome → Theme → Tools → Project →
Done**. Welcome's button reads **"Choose a theme"** and opens the Theme step: "Pick a look", its description, the mode
switch at the top right and the picker (T-0172), then "Change it anytime in Settings → Appearance." and **"Check my
tools"**, which goes on to Tools and runs the check as before. The window recolors as soon as a card or mode is clicked.
Onboarding still appears only when there are no projects; "Check tools" from the empty board still opens straight at Tools.
People who already finished onboarding never see the step and keep their mode with the Crewboard themes (T-0168's
defaults) until they change them in Settings (d0069).

**Done when**
- [x] the step indicator shows Welcome · Theme · Tools · Project · Done with Theme as step 2; Tools, Project and Done move to 3, 4, 5
- [x] Welcome → "Choose a theme" → Theme → "Check my tools" → Tools (with the check running), as in the frames
- [ ] a click on the Theme step is applied and saved at once (T-0172); continuing keeps it
- [x] users with projects never see the step; "Check tools" from the empty board skips it
- [ ] tests: view-model tests for the step order and the indicator; by hand against the named frames, light and dark
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11, UI-08 and decision 0069

### T-0174 · Settings: Appearance and Theme credits
- **Epic:** M13 · Themes
- **Requirements:** UI-11, SET-01
- **Decisions:** 0069, 0054
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Global · Usage, notifications, data, appearance → Appearance; crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Dark · project settings → Settings · Dark; crewboard.pen → 15 · Settings → Theme credits · dialog
- **Depends on:** T-0045, T-0172
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Settings → Appearance (`settings.rs`, `view_models/settings.rs`) replaces the single "Theme" switch with the frames'
**Mode** row ("Follows the OS by default. System switches between your dark and light theme below."), the picker's Dark
theme and Light theme sections (T-0172, with the "In use" tag in System mode), the **Theme credits** row ("Bundled themes
are based on open-source palettes, used under the MIT License. Copyright stays with their authors.") with **View
credits**, then Terminal font size. View credits opens the **Theme credits dialog**: the description (Crewboard adapted some
colors and isn't affiliated with or endorsed by these projects), one entry per source project (Catppuccin, Tokyo Night,
Dracula, Nord, Gruvbox, Rosé Pine, Atom One Dark, Primer Primitives, Solarized) with the themes taken from it, its
copyright line, its license and a **Source** link, then the full **MIT License** text, and a footer with Esc, **Copy
notices** and **Done**. Each copyright line, license and theme list is copied exactly from the frame; this is the notice
those licenses ask Crewboard to ship (d0069, d0054).

**Done when**
- [ ] the Appearance section matches the frames in light and dark: Mode, Dark theme, Light theme, Theme credits, Terminal font size
- [ ] every click applies and saves at once; the "In use" tag follows the OS in System mode
- [x] the dialog lists the nine projects with the frame's theme lists, copyright lines and licenses (Gruvbox `MIT/X11`), and the full MIT text
- [ ] Source opens the project's repository in the browser; Copy notices puts every entry and the MIT text on the clipboard; Esc and Done close the dialog
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (the credit entries against the frame's text, the copied notice); by hand against the named frames
- [x] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-11, SET-01 and decisions 0069, 0054

**Notes:** the Source links are not in the design. Use the projects' own repositories and name them as an implementation
choice: Catppuccin `https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin`, Tokyo Night `https://github.com/enkia/tokyo-night-vscode-theme`,
Dracula `https://github.com/dracula/dracula-theme`, Nord `https://github.com/nordtheme/nord`, Gruvbox
`https://github.com/morhetz/gruvbox`, Rosé Pine `https://github.com/rose-pine/rose-pine-theme`, Atom One Dark
`https://github.com/atom/atom/tree/master/packages/one-dark-syntax`, Primer Primitives `https://github.com/primer/primitives`,
Solarized `https://github.com/altercation/solarized`.

## M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
Goal: an agent's submit is checked by a separate read-only validator before the user sees it; validated work can merge by
itself; tickets get a priority and their own Ready for Dev column; "Done when" goes away; Project agents investigate and
create tickets next to several Project terminals (d0063–d0068). Built after M11 and M13 on macOS, then re-checked in the
VMs (d0053). Design: update K, with the fixes of L and M, applied in `crewboard.pen`.

### T-0175 · Remove "Done when": migration, core, CLI and prime
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** TKT-04, TKT-03
- **Decisions:** 0067, 0019, 0032, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0022, T-0026, T-0069, T-0071, T-0074
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The checklist goes away completely; the description is the only place a ticket says what "done" means, and Crewboard never
judges it (d0067). A new migration (`crates/crewboard-core/migrations/0022_…`) appends every ticket's `ticket_done_when`
items to `tickets.body` as a Markdown list under a `## Done when` heading (in `position` order, after a blank line; a ticket
without items is untouched) and drops the table. In `crewboard-core`: `DoneWhenItem`, `Ticket.done_when`,
`NewTicketInput.done_when`, `TicketUpdate.done_when`, `DoneWhenEdit`, `Field::DoneWhen`, `write_done_when` /
`apply_done_when_edit` (`board/tickets.rs`), `Refusal::EmptyDoneWhen` and its "no such item" error (`board/mod.rs`),
`MoveWarning::EmptyDoneWhen` and the warning branch of `check_move` (`board/moves.rs`: Backlog → Dev no longer warns),
`change_note.rs::done_when_parts` ("Added to 'Done when'") and the other readers (`delete.rs`, `readiness.rs`) lose it.
`prime.rs::render` drops the "## Done when" section and rule 3 now reads "When the ticket is done, as its description
describes, submit: …". The CLI drops `create --done-when` and `update --add-done-when` (`cli.rs`, `write.rs`; the "nothing
to change" error lists `--title, -d or --stdin`) and `show` (`read.rs`) prints no Done when block. The `create -f` batch
format (`board/batch.rs`) keeps reading a `**Done when**` block, but appends it to the body under the same `## Done when`
heading, so existing files (including this tickets file) still import. `plugin/skills/work-ticket/SKILL.md` stops
mentioning `--add-done-when` (the full skill rewrite is T-0191). The app keeps compiling: its Done-when code is removed in
T-0176, so this ticket only cuts what the app reads from the core (an empty list is fine for one commit if needed, but no
`done_when` field may remain in the protocol).

**Done when**
- [ ] the migration moves each ticket's items into its description under `## Done when`, in order, and drops `ticket_done_when`; tickets without items keep their body byte for byte
- [ ] no `done_when` / `DoneWhen` remains in `crewboard-core`, `crewboard-cli` or the protocol; Backlog → Dev never warns
- [ ] `crewboard create --done-when` and `update --add-done-when` are unknown flags; `prime` and `show` print no Done when section
- [ ] `create -f` turns a `**Done when**` block into the `## Done when` part of the body; `the_v0_1_tickets_file_imports` checks a non-empty body instead of a Done-when list
- [ ] tests: migration test on a database written by migration 0021 (items, no items, an epic); core tests updated (moves, change notes, prime, batch); CLI tests for the removed flags
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-04, TKT-03 and decisions 0067, 0019, 0032, 0048

**Notes:** the tests that only built Done-when fixtures (`tests/{agent_edits,limits,needs_you,order,…}.rs`) change their
fixtures, not their assertions.

### T-0176 · Remove "Done when" from the app
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** TKT-04, UI-03, UI-05
- **Decisions:** 0067
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 08 · Ticket editor → Quick-create in Backlog, Full form · filled, Validation · empty form, Ticket tab · read mode, with agent edits, Compare · before / after, Dark · full form; crewboard.pen → 03 · Board & columns → Drag · over an allowed column
- **Depends on:** T-0175, T-0036, T-0040, T-0042
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Every Done-when surface in `crewboard-app` goes: the editor's checklist (`editor.rs`: `new_criterion` "Add a criterion",
`criteria_list`, `DraggedCriterion`, `rebuild_criteria`, the read-mode "Done when" section and its "No “Done when” yet"
state), the editor model (`view_models/editor.rs`: `Criterion` and the `*_criterion` functions, `done_when_edits`),
`components/field.rs::CheckItem` if nothing else uses it, the card's "no “Done when”" / "N criteria" meta
(`view_models/card.rs::meta_row`), the empty-Done-when drop warning (`view_models/drag.rs` warning variant,
`empty_done_when_title`, `board.rs::warning_dialog` "empty-done-when", `EditCriteria`) and the ⇧⌘D path in `panel.rs`, and
the other readers (`agent_edits.rs::done_when_note`, `created_summary.rs`, `recovery.rs`, `agent_activity.rs`,
`card_fixtures.rs`, `service_link.rs`, `root.rs`). "Compare · before / after" compares the description only. A moved
"Done when" now shows as part of the description (the frames show it as a heading inside the description text).

**Done when**
- [ ] the form, the Ticket tab and quick-create match the named frames, light and dark, with no checklist or add row
- [ ] dragging a ticket with a short or empty description to Ready for Dev or Dev never shows a warning
- [ ] cards show no criteria count or missing-Done-when hint; the agent-edit compare covers title and description only
- [ ] no `criteria` / `done_when` code remains in `crewboard-app`
- [ ] tests: view-model tests updated (editor, card meta, drag, agent edits); by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-04, UI-03, UI-05 and decision 0067

### T-0177 · Priority: field, inheritance, CLI and prime
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** TKT-09, TKT-02, TKT-03
- **Decisions:** 0065, 0032, 0039
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0175, T-0025, T-0068, T-0069
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Tickets and epics get a priority **1 Urgent · 2 High · 3 Normal · 4 Low**, default **3** (d0065). Migration: a `priority`
column on `tickets`, nullable for epic children. An epic child **inherits the epic's priority** unless it has its own (the
effective priority is computed in the core; changing the epic's priority changes the children that have none). A
`Priority` domain type with labels in `domain/` replaces the "no priorities" note in `domain/status.rs` and its test
`two_kinds_and_no_priority`. The value travels in `NewTicketInput` / `TicketUpdate` and the ticket snapshot, is an editable
field with History entries and agent-edit records like the title, and pinning never changes it. CLI: `create -p N`,
`update -p N` (1–4, anything else refused with the allowed values), `list --priority N`, `show` prints it; the `create -f`
format reads an optional `- **Priority:** 2` line. `crewboard prime` prints the ticket's priority. A priority change
doesn't concern the agent's work, so it sends no SES-08 note (implementation choice, named in the commit). The start order
that uses it is T-0178.

**Done when**
- [ ] new tickets and epics are priority 3; children without their own priority follow the epic's, also after the epic changes
- [ ] `-p 1`…`-p 4` on create and update, `list --priority`, `show` and `prime` work; `-p 0` / `-p 5` are refused with "Priority is 1 (Urgent) to 4 (Low)"
- [ ] `create -f` reads `- **Priority:**`; a file without it imports at priority 3
- [ ] tests: core unit tests (default, inheritance, override, epic change); protocol round trip; CLI tests; migration on a 0022 database
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-09, TKT-02, TKT-03 and decisions 0065, 0032, 0039

### T-0178 · Ready for Dev column and the start order (core)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** SCH-01, SCH-02, TKT-06, TKT-09
- **Decisions:** 0066, 0065, 0016, 0047, 0049
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0177, T-0026, T-0027, T-0077, T-0079
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
Queued becomes its own column with no new rules (d0066). `Column` (`domain/status.rs`) gains **ReadyForDev** between Backlog
and Dev; `Queued`, `WaitingForSlot`, `Held`, `ParentClosed` and `StartInterrupted` map to it (a migration rewrites the
stored column of queued tickets). `DropTarget` (`board/moves.rs`) replaces `DevQueued` with `ReadyForDev`; a drop on Dev
from Backlog still lands in Ready for Dev (it starts when it can); **Ready for Dev → Backlog** is allowed and takes the
ticket out of the queue (clears `queued_at`, nothing had started). Backlog never starts by itself (SCH-02). A `ready_since`
time is kept: when a queued ticket becomes ready (its last parent merged, or the moment it was queued if it was already
ready). **Start order** (SCH-01, d0065): pinned (most recently pinned first) → effective priority 1→4 → ready the longest;
`scheduler::plan` uses it instead of `queue_time()`, and `board/order.rs::sort_column` sorts Ready for Dev the same way after
its needs-you cards (Start interrupted, Parent closed). Dev's order is pinned → orange → Interrupted / Resume failed →
working; `fold_dev` folds only working cards ("4 more · working"), never needs-you cards, and has no Queued part any more
(`ColumnSnapshot.queued` goes). A Backlog ticket whose dependencies are all ready gets a derived **Ready to start** fact
(not for epics); it never starts by itself.

**Done when**
- [ ] six columns in the snapshot: Backlog, Ready for Dev, Dev, Ready for Review, Approved, Done; every queued status sits in Ready for Dev
- [ ] Backlog → Ready for Dev and Backlog → Dev both queue; Ready for Dev → Backlog unqueues; Agent Working and Ready for Review still refuse drops
- [ ] start order: a pinned P3 starts before an unpinned P1; among equal pins and priorities the one ready the longest starts first
- [ ] Ready for Dev lists Start interrupted and Parent closed first, then the start order; Dev's fold covers only working cards
- [ ] Backlog tickets with all parents merged carry Ready to start; they don't start
- [ ] tests: `check_move` table, `sort_column` and `fold_dev` cases, `scheduler::plan` order with pins, priorities and `ready_since`; migration of queued rows
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names SCH-01, SCH-02, TKT-06, TKT-09 and decisions 0066, 0065, 0016, 0047, 0049

### T-0179 · Board: six columns, Ready for Dev hint, Ready to start, drag
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** UI-02, UI-03
- **Decisions:** 0066, 0065, 0037, 0041
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 03 · Board — shop-web · 1440 · Light, 03 · Board — shop-web · 1440 · Dark; crewboard.pen → 03 · Board & columns → Drag · over an allowed column, Drag · over a forbidden column, Annotations, Pinned tickets, Chain highlight · hover or select SHOP-20, Width strategy · recommended vs alternative (A · 1440: six columns, all open; B · 1280 or panel open: Done becomes a 34 px rail); crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light → Backlog · P4 · Ready to start, Queued · waiting on deps, Queued · waits for merge, Queued · ready to start, Queued · start interrupted
- **Depends on:** T-0178, T-0176, T-0035, T-0036, T-0083
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`board.rs` and `view_models/board.rs` draw six columns. **Ready for Dev** has the hint "Starts when ready, in priority
order" and holds the cards the Queued section of Dev used to hold (waits-for text, waiting for a slot, held, Start
interrupted, Parent closed; T-0083's texts unchanged). Dev loses its Queued section and `QUEUED_HINT`; its "Section Agent
Working" and the fold "N more · working" follow the frame. Backlog cards with Ready to start show the hint from the frame.
Drag feedback (`view_models/drag.rs`) follows §7.4: Backlog → Ready for Dev or Dev (lands in Ready for Dev), Ready for Dev
→ Backlog, the forbidden columns as before. The width rules (`dev_budget`, `hidden_columns`, `done_collapsed`,
`DONE_OPEN_FROM_WIDTH`) are re-tuned so 1440 shows six open columns and 1280 (or an open panel) folds Done to its rail, as in
*Width strategy*. Chain highlight, search/filter (the status filter lists the M12 states) and empty states keep working
across the new column.

**Done when**
- [ ] the board matches *03 · Board — shop-web · 1440* light and dark: six columns, counts, the Ready for Dev hint, Dev without a Queued section
- [ ] the allowed/forbidden drag frames match, including Ready for Dev → Backlog
- [ ] 1440 shows six open columns; 1280 and an open panel fold Done to the 34 px rail, as in *Width strategy*
- [ ] needs-you cards in Dev are never folded; the fold names only working cards
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (columns, hints, fold, drag targets, widths); by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-02, UI-03 and decisions 0066, 0065, 0037, 0041

### T-0180 · Priority in the app: chip, editor, card menu, panel header
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** TKT-09, UI-03, UI-04, UI-05
- **Decisions:** 0065, 0047
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Primitives → Priority; crewboard.pen → 01 · Foundations → Status Legend → 5 · Priority; crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / Dark → Row · priority & Ready for Dev (0065, 0066) (Backlog · P1 Urgent · other base, Ready for Dev · P2 · waiting for a free slot, Ready for Dev · pinned beats priority, Note) and Priority rule; crewboard.pen → 08 · Ticket editor → Quick-create in Backlog, Full form · filled, Ticket tab · read mode, with agent edits (Priority, Priority · editable)
- **Depends on:** T-0177, T-0179, T-0031, T-0039, T-0040, T-0059
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
A **Priority** component (the frame's chip, icon + label, color never the only signal) shows on cards by the frame's rule:
P1, P2 and P4 are shown, Normal (P3) is hidden on cards. The editor gets the Priority field (optional in quick-create,
default Normal; in the full form; editable in the Ticket tab), the card's right-click menu gets a Priority submenu next to
Pin / Unpin, and the panel header shows the priority, editable. Changes go through `ticket.update` like other fields.
Epic children show the inherited priority and can set their own. The gallery shows the component next to the Status
Legend.

**Done when**
- [ ] cards show P1, P2 and P4 as in the frames and nothing for P3; "pinned beats priority" renders as in its frame
- [ ] the priority can be set from quick-create, the full form, the Ticket tab, the card menu and the panel header; each change shows on the board at once
- [ ] an epic child shows the epic's priority until it gets its own
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (chip visibility per priority, menu items, editor input); by hand against the named frames, light and dark
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names TKT-09, UI-03, UI-04, UI-05 and decisions 0065, 0047

### T-0181 · Settings: Validate skill, validation, rounds, Auto-merge, Project agent instructions (core)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-13, AMG-01, PAG-03, SET-01
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0064, 0068, 0040, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0028, T-0139
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`board/settings.rs`: `GlobalSettings` gains `validation` (on by default), `validation_rounds` (1–5, default 3),
`auto_merge` (a `stored_enum!` **off** / **epic_children** / **all**, default off) and `project_agent_instructions` (text,
empty by default). `WorkflowSkills` gains **Validate** (default `/crewboard:validate`), resolved like Commit / Push / Open PR
(T-0139). `ProjectSettings` gets an override for each (empty = use global). `EffectiveSettings` resolves them, and reports
that Auto-merge **does nothing while validation is off** for the project (a fact the settings screen shows). A rounds
value outside 1–5 is a `SettingProblem`. Stored in the app's database only (d0023).

**Done when**
- [ ] defaults: validation on, rounds 3, Auto-merge off, Validate skill `/crewboard:validate`, no Project agent instructions
- [ ] project overrides win; empty overrides use the global values; the effective settings say when Auto-merge is inactive because validation is off
- [ ] rounds 0 or 6 are flagged and never used
- [ ] tests: core unit tests (defaults, overrides, resolution, the inactive fact, invalid rounds); `settings.set` → `settings.get` over the protocol
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-13, AMG-01, PAG-03, SET-01 and decisions 0063, 0064, 0068, 0040, 0050

### T-0182 · Validation and Auto-merge states, the validations table, transitions (core)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-01, VAL-03, VAL-06, VAL-07, AMG-02, AMG-03, AMG-04, SCH-03, ATT-06
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0064, 0042, 0016
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0178, T-0019, T-0078, T-0080
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The M12 states of SDD §7.2 enter the domain. `Status` gains **Validating** (neutral, Dev), **Needs you · validator**,
**Validation failed M times**, **Validation ended without a result** (also used, with its own reason, for "Validation
discarded: the validator changed files", VAL-02), **Merge conflict · fixing** (working) and an **Auto-merge stopped** status
with a reason (keeps conflicting with `<target>` / base diverged / checkout dirty). Done gains **Merged automatically**
(a merged outcome with `merged_automatically`). Flags (`domain/flags.rs`) gain the **"Validation failed · round N of M"**
marker on a working card and the **Review entry** (Validated · round N / Validation skipped by you / Submitted by you /
Failed M times · moved by you). Migration: the `validations` table of SDD §8 (id, ticket_id, round, trigger, session_id,
started_at, ended_at, outcome, report, notes_json, head_sha) and the ticket columns `review_entry`, `validation_round`,
`auto_merge_conflicts`, `merged_automatically`. The orange set (`Status::ORANGE`, `is_orange`) gains the four orange
states; `needs_you.rs::group_of` puts them in group 1; Validating holds one slot (`HOLDS_A_SLOT`, one per ticket, not per
session) and never creates an inbox item. The transition rules of §7.3's M12 rows become pure functions, tested row by
row, used by T-0183–T-0189. The exact enum shape is an implementation choice; the labels are fixed by §7.2.

**Done when**
- [ ] every M12 label of §7.2 has a status or flag with that exact text; orange ones sort and group like the orange family
- [ ] Validating counts once toward `max_concurrency` and creates no inbox item; Merged automatically is a Done state
- [ ] the `validations` table and the ticket columns exist; a database from before M12 migrates with no Review entry set
- [ ] tests: status/flag unit tests (labels, orange, groups, slots); a transition table test for every M12 row of §7.3; migration test
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-01, VAL-03, VAL-06, VAL-07, AMG-02, AMG-03, AMG-04, SCH-03, ATT-06 and decisions 0063, 0064, 0042, 0016

### T-0183 · Validator session; `review submit` starts validation; `attach --validator`
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-01, VAL-13, SES-03, SES-05, SCH-03, SCH-04, REV-07
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0050, 0018, 0020, 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0181, T-0182, T-0052, T-0055, T-0072, T-0129
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
`SessionKind` gains **Validator** (`runs_claude`). `session/launch.rs` gets a validator launch: cwd = the ticket's
worktree, `CREWBOARD_TICKET=<KEY>`, a **role** variable named from the naming constant (ARC-01, e.g. `<PREFIX>_ROLE=validator`)
so hooks and the CLI can tell it apart, no `PORT`, the same `--plugin-dir`, `--allowedTools "Bash(crewboard:*)"`,
status-line `--settings` and `--session-id`, no permission flags, and the project's effective **Validate** skill as the
prompt (default `/crewboard:validate`). `review submit` (`board/review.rs::submit_for_review`, `service/board_requests.rs`)
with validation **on**: same uncommitted-files refusal; records the summary and head; opens round `validation_round + 1` in
`validations` (trigger agent); sets **Validating…**; replies "Submitted. Validation is running; the result will come back
to you here. End your turn." (instead of "SHOP-18 is Ready for Review."); then starts the validator (the agent's submit is
the go-ahead, d0063). Validation **off** keeps the pre-M12 path. While the plan limit is active the validation doesn't start:
the ticket shows "Waiting for limit reset" and the validator starts on Resume all (SCH-04). Only one validator per ticket;
its session ID is stored like every Claude session (d0045). `crewboard attach <KEY> --validator` (`cli.rs` `Attach`,
`attach.rs`) opens the running validator, else "SHOP-29 has no running validator". Verdicts, guard and hooks are T-0184–T-0186.

**Done when**
- [ ] with validation on, `review submit` replies with the validation text, the ticket shows Validating…, and a validator session runs in the worktree with the role, the Validate skill and no `PORT`
- [ ] with validation off for the project, `review submit` moves the ticket to Ready for Review as before
- [ ] uncommitted files still refuse the submit, before any validator starts
- [ ] a project's Validate override is the validator's prompt
- [ ] under the plan limit the validator waits and starts with Resume all
- [ ] `attach --validator` reaches the validator; without one it prints "SHOP-29 has no running validator"
- [ ] tests: launch command test (flags, env, role, no PORT, no permission flags); submit tests on and off; the PTY plumbing with an ordinary command in place of `claude`; attach flag parsing
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-01, VAL-13, SES-03, SES-05, SCH-03, SCH-04, REV-07 and decisions 0063, 0050, 0018, 0020, 0022

**Notes:** the exact variable name and value come from the naming module (`naming::…`), like `CREWBOARD_TICKET`.

### T-0184 · `crewboard validate pass|fail`, `prime --validate`, rounds
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-03, VAL-04, VAL-05, VAL-06, VAL-10, CLI-05, SES-06
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0031, 0034, 0040, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0183, T-0054, T-0064, T-0065, T-0071, T-0086
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
Caller identity (`caller.rs`) gains a **validator** caller, found by process ancestry from validator session roots like
agents (CLI-01). `policy.rs`: a validator may read anything and run `validate` for its own ticket; every change is refused
("SHOP-29's validator can't change tickets", wording an implementation choice in the style of CLI-02). New CLI
`crewboard validate pass --stdin` / `validate fail --stdin` (validator callers only; anyone else: "Only a validator can
report a verdict") stores the report on the round (outcome, report, notes) and ends the validator once its turn ends.
**Pass** → Ready for Review with Review entry "Validated · round N" (Auto-merge hooks in here, T-0189). **Fail with rounds
left** → the findings are queued as an automatic delivery to the agent's session (SES-06 rules), e.g. "Validation failed
(round 1 of 3). Fix these, commit, and submit again: …", and the ticket is Agent Working with the round marker. **Last round
failed** (effective rounds) → "Validation failed M times", findings **not** delivered. **After Send back** the round
counter restarts at 0. `crewboard prime --validate` (`prime.rs`, `Request::TicketPrime` with a mode) prints: title and
description, the submit summary, the diff against `base_sha`, the review comments when sent back, earlier rounds'
findings, the Agent instructions (global + project), and the linked tickets (parents with summaries, children, epic
siblings and the epic's description).

**Done when**
- [ ] `validate pass|fail` works only from a validator session of that ticket; agents, Project agents and users get the refusal
- [ ] pass → Ready for Review "Validated · round N"; fail with rounds left → findings delivered with "round N of M", Agent Working; last fail → orange, nothing delivered
- [ ] the report of every round is stored; the validator session ends after its verdict turn
- [ ] Send back resets the counter; the next `prime --validate` includes the review comments
- [ ] `prime --validate` prints every part listed above, linked tickets included
- [ ] tests: caller and policy tests; verdict tests for pass, fail, last fail, rounds 1 and 5; delivery text; prime snapshot tests (plain, sent back, epic child with siblings)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-03, VAL-04, VAL-05, VAL-06, VAL-10, CLI-05, SES-06 and decisions 0063, 0031, 0034, 0040, 0048

### T-0185 · Read-only guard and changes during validation
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-02, VAL-08, SES-08
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0048
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0184, T-0074
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
At validator start Crewboard records the head commit and the state of **tracked** files (files ignored by `.gitignore`
don't count). **Validator changed files (VAL-02):** if, at the verdict or the end of its turn, the head or tracked files
differ and the agent's session took no turn meanwhile, the result is **discarded**, logged in History, and the ticket shows
"Validation discarded: the validator changed files" with the no-result actions (T-0187). **Changes through the agent
(VAL-08):** if the agent's session takes a turn and tracked files change or a new commit appears, or the user saves a
description change, the validator is stopped, the round doesn't count (outcome cancelled), the ticket returns to Agent
Working, and the agent gets "Validation was cancelled because the code changed. Submit again when you're done." (a
description edit also queues the SES-08 change note). Typing to the agent alone cancels nothing. How changes are noticed
(on hook events and verdicts, or a short poll) is an implementation choice; Crewboard never stashes or modifies the
worktree to check.

**Done when**
- [ ] a validator that edits a tracked file or commits gets its verdict discarded with the message; an ignored file (e.g. `coverage/`) changes nothing
- [ ] an agent commit or tracked-file change during validation cancels it with the message; the round isn't counted
- [ ] a user description edit cancels it and also sends the change note; a title-only edit sends only the SES-08 note
- [ ] tests: temp-repo tests (tracked change, ignored change, new commit; validator vs agent turn); change-note text
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-02, VAL-08, SES-08 and decisions 0063, 0048

### T-0186 · Hooks for validators, and the agent's Stop while validating
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-07, VAL-11, ATT-01, ATT-02, USG-03
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0042, 0046, 0009
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0183, T-0066, T-0067, T-0128
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard hook` (`crewboard-cli/src/hook.rs`) passes the role to the service; `signals/hooks.rs::map_hook` gets the §11.4
M12 rows. **Validator:** Stop with no `validate pass|fail` this turn → **Validation ended without a result** (orange);
Notification with a needs-you type → **Needs you · validator**; UserPromptSubmit (the user typed into it) → back to
Validating; StopFailure with the limit → "Waiting for limit reset" (the validation restarts on Resume all, T-0188).
**Agent while its ticket validates:** Stop never becomes Idle (the agent was told to end its turn); a needs-you
Notification still makes it orange. Crewboard never nudges the validator (d0018). Hooks stay no-ops in the user's own
`claude` sessions (PLG-04). Payload fields beyond the documented ones are checked by logging real payloads (d0020, d0042).

**Done when**
- [ ] validator Stop without a verdict → "Validation ended without a result"; with a verdict → nothing extra
- [ ] validator permission prompt → "Needs you · validator"; typing into it returns to Validating
- [ ] the agent's Stop during validation leaves the ticket Validating, never Idle
- [ ] tests: `crewboard hook <event>` fed real-shaped JSON for every row above (validator and agent); unset ticket and role → no-op
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-07, VAL-11, ATT-01, ATT-02, USG-03 and decisions 0063, 0042, 0046, 0009

### T-0187 · User actions: Validate and submit, Stop, Skip, Move to Review anyway, another round, Retry
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-06, VAL-07, VAL-09, CLS-01, CLS-05, CLS-06
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0052, 0033
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0185, T-0186, T-0100, T-0103, T-0104
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
New board actions over the protocol (user callers only): **Validate and submit** (agent not mid-turn; same checks as
the agent's submit; trigger user; rounds and Auto-merge apply); **Submit for review** (`submit_by_user`) now **skips
validation**: Review entry "Submitted by you", never auto-merged; while validating: **Stop validation** (validator
stopped, round not counted, Agent Working) and **Skip to Review** ("Validation skipped by you", never auto-merged), and the
two submit actions are refused. On "Validation failed M times": **Move to Review anyway** ("Failed M times · moved by
you", the last report kept for the review, never auto-merged), **Give it another round** (deliver the last findings, allow
one extra round), **Answer the agent** (the user's note delivered with the findings, counter restarts at 0; a user click,
so held-text rules SES-07 apply). On "ended without a result" / discarded: **Retry validation** (a fresh validator, not a
round) and **Move to Review anyway** (Review entry "Validation skipped by you", never auto-merged). **Park** and **Close**
stop the agent and the validator and discard the validation (`review/park.rs`, `review/close.rs`); dragging a parked
ticket back resumes only the agent.

**Done when**
- [ ] each action moves the ticket and sets the Review entry exactly as listed; none of the user's routes to Review auto-merges
- [ ] Stop validation and Skip to Review only while validating; the submit actions refused while validating
- [ ] Give it another round and Answer the agent deliver the findings (with the note) and set the counter as listed
- [ ] Park and Close stop both sessions; the validation is recorded as stopped
- [ ] tests: core tests per action and state (incl. refusals); delivery texts; park/close with a running validator (ordinary command in place of `claude`)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-06, VAL-07, VAL-09, CLS-01, CLS-05, CLS-06 and decisions 0063, 0052, 0033

**Notes:** the Review entry after **Move to Review anyway** from "ended without a result" is "Validation skipped by you"
(d0063, user decision 2026-10-11).

### T-0188 · Validation after a restart and under the usage limit
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** REC-01, VAL-07, USG-06, SCH-04
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0045, 0046, 0018
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0187, T-0111, T-0112, T-0129
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`recovery/reconcile.rs` gains the M12 row: a **Validating** ticket whose validator is gone after a reboot, crash or service
restart becomes **Interrupted** (`interrupted_from` Validating, inbox group 2, in Resume all); **Resume** starts a **fresh
validation**, not counted as a round; the old validator conversation is never resumed. The agent's own session is resumed
only when something must be delivered to it (findings or a cancel message). A validator stopped by the usage limit shows
"Waiting for limit reset" like agents; **Resume all** restarts the validation fresh. The round's outcome is recorded as
interrupted. Nothing resumes automatically.

**Done when**
- [ ] after a simulated restart a Validating ticket is Interrupted and in Resume all; Resume starts a new validator with the same round number
- [ ] Resume all after a limit reset restarts limit-stopped validations
- [ ] the agent's session is resumed only when a delivery needs it
- [ ] tests: reconcile table test with the new row; resume and Resume all tests (ordinary command in place of `claude`)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REC-01, VAL-07, USG-06, SCH-04 and decisions 0063, 0045, 0046, 0018

### T-0189 · Auto-merge (core)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** AMG-01, AMG-02, AMG-03, AMG-04, AMG-05, GIT-03, REC-05, SCH-05
- **Decisions:** 0064, 0038, 0018, 0049, 0059
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0184, T-0181, T-0081, T-0089, T-0090, T-0099
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
On **pass**, when the project's effective Auto-merge applies (Epic children only → epic children; All tickets → epic
children and standalone tickets; never epics; validation on; Review entry Validated), Crewboard runs the Local merge
(`review/mod.rs::Review::local_merge`, `git/merge.rs`) **without the confirmation**, with the squash message the
confirmation would propose (`board/merge.rs::merge_proposal`). **Success** → Done · "Merged automatically", a History line,
no inbox item, no notification; waiting tickets start as after any merge (`on_parent_merged`, base fetched for base
branches); Done cleanup (`review/finish.rs`: worktree removed unless dirty, branch kept, Run and Shell stop, the agent's
session ends). **Conflict** → nothing merged, the ticket returns to its agent **without a click** with the `restack` skill
(`Review::return_to_agent`, same session or resumed), "Merge conflict · fixing", `auto_merge_conflicts + 1`; the next
submit validates again with the counter at 0. **3 conflicts in a row** (reset by a successful merge) → orange "Keeps
conflicting with `<target>`". **Base diverged** or **dirty target checkout** → orange with GIT-01's / GIT-03's message. On
those stops: **Retry merge** (runs the merge again) and **Move to Review** (Ready for Review, still "Validated · round N",
not auto-merged again). A manual Local merge keeps its confirmation and "Send to agent to fix" (AMG-05).

**Done when**
- [ ] Off: a pass lands in Ready for Review; Epic children only: children merge into the epic branch, standalone tickets don't; All tickets: both; epics never
- [ ] Submitted by you, Validation skipped by you, Failed · moved by you and validation off never auto-merge
- [ ] success → Done · Merged automatically, cleanup done, dependents start; no inbox item or notification
- [ ] conflict → back to the agent with restack, no click; the third conflict in a row → "Keeps conflicting with `<target>`"; a merge in between resets the count
- [ ] base diverged and dirty checkout → orange with the exact messages; Retry merge and Move to Review work
- [ ] tests: temp-repo tests (clean merge into an epic branch and a base branch, conflict, diverged base, dirty checkout); counter tests; dependents start after an auto-merge
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names AMG-01, AMG-02, AMG-03, AMG-04, AMG-05, GIT-03, REC-05, SCH-05 and decisions 0064, 0038, 0018, 0049, 0059

### T-0190 · Validator tokens on the ticket and in today's total
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-14, USG-01
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0020
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0183, T-0125, T-0126
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`usage/mod.rs`: a ticket's total sums its agent **and** validator sessions; `TicketUsage` splits them (agent tokens,
validation tokens, number of rounds counted). Today's total includes validators. The card shows the sum; the panel split
"agent 61k · validation 23k (2 rounds)" is drawn in T-0193. An uncounted round (cancelled, interrupted) still counts its
tokens, shown as "(round not counted)" where the design shows it.

**Done when**
- [ ] card total = agent + validation tokens; today's total includes validators
- [ ] the split reports agent and validation tokens and the number of rounds
- [ ] tests: usage tests with agent and validator sessions of one ticket (incl. an uncounted round)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-14, USG-01 and decisions 0063, 0020

### T-0191 · Plugin: the validate skill; work-ticket and address-review without "Done when"
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** PLG-07, PLG-08, PLG-01, PLG-02, PLG-05
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0067, 0040, 0050
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0184, T-0073, T-0087, T-0140
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
New `plugin/skills/validate/SKILL.md` (`disable-model-invocation: true`): you are a **read-only judge**; never edit,
commit or fix; run `crewboard prime --validate` and follow the Agent instructions it prints; check (1) the change against
the description (and each review comment after a Send back), inferring reasonable criteria when none are written, (2) the
checks the Agent instructions name, else the project's obvious ones, (3) leftovers and out-of-scope edits; report one line
per finding with file and line; a gap **covered by a linked ticket** ("covered by SHOP-40.2") or **not asked for by this
ticket** ("Not covered by any ticket: …") is a **note**, never a failure; finish with `crewboard validate pass|fail
--stdin`, long text through stdin. `work-ticket` and `address-review` no longer mention "Done when": work "until the ticket
is done, as its description describes"; after `review submit`, **end your turn** and wait for the validation result, which
arrives as a message; on a failed-validation message, fix, commit and submit again.

**Done when**
- [ ] the validate skill exists with every rule above and is the default Validate skill
- [ ] no skill mentions "Done when"; work-ticket and address-review say to end the turn after submitting
- [ ] tests: the plugin tests (`crates/crewboard-core/tests/plugin.rs`, `crates/crewboard-cli/tests/skills.rs`) cover the new skill, its frontmatter and the commands it names; real `claude` behaviour checked by hand once (a pass and a fail)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLG-07, PLG-08, PLG-01, PLG-02, PLG-05 and decisions 0063, 0067, 0040, 0050

### T-0193 · Panel: validation actions, "…" menu, confirmations, token split, editing while it validates
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents

Submit for review dialog says it skips validation. The header shows the token split "agent 61k · validation 23k (2

what changed." when editing a validating ticket's description.

### T-0194 · Terminal tab: the Agent / Validator switch
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-11, ATT-03, ATT-07, SES-11
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0042, 0022
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 06 · Terminal tab → Row · validator (0063) → Validator running (SHOP-29), Validator waiting on a permission prompt (SHOP-33), Validator interrupted (SHOP-29) (+ Row · validator (0063) · Dark); crewboard.pen → 06 · Terminal tab → Row · validation results in the terminal (0063) → Validator ended with a verdict · read-only (SHOP-30), Validation failed · message in the agent’s terminal (SHOP-30), Validation cancelled · message in the agent’s terminal (SHOP-29) (+ Dark)
- **Depends on:** T-0186, T-0056, T-0061, T-0110, T-0119
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
The Terminal tab (`terminal/mod.rs` `TerminalTarget`, `view_models/terminal_strip.rs`) gets an **Agent / Validator** switch
once a validation has run. The Validator side shows the running validator (the user can type into it, it is the real
CLI) or the last one **read-only** until the next round starts; the header strip names which session is shown and has its
own "Copy attach command" (`crewboard attach SHOP-29 --validator`). ⌘J on a "Needs you · validator" item opens the
Terminal tab on the Validator side with focus. The focused-terminal rule (ATT-03, `attention/focus.rs`) applies per
session: while the validator's terminal has focus (in the app or attached), the validator raises no orange, inbox item or
notification.

**Done when**
- [ ] the switch appears only after a validation ran; both sides match the frames, light and dark
- [ ] typing into a running validator works; a finished one is read-only until the next round
- [ ] ⌘J on Needs you · validator lands on the Validator side, focused
- [ ] focusing or attaching to the validator suppresses its orange and notification
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (switch visibility, read-only, copied command, ⌘J target); focus-rule core test for validator sessions; by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-11, ATT-03, ATT-07, SES-11 and decisions 0063, 0042, 0022

### T-0195 · Validation reports in History and Review; Create ticket from a note
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** VAL-12, UI-04, REV-02
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0064
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 05 · Ticket side panel → Row · validation (0063) → History · validation rounds (SHOP-32, 0063) (+ Dark); crewboard.pen → 07 · Diff, review, approve & send back → Row · validation report & Auto-merge (0063, 0064) → SHOP-18 · Diff · Validated · round 2, Review · Failed 3 times · moved by you (SHOP-32), Auto-merge · success (SHOP-40.1), Auto-merge · conflict handed back vs manual Local merge (+ Dark); crewboard.pen → 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → Success toasts (Created SHOP-41 from a validation note)
- **Depends on:** T-0184, T-0189, T-0062, T-0091
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
History (`view_models/history.rs`) lists one entry per validation round (outcome, duration, tokens) that opens its report,
and "Merged automatically" for an auto-merge. In Review the Diff tab shows how the ticket got there (the Review entry) and
the **final report**, collapsible, next to the agent's summary: what was checked, the notes, linked keys clickable. A
"Failed 3 times · moved by you" review shows the last findings. Each "Not covered by any ticket" note has **Create
ticket**: the user's click creates a prefilled Backlog ticket (title from the note, description naming the source ticket)
in the same epic, with the toast "Created SHOP-41 from a validation note". The Auto-merge frames (success, conflict handed
back vs the manual Local merge that keeps "Send to agent to fix") are matched here.

**Done when**
- [ ] History shows every round with its report; the auto-merge line appears after an auto-merge
- [ ] the Diff tab shows the Review entry and the final report as in the frames, light and dark
- [ ] Create ticket makes one Backlog ticket in the same epic and shows the toast; the validator never creates tickets
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (history rows, report sections, Create ticket input); by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names VAL-12, UI-04, REV-02 and decisions 0063, 0064

### T-0198 · Several Project terminals per project (core)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** RUN-06, CLI-03
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0019, 0022
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0138, T-0167
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
`terminals/shell.rs::open_project` and `service/terminal_requests.rs` stop treating the Project terminal as one per
project: each open starts a **new** plain shell in the project folder, named "Terminal 1", "Terminal 2"… (the next free
number per project; an implementation choice, named in the commit). The protocol lists a project's terminals and closes
one (closing ends its shell). They survive closing the window and are gone after a restart. Full `crewboard` access as
before (CLI-03). The created-tickets summary of T-0167 moves to Project agents (PAG-05, T-0200); tickets created from a
terminal still appear live in Backlog with the highlight (brief 17).

**Done when**
- [ ] two opens give two shells, "Terminal 1" and "Terminal 2"; closing one ends only its shell
- [ ] terminals survive an app restart and are gone after a service restart
- [ ] tests: core tests for open, list, close and naming (ordinary shell command); protocol round trip
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names RUN-06, CLI-03 and decisions 0068, 0019, 0022

### T-0199 · Project agents (core): sessions, naming, lifecycle
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** PAG-01, PAG-04, PAG-06, PAG-07, SCH-03, SCH-04, REC-01
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0011, 0018, 0022, 0045
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0198, T-0181, T-0052, T-0111, T-0127, T-0129
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
Migration: the `project_agents` table of SDD §8 (id, project_id, name, name_source, claude_session_id, state, created_at,
ended_at) and `sessions.project_agent_id`. `SessionKind` gains **ProjectAgent** (`runs_claude`). Opening one (the user's
click is the go-ahead) launches `claude` in the project folder with the Project agent role (naming constant), **no
`CREWBOARD_TICKET`**, `--plugin-dir`, `--allowedTools "Bash(crewboard:*)"`, the status-line `--settings`, `--session-id`, no
permission flags, and `/crewboard:project-agent`. **Naming:** "New agent" until the first request; `crewboard agent name
"<name>"` (that Project agent only, once; a second call is refused unless the user asked for a rename) sets it; if it
hasn't named itself by the end of its first turn, Crewboard uses the first words of the request (from the
UserPromptSubmit payload); the **user's rename always wins** and is never overwritten (`name_source`). **Lifecycle:**
owned by the service; closing ends it (the app confirms when mid-turn); a "New agent" that never got a request is
discarded on close and disappears after a restart; others become **"Ended by restart · Resume"** after a restart and
resume the same conversation on click; the **10 most recent ended** per project are listed and resumable; transcripts are
never deleted. No instance limit, not counted toward `max_concurrency`; the plan-limit hold applies: no new Project agent
opens while the usage limit is active (it says why), and limit-stopped ones continue on Resume all.

**Done when**
- [ ] opening starts the session with the role, the skill and no ticket variable; several per project run at once
- [ ] naming: agent name once; fallback to the request's first words after the first turn; a user rename is never overwritten
- [ ] close, discard of an unused "New agent", Ended by restart · Resume, and the 10-entry recent list behave as listed
- [ ] Project agents never count toward the agent limit; none opens during the usage limit
- [ ] tests: launch command test; naming tests (agent, fallback, user wins); lifecycle and reconcile tests (ordinary command in place of `claude`); migration test
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PAG-01, PAG-04, PAG-06, PAG-07, SCH-03, SCH-04, REC-01 and decisions 0068, 0011, 0018, 0022, 0045

### T-0200 · Project agent permissions, `prime --project`, Created and changed
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** CLI-06, PAG-02, PAG-03, PAG-05, TKT-08, CLI-04, MSG-01
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0031, 0030, 0060, 0019
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0199, T-0177, T-0064, T-0065, T-0071, T-0151, T-0161, T-0163
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
`caller.rs` gains a **Project agent** caller (process ancestry from Project agent session roots). `policy.rs`: read
anything; create tickets in Backlog (incl. epics, children, External ID, priority, `create -f`); edit, link and
prioritise tickets **still in Backlog**; `ask <KEY>` (to any ticket; delivery and resuming follow §16, the answer is
delivered into the Project agent's session); `ask human` (a question on the Project agent, with options, T-0163); `agent
name` for itself. Everything else (move, start, approve, merge, close, delete, edit a started ticket) is refused with
"Project agents can only change Backlog tickets". `--actor` defaults to the Project agent, so tickets get "created by" /
"Updated by" naming it ("by Checkout slowness", the current name). `crewboard prime --project` prints every ticket with
status, priority, dependencies and epic; for each started ticket its worktree path and branch; and the Project agent
instructions (global + project). Each Project agent keeps a **Created and changed** record ("Created SHOP-35, SHOP-36 ·
edited SHOP-31 · 2 dependencies") from its events, with **Delete** per created ticket that never went to Dev (TKT-08
confirmation in the app).

**Done when**
- [ ] a Project agent can create, edit, link and prioritise Backlog tickets; every other change is refused with the exact text
- [ ] `ask <KEY>` and `ask human` work from a Project agent; answers come back into its session
- [ ] created / updated tickets name the Project agent; `prime --project` prints every part listed above
- [ ] Created and changed lists the agent's creations, edits and dependencies; Delete removes a never-started created ticket
- [ ] tests: caller and policy tests (allowed and refused per command); prime snapshot test; created-and-changed derivation; ask and answer routing
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names CLI-06, PAG-02, PAG-03, PAG-05, TKT-08, CLI-04, MSG-01 and decisions 0068, 0031, 0030, 0060, 0019

**Notes:** questions from a Project agent have no asking ticket; the `messages` table needs the asker to be a Project
agent (a migration), an implementation detail named in the commit.

### T-0201 · Project agent hooks, inbox, notifications and tokens
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** PAG-07, ATT-03, ATT-06, ATT-10, ATT-11, USG-01
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0016, 0042, 0020
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 10 · Needs-you inbox → Row · M12 item types (0063, 0064, 0068) → New item types · group 1 (M12) (the Project agent “Import GitHub issues” item); crewboard.pen → 11 · Banners, toasts & OS notifications → OS notifications → macOS · M12 (Project agent asks a question, Project agent needs permission)
- **Depends on:** T-0200, T-0066, T-0110, T-0115, T-0117, T-0125
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
`crewboard hook` runs for the Project agent role even without `CREWBOARD_TICKET`; `map_hook` maps its permission prompts to
**Needs you** and its `ask human` to **Asks you** on the Project agent, never Idle. These are inbox group 1 items labelled
with the project and the agent's name (no ticket key), answered inline like ticket questions, counted in the header and
project switcher, and notified ("shop-web · Project agent asks a question" / "needs permission"), except while its
terminal has focus (ATT-03). Its tokens count in today's total and per agent (usage by session).

**Done when**
- [ ] a Project agent's permission prompt and question create group 1 items with its name and a notification
- [ ] answering inline delivers into the agent's session; the item disappears when resolved elsewhere
- [ ] no Idle state for Project agents; focus suppresses its items
- [ ] tokens per agent and in today's total
- [ ] tests: hook tests with real-shaped JSON and the role; needs-you derivation; notify plan; usage per agent; by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PAG-07, ATT-03, ATT-06, ATT-10, ATT-11, USG-01 and decisions 0068, 0016, 0042, 0020

### T-0202 · Plugin: the project-agent skill
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** PLG-09, PLG-01, PLG-02, PLG-05
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0040
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0200, T-0191
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
New `plugin/skills/project-agent/SKILL.md` (`disable-model-invocation: true`): run `crewboard prime --project` first and
follow the Project agent instructions it prints; right after reading the first request, name yourself with `crewboard
agent name "<3–4 words>"`; investigate by reading and running code in the checkout and in ticket worktrees, but **never
edit or commit**; create, edit, link and prioritise Backlog tickets with the CLI (long text through `--stdin`, batches with
`create -f`); imports from trackers use the user's own skills; ask a ticket's agent with `ask <KEY>` and the user with
`ask human` (with options when there are clear alternatives); if the CLI refuses, tell the user what you would need.

**Done when**
- [ ] the skill exists with every rule above
- [ ] tests: plugin tests cover its frontmatter and the commands it names; real `claude` checked by hand once (it names itself and creates a Backlog ticket)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLG-09, PLG-01, PLG-02, PLG-05 and decisions 0068, 0040

### T-0203 · Terminals & agents: header button, drawer, tabs, + menu, ⇧⌘T / ⇧⌘A
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** UI-09, UI-01, RUN-06, PAG-01
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0058, 0019
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 02 · App shell & project switcher → Row · Terminals & agents button & switcher (0068) → Terminals & agents button · a Project agent waits (+ Dark); crewboard.pen → 17 · Project terminals (Empty · shell prompt, Plain shell use, Ended, Cycle rejected by the tool, Adding a ticket to an epic (0039), After a restart · terminals are gone); crewboard.pen → 18 · Project agents & terminals → Row · lifecycle → + menu · New terminal / New Project agent; crewboard.pen → 01 · Components → Keyboard map
- **Depends on:** T-0198, T-0199, T-0201, T-0033, T-0058, T-0158
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
`project_terminal.rs` / `view_models/project_terminal.rs` become the **Terminals & agents** drawer: the header button
"Terminals & agents" (`shell.rs`; tooltip with ⇧⌘T; an orange dot while a Project agent waits), a drawer header with one
tab per Project agent and per terminal ("Tab · Checkout slowness", "Tab · Terminal 1"…), **+ New** (menu: New terminal /
New Project agent "⇧⌘A · uses tokens") and **Recently ended**. Terminal tabs show the frame-17 states (empty hint, plain
use, ended, the CLI's cycle error, after a restart gone). **⇧⌘T** opens the drawer on the last-used terminal, or a new one
if none is open; **⇧⌘A** opens a new Project agent. `keymap.rs` gets `KeyAction::NewProjectAgent` (⇧⌘A / Ctrl+Shift+A, also
in the Win/Linux terminal column) and renames the ⇧⌘T row "Terminals & agents (last-used terminal)"; the keymap tests keep
"no shortcut twice". The project switcher counts waiting Project agents with the other needs-you items. Agent tab contents
are T-0204.

**Done when**
- [ ] the header button, tooltip and dot match the frame, light and dark
- [ ] the drawer opens several terminals and agents per project as tabs; closing a terminal tab ends its shell
- [ ] ⇧⌘T focuses the last-used terminal or opens one; ⇧⌘A opens a new Project agent; the keyboard map matches *01 · Components → Keyboard map*
- [ ] frame 17's scenes match
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (tabs, last-used, + menu, dot); keymap tests; by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-09, UI-01, RUN-06, PAG-01 and decisions 0068, 0058, 0019

### T-0204 · Project agent views: New agent, rename, close, Created and changed, recently ended
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** UI-09, PAG-04, PAG-05, PAG-06, TKT-08, TKT-05
- **Decisions:** 0068, 0060, 0019
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 18 · Project agents & terminals → Row · investigating (Checkout slowness · investigating), Row · importing (Import GitHub issues · importing), Row · dark, Row · states (New agent · no request yet, Waiting for you · permission prompt, Waiting for you · a question, Refused action, Rename · your name wins) (+ Dark), Row · lifecycle (Close an agent mid-turn · confirm, Ended normally, Ended by restart · Resume, Recently ended · list, Delete a created ticket · confirm (0060)) (+ Dark); crewboard.pen → 04 · Ticket card variants · Light / Dark → Backlog · created by Project agent
- **Depends on:** T-0203, T-0200, T-0076, T-0162
- **Size:** L

**What and why**
An agent tab shows the Agent header (name, state, tokens) above its terminal, and the **Created and changed** summary with
links and **Delete** per created ticket (the TKT-08 confirmation). States from frame 18: "New agent" before the first
request; waiting for you (permission prompt / a question, the same answer UI as the inbox); a refused action (the CLI's
refusal in the terminal); **Rename** (the user's name wins); **Close mid-turn** confirms ("Stop and close"), an unused New
agent closes without asking; Ended normally; **Ended by restart · Resume**; **Recently ended** (with entries and empty).
Tickets a Project agent creates appear in Backlog live with a subtle highlight, and cards say "by <agent name>".

**Done when**
- [ ] every listed scene matches its frame, light and dark
- [ ] rename, close (with and without confirmation), Resume and the recent list call the T-0199 actions
- [ ] Created and changed updates live; Delete asks and removes the ticket
- [ ] new tickets highlight in Backlog; cards name the agent
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (states, summary text, confirmations, recent list); by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names UI-09, PAG-04, PAG-05, PAG-06, TKT-08, TKT-05 and decisions 0068, 0060, 0019

### T-0205 · After a restart: validating tickets and Project agents in the summary
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** REC-01, REC-06, UI-04
- **Decisions:** 0063, 0068, 0045
- **Design:** crewboard.pen → 16 · Recovery & cleanup → Opening after a reboot; crewboard.pen → 16 · Recovery & cleanup → Row · validation & Project agents after a restart (0063, 0068) → Validating ticket after a restart (SHOP-29) (+ Dark · Validating ticket after a restart (SHOP-29))
- **Depends on:** T-0188, T-0204, T-0121, T-0122
- **Size:** S

**What and why**
The after-reboot summary (`view_models/after_restart.rs`) counts Project agents ("8 sessions were interrupted · 2 Project
agents ended"), lists them with Resume, explains that an unused New agent is gone, and shows a validating ticket's row
"was Validating · Resume = fresh validation". The Interrupted card and panel for a ticket that was validating match their
frame (Resume starts a fresh validation, not counted).

**Done when**
- [ ] the summary and the validating-ticket frames match, light and dark
- [ ] Resume from the summary starts a fresh validation / resumes the Project agent
- [ ] tests: view-model tests (counts, rows); by hand against the named frames
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names REC-01, REC-06, UI-04 and decisions 0063, 0068, 0045

### T-0206 · M12 checks on Windows and Linux (local VMs)
- **Epic:** M12 · Validation, Auto-merge, priorities, Project agents
- **Requirements:** PLT-02
- **Decisions:** 0053, 0056, 0021
- **Design:** —
- **Depends on:** T-0160, T-0205
- **Size:** M

**What and why**
M12 is built on macOS and re-checked in the VMs (d0053), as in T-0160: `scripts/check.ps1` in the Windows VM and
`scripts/check.sh` in the Ubuntu VM on the final M12 tree. Platform-specific M12 paths are checked there: the role
variable reaches validator and Project agent sessions under ConPTY, caller identity finds validator and Project agent
callers (the Windows job-object path of T-0156), and Ctrl+Shift+A works in the terminal on Windows and Linux. Anything that
fails is fixed in this ticket.

**Done when**
- [ ] checks green in both VMs on the final M12 tree (test counts recorded next to macOS)
- [ ] validator and Project agent callers are identified on Windows and Linux (tests)
- [ ] Ctrl+Shift+A opens a Project agent with the terminal focused on Windows and Linux (by hand)
- [ ] checks green (`scripts/check.sh`: fmt, clippy -D warnings, tests); commit names PLT-02 and decisions 0053, 0056, 0021
